### [1] [Theory of Gravity as Theory of Local Inertial Frames](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22354)
*Bartłomiej Bąk*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This dissertation establishes the affine theory of the full Riemann tensor as an extended theory of gravity, where a symmetric spacetime connection—not the metric—is fundamental; it interprets gravity as a local version of Newton’s First Law, provides a variational formulation, and rigorously derives the transition from the affine to the metric picture—showing non-metricity maps to matter fields, echoing Weyl’s geometric unification of electromagnetism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend general relativity by elevating the symmetric affine connection (representing local inertial frames) to the fundamental dynamical variable, thereby providing a geometric foundation for gravity as inertia and enabling a unified description where non-metricity encodes matter—reviving and generalizing Weyl’s idea.

Method: Develops an affine gravitational theory based on the full Riemann tensor of a symmetric connection; constructs a variational principle using previously established results; analyzes the affine-to-metric transition via Legendre transformation or field redefinition; examines non-metricity’s physical role through explicit examples.

Result: Proves that the affine theory with symmetric connection is a consistent extended gravity theory; demonstrates that non-metricity in the affine picture corresponds to matter fields (e.g., electromagnetic-like fields) in the metric picture; achieves the first fully general derivation of the metric limit from the full affine Riemann theory, validated across multiple examples.

Conclusion: The affine description is more fundamental than the metric one; gravity emerges intrinsically from inertial structure, and matter–geometry unification arises naturally via non-metricity—realizing a generalized Weyl-type framework beyond Einstein–Cartan or pure metric theories.

Abstract: It is proved that the affine theory of the full Riemann tensor constitutes the extended theory of gravity. The fundamental object here is a spacetime symmetric connection. Its physical interpretation is that of a field of local inertial frames. In this approach, gravity arises, in a natural way, as a local version of Newton's First Law. A variational formulation of the theory is presented, based on the results introduced in the article co-authored by the present author and one of the supervisors. The general framework is supported by several examples. A central result of the dissertation is the transition from the affine picture to the metric picture. A simplified version of this procedure -- valid for a specific class of Lagrangians. It was proved there that the non-metricity in the affine picture can be interpreted as a matter field in the metric picture. This is precisely Hermann Weyl's interpretation of electromagnetism. Here, the transformation from the full Riemann tensor theory to the conventional metric theory is examined in full generality for the first time and illustrated using the previously introduced examples.

</details>


### [2] [Sourced Gravitational Radiation in Parity-Violating Tetrad Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22396)
*G. G. L. Nashed*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes linear gravitational modes, source coupling, and energy balance in a four-parameter tetrad-based gravity theory with parity-even and parity-odd torsion couplings; it identifies eight physical degrees of freedom (2 tensor, 2 scalar, 4 vector), determines their propagation speeds and kinetic properties, reveals indefinite vector Hamiltonian despite real speeds and positive kinetic eigenvalues, derives explicit source-to-amplitude mappings for all polarizations—including spin-multipole effects via $V_μ=\partial^νB_{νμ}$—and proves equivalence of quadratic Noether and Møller currents, while noting the vector energy flux remains formal due to Hamiltonian unboundedness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize gravitational excitations and energy transport in a general parity-violating tetrad theory beyond GR and TEGR, specifically probing consistency (e.g., ghost-freedom, causality, positivity) and observational signatures (e.g., mode splitting, spin-coupled sources) in the weak-field regime.

Method: Linearization of the tetrad field around Minkowski spacetime; scalar–vector–tensor decomposition; analysis of dispersion relations, kinetic matrices, and Hamiltonian definiteness; derivation of Green’s function responses to compact sources; use of $V_μ = \partial^νB_{νμ}$ to isolate intrinsic-spin multipoles; proof of quadratic Noether–Møller current equivalence for first-derivative tetrad Lagrangians; parameter-space scanning for degeneracy and splitting.

Result: Eight linear degrees of freedom: two luminal tensor modes, two luminal scalar modes, and four vector modes with coupling-dependent speeds; vector kinetic eigenvalues can be positive and speeds real, yet Hamiltonian is generically indefinite; explicit amplitude formulas for all eight polarizations, including spin-multipole coupling; exact equality of quadratic Noether and second-order Møller currents; vector-mode energy flux remains formal due to unbounded Hamiltonian; stationary limit recovers prior work; parameter scans show vector-mode splitting and kinetic degeneracy.

Conclusion: The generic parity-violating tetrad theory admits consistent luminal tensor and scalar propagation but harbors structurally unstable vector sectors—manifested by indefinite Hamiltonian—even when local causality and kinetic positivity hold; this undermines a physical interpretation of vector-mode energy, highlighting a fundamental tension between Lorentz invariance, parity violation, and Hamiltonian boundedness in tetrad-based gravity.

Abstract: We study linear gravitational modes, sourced far-zone fields, and quadratic energy balance in a four-parameter tetrad theory of gravity. The theory contains three parity-even torsion invariants and a parity-odd coupling $v^μa_μ$, where $v_μ$ and $a_μ$ are the vector and axial torsion components. Linearizing around Minkowski spacetime and applying a scalar--vector--tensor decomposition, we find two tensor, two scalar, and four vector degrees of freedom in the generic nondegenerate parity-violating region. The tensor and scalar modes propagate at the speed of light, while the two vector branches have coupling-dependent propagation speeds. We derive their kinetic eigenvalues, characteristic speeds, and flux normalizations. An exact trace identity shows that the vector Hamiltonian is not positive definite in the generic branch, even when the kinetic eigenvalues are positive and the propagation speeds are real. We also derive the response to a general compact canonical source and obtain explicit source-to-amplitude relations for all eight linear polarizations. The field $V_μ=\partial^νB_{νμ}$ is used to illustrate intrinsic-spin multipoles. {We further prove that the quadratic Noether current equals the second-order Møller current for the chosen first-derivative tetrad Lagrangian and derive the corresponding mode-resolved energy and momentum fluxes.} The vector contribution remains a formal signed response because of the unbounded Hamiltonian. The stationary limit reproduces the results of Ref.~\cite{ShirafujiNashed1997}, and a representative parameter scan illustrates vector-mode splitting and kinetic degeneracy.

</details>


### [3] [Charged particles and fields in bumblebee gravity: Periodic orbits and superradiance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22482)
*Marco A. A. de Paula,Renan B. Magalhães,V. B. Bezerra,A. A. Araújo Filho*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies charged particle dynamics and scalar wave absorption in the spacetime of a charged black hole in bumblebee gravity, focusing on the Lorentz-violating parameter $l$. It finds that $l$ increases the innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO), modifies absorption cross sections via asymptotic momentum corrections, introduces a transition velocity in low-frequency absorption, and suppresses superradiance as $l$ grows—especially for $l > 0$, while $l < 0$ enhances superradiance relative to Reissner–Nordström.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how Lorentz violation—parameterized by $l$ in bumblebee gravity—affects fundamental black hole phenomena: geodesic motion of charged particles, scalar wave absorption, and superradiance, particularly in a non-asymptotically flat spacetime.

Method: Analytical derivation of the charged bumblebee black hole metric and its geometric properties; analysis of timelike circular orbits and ISCO; numerical computation of total absorption cross section for scalar waves with modified asymptotic flux due to non-flat asymptotics; analytical approximations in low- and high-frequency limits; identification of superradiant regime via $\omega < \omega_c$.

Result: ISCO radius increases with $l$; total absorption cross section exhibits three regimes (unbounded/bounded absorption, bounded superradiance); low-frequency absorption shows a transition velocity generalizing the RN case; superradiance threshold $\omega_c$ exists and amplification decreases with increasing $l$; $l < 0$ enhances superradiance over RN; despite lowering the potential barrier, larger $l$ reduces absorption due to the $1/(1+l)$ geometrical factor.

Conclusion: The Lorentz-violating parameter $l$ in bumblebee gravity qualitatively and quantitatively alters black hole physics: stabilizing orbits, reshaping wave absorption, and modulating superradiance—demonstrating that Lorentz violation leaves distinct imprints observable in both geodesic and scattering regimes.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamics of charged particles and fields in the background of a charged black hole in bumblebee gravity, aiming to better understand the role played by the Lorentz-violating parameter $l$. We first review the derivation of the charged bumblebee black hole and investigate its main physical and geometrical properties. We then obtain the circular and bound trajectories of charged particles and find that the innermost stable circular orbit increases with $l$. By using a numerical approach, we compute the total absorption cross section for arbitrary values of the scalar wave frequency $ω$. Since the spacetime is not asymptotically flat, we construct the total absorption cross section by taking into account the modified asymptotic momentum and flux. Analytical approximations are derived in the low- and high-frequency regimes. The low-frequency expression displays a transition velocity that separates two distinct asymptotic branches and generalizes the corresponding Reissner-Nordström result. Our numerical results for the cross section agree with the analytical approximations in their corresponding limits, and the absorption parameter space can be divided into three regions: Unbounded absorption, bounded absorption, and bounded superradiance. In particular, superradiance occurs whenever $ω<ω_{c}$, where $ω_{c}$ is the threshold for superradiance. Moreover, the superradiance amplification decreases as we increase $l$, and charged bumblebee black holes with $l < 0$ lead to greater superradiant scattering than in the Reissner-Nordström case. We also note that although increasing $l$ lowers the potential barrier, it reduces the total absorption cross section because of the geometrical factor $1/(1+l)$ present in the cross section formula.

</details>


### [4] [Flexible numerical characteristic gluing to extremal Reissner-Nordström black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22518)
*Junwoo Lee*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper numerically investigates characteristic gluing for gravitational collapse to extremal Reissner–Nordström black holes, improving lower bounds on admissible black hole size for massless scalar fields and providing evidence that the mass-to-charge ratio bound of unity for massive scalar fields is sharp.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To numerically test and refine the characteristic gluing construction for extremal black hole formation, particularly addressing feasibility bounds—namely, minimum black hole size for massless fields and the critical mass-to-charge ratio threshold for massive fields.

Method: Numerical implementation of the characteristic gluing method applied to spherically symmetric gravitational collapse of charged scalar fields; high-precision integration along null hypersurfaces and careful parameter scanning near critical thresholds.

Result: 1) Significantly improved lower bound on final black hole size permitting gluing for massless scalar fields; 2) Numerical construction of gluing solutions with scalar field mass-to-charge ratio arbitrarily close to (but less than) unity, strongly supporting sharpness of the unit bound.

Conclusion: The numerical results confirm theoretical expectations about the limits of characteristic gluing: extremal black hole formation via charged scalar collapse is constrained by quantifiable geometric and field-parameter thresholds, and those thresholds appear tight.

Abstract: Recent work has used the method of characteristic gluing to construct spacetimes violating the third law of black hole mechanics. This paper uses numerical methods to investigate two aspects of the gluing construction of solutions describing the gravitational collapse of a charged scalar field to form an extremal Reissner-Nordström black hole. First, for a massless scalar field, we improve significantly the lower bounds on the size of the final black hole for which gluing is possible. Second, for a massive scalar field, it is known that the formation of an extremal Reissner--Nordström black hole is impossible if the mass-to-charge ratio of the scalar field exceeds unity. We present strong evidence that this bound is sharp by constructing gluing solutions with a scalar field mass-to-charge ratio just below unity.

</details>


### [5] [Black Holes as Frequency-Dependent Filters of Stochastic Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22644)
*Sefi Katznelson,Aidan Minger,Stefano Profumo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper quantifies how black holes filter gravitational-wave backgrounds, showing that Schwarzschild holes induce only absorption-based spectral distortion (no net monopole scattering), while Kerr holes allow superradiant amplification—but only under highly aligned, non-isotropic conditions; cosmological cumulative effects are negligible.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the distinction between black-hole ringdown (transient, QNM-dominated) and stationary gravitational-wave background scattering, and to assess whether cosmological black-hole populations imprint observable spectral features on stochastic gravitational-wave backgrounds.

Method: Treats black holes as frequency-, angle-, and polarization-dependent linear filters; computes the transfer function for Schwarzschild holes via elastic scattering and horizon absorption; extends to angular/polarization transport kernel for cosmological populations; generalizes to Kerr geometry including superradiance; accounts for spin orientation statistics and isotropy assumptions.

Result: For isotropic stationary backgrounds: (i) Schwarzschild holes produce zero net monopole scattering; spectral distortion arises solely from horizon absorption—quantified via a transfer function with absorptive and phase-delay signatures; (ii) cosmological optical depth is negligible even for asteroid-mass primordial black holes constituting all dark matter; (iii) Kerr holes permit superradiant amplification in co-rotating modes, but isotropic incidence and random spin orientations suppress the diffuse signal by orders of magnitude.

Conclusion: Observable modifications to gravitational-wave backgrounds from black holes are not cumulative across cosmological populations but require rare, nearby, spin-aligned, rapidly rotating, or transiently illuminated systems—ruling out detectable population-level imprints for current and near-future observatories.

Abstract: Black holes ring when perturbed, whereas their response to a stationary gravitational-wave background is a real-frequency scattering problem, not a source of additional quasi-normal-mode lines. We make this standard distinction quantitative by treating a black hole as a frequency-, angle-, and polarization-dependent filter. For an isotropic stationary background around Schwarzschild holes, elastic scattering produces no net monopole signal, so horizon absorption is the only population-level spectral distortion. We calculate this transfer function for Schwarzschild holes in detail, identify its absorptive and phase-delay signatures, and connect the stationary response to the causal ringdown excited by a finite wave packet. We then promote the single-hole result to an angular and polarization transport kernel for a cosmological population. The resulting optical depth is negligible for realistic black-hole populations, including asteroid-mass primordial black holes comprising all dark matter. We then extend the analysis to Kerr holes, for which superradiance allows genuine amplification in selected co-rotating modes, but isotropic incidence and random spin orientations strongly dilute the diffuse signal. Observable effects are therefore more likely in rare, nearby, aligned, rapidly spinning, or transiently illuminated systems than through cumulative cosmological propagation.

</details>


### [6] [An exact Kerr-like rotating Morris--Thorne wormhole: throat, ergoregion and equatorial shadow slice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22823)
*Mark Sukaiti,Davide Batic,Denys Dutykh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs an exact rotating (Kerr-like) extension of the zero-redshift Morris–Thorne wormhole in a Teo-type stationary, axisymmetric spacetime. It solves Einstein’s equations for an anisotropic comoving source, fixes the curvature function K(r)=r²+a², and expresses frame dragging via elliptic integrals. The solution is asymptotically flat with zero ADM mass, so angular momentum J_ADM and oblateness parameter a are independent; the choice J_ADM = a r₀ is illustrative, not enforced. A physically meaningful throat radius r_th = √(r₀² − a²) emerges, requiring |a| < r₀ for regularity. The throat is timelike, quasi-locally defined by vanishing mean curvature, and free of CTCs. No fundamental matter model is proposed — the stress-energy is phenomenological. Ergoregion onset and equatorial capture intervals are analyzed, though full geodesic motion is nonseparable.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct an exact, asymptotically flat, rotating generalization of the zero-redshift Morris–Thorne wormhole that preserves key geometric and physical properties (e.g., traversability, absence of horizons/CTCs) while incorporating frame-dragging in a controlled, analytically tractable way—without relying on approximations or numerical relativity.

Method: Uses a Teo-type stationary, axisymmetric metric ansatz; solves the vacuum Einstein equations with an anisotropic, zero-angular-momentum-comoving stress-energy tensor; imposes zero redshift (lapse = 1); derives frame-dragging function via radial quadrature solved with incomplete elliptic integrals; defines the throat quasi-locally via vanishing mean curvature and positive area second variation; analyzes causal structure, ergoregion, and equatorial null geodesics using Hamilton–Jacobi methods.

Result: An exact 3-parameter family of rotating wormhole solutions (r₀, a, J_ADM), asymptotically flat with zero ADM mass; explicit expression for throat radius r_th = √(r₀² − a²) and reality bound |a| < r₀; proof that the throat is timelike, regular, and CTC-free; identification of ergoregion onset; derivation of equatorial null capture interval; no horizon formation despite rotation.

Conclusion: A consistent, exact, rotating wormhole geometry exists within classical GR with anisotropic matter support; it generalizes the static zero-redshift wormhole while preserving traversability and asymptotic flatness; the parameters a and J_ADM are independent—J_ADM = a r₀ is merely a convenient choice for illustration; the solution provides a rigorous benchmark for studying rotation effects in exotic spacetimes.

Abstract: We construct an exact Kerr-like rotating extension of the zero-redshift Morris--Thorne wormhole in a Teo-type stationary, axisymmetric ansatz. The Einstein equations for an anisotropic source comoving with zero angular momentum observers fix $\mathcal{K}(r)=r^2+a^2$ and reduce frame dragging to a radial quadrature. For $b(r)=r_0^2/r$, this is evaluated using incomplete elliptic integrals and normalised by the ADM angular momentum. The spacetime is asymptotically flat with zero ADM mass, so $a=J_{\rm ADM}/M_{\rm ADM}$ does not apply. The oblateness length $a$ and angular momentum $J_{\rm ADM}$ remain independent, yielding a family $(r_0,a,J_{\rm ADM})$. The choice $J_{\rm ADM}=ar_0$ selects only a one-parameter slice for numerical ergoregion and equatorial-capture illustrations; it is not a field-equation constraint. We find $r_{\rm th}=\sqrt{r_0^2-a^2}$, with reality bound $|a|\leq r_0$ and regular range $0\leq |a|<r_0$. In the canonical stationary foliation, the throat is defined quasi-locally as the unique closed surface $S_0$ in the family $S_\ell$ with zero mean curvature and positive area second variation. Moreover, $r=r_{\rm th}$ and $\ell=0$ are coordinate representations. A signed throat-adapted coordinate reveals two asymptotically flat ends, excludes closed timelike curves, and establishes that the throat is timelike, not a horizon. These conclusions hold independently of $J_{\rm ADM}=ar_0$. The supporting stress tensor, reconstructed from the Einstein tensor, is an effective phenomenological source. We do not claim any microscopic matter Lagrangian or realistic equation of state. We also determine the ergoregion onset. Since the full Hamilton--Jacobi equation is nonseparable, we limit ourselves to derive the optical result for a one-dimensional equatorial capture interval.

</details>


### [7] [Covariant kinetic theory of photons coupled to massive particles in curved spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22837)
*Long Cui,Yi-fan Cai,Xin Hao,Bin Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper formulates a covariant Boltzmann equation for photons and massive particles in general curved spacetimes, derives collision integrals for key radiative processes (emission/absorption, Compton, particle scattering), enforces the H-theorem to recover the Einstein relation and detailed balance at equilibrium, and applies the framework to thermal radiation from Kerr accretion disks—both globally and locally in thermodynamic equilibrium.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously model non-equilibrium radiative transfer in strong-field gravity (e.g., near black holes) using a fully covariant, first-principles kinetic theory that respects general covariance, conservation laws, and statistical mechanics principles like the H-theorem.

Method: Covariant phase-space formulation of the Boltzmann equation; derivation of geometrically consistent collision integrals for photon–matter interactions (including emission/absorption, Compton, and massive-particle scattering); imposition of the H-theorem to constrain transition rates; derivation of equilibrium conditions (vanishing entropy production → detailed balance → covariant thermodynamics); application to Kerr spacetime with accretion disk models (global and local thermodynamic equilibrium).

Result: A fully covariant Boltzmann equation with explicitly constructed, conserved collision terms; proof that H-theorem enforcement yields the relativistic generalization of the Einstein A/B coefficient relation; derivation of covariant photon thermodynamics (e.g., temperature-redshift law, entropy density) from detailed balance; concrete computation of thermal spectra from Kerr accretion disks under both global and local equilibrium assumptions.

Conclusion: The framework provides a foundational, geometrically exact description of radiative kinetics in general relativity, unifying non-equilibrium transport, statistical mechanics, and strong-field astrophysics—enabling predictive modeling of high-precision observables (e.g., X-ray spectra, polarization) from compact objects.

Abstract: This paper develops the covariant Boltzmann equation governing the coupled evolution of photons and massive particles in general curved spacetimes. We construct the collision integrals for photon emission-absorption, Compton scattering, and scattering between massive particles, and show the conservation laws. Requiring the H-theorem to hold determines the relation between the absorption and emission transition rates, which reduces to the Einstein relation in the non-relativistic limit. At equilibrium the entropy production vanishes, yielding the detailed balance condition, from which we obtain the covariant thermodynamic relations of the photon gas. We illustrate these results with the thermal radiation from an accretion disk in Kerr spacetime, first in global equilibrium and then in a Keplerian disk in local thermodynamic equilibrium. We further extend the collision integrals to arbitrary multi-species processes involving both null and massive particles. The formulation treats non-equilibrium radiative processes in strong gravitational fields from first principles.

</details>


### [8] [Charged Klein-Gordon modes on an Ellis wormhole: magnetic confinement and conditional Heun solvability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22860)
*Abdullah Guvendi,Omar Mustafa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes charged Klein-Gordon modes on a 2+1D Ellis wormhole in a uniform external magnetic field (defined via Euclidean embedding), finding radial confinement for nonzero magnetic coupling, reducing the radial equation to confluent Heun form, and showing that exact polynomial solutions exist only on constrained parameter submanifolds—not generically—while distinguishing continuous-ℓ solvability from physically required integer azimuthal quantum numbers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand quantum scalar field behavior—specifically charged Klein-Gordon modes—on a topologically nontrivial, curved spacetime (2+1D Ellis wormhole) under an externally imposed magnetic field, with emphasis on spectral properties, confinement, and exact solvability.

Method: Constructs a uniform magnetic field via pullback from a Euclidean embedding space; derives the intrinsic gauge potential and magnetic scalar; separates the Klein-Gordon equation; maps the radial equation to a one-dimensional Schrödinger operator with effective potential; identifies its asymptotic confinement behavior; reduces it to confluent Heun form; applies polynomial truncation conditions to obtain exact solutions on constrained parameter submanifolds; distinguishes formal continuous-ℓ solutions from physically admissible integer ℓ.

Result: Radial confinement arises for nonzero magnetic coupling due to a quadratic asymptotic potential term; the radial equation is exactly reducible to confluent Heun type; exact polynomial (quasi-polynomial) solutions exist only when simultaneous termination conditions are satisfied—defining lower-dimensional parameter submanifolds; relativistic energy eigenvalues are derived for these special cases; the lowest polynomial sector is explicitly analyzed; angular periodicity enforces integer ℓ, distinct from formal continuous-ℓ solvability.

Conclusion: Exact solvability of charged scalar fields on this magnetic wormhole is nongeneric—restricted to fine-tuned parameters—and reveals a rich interplay between topology, geometry, and gauge structure; the system supports discrete bound-state spectra under magnetic coupling, but full spectral characterization requires beyond-polynomial methods.

Abstract: We study charged Klein-Gordon modes on a $(2+1)$-dimensional Ellis traversable wormhole in an external magnetic field defined as uniform in an auxiliary Euclidean embedding space. The spatial geometry is intrinsically catenoidal, while the embedding is used only to construct the external gauge connection. Pullback of the ambient potential yields a regular azimuthal gauge field whose associated intrinsic magnetic scalar reverses sign across the throat. Separation of the Klein-Gordon equation leads to a radial problem that is unitarily equivalent to a one-dimensional Schrödinger operator with a regular effective potential. For nonzero magnetic coupling, the quadratic asymptotic term produces radial confinement on the complete wormhole. The radial equation can be reduced to the confluent-Heun class. Polynomial truncation is possible only on constrained parameter submanifolds determined by simultaneous termination conditions and therefore does not represent the generic confined spectrum. We derive the corresponding relativistic energies and analyze the lowest polynomial sector, while distinguishing the formal continuous-$\ell$ solvability correlations from the integer values of the azimuthal separation constant required by the standard angular periodicity condition.

</details>


### [9] [Probing black holes surrounded by Dust field in Quantum Fluctuation Modified Gravity with twin-peak QPOs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22874)
*Kamola Shermatova,Qodir Badalov,Abubakir Shermatov,Javlon Rayimbaev,Bekzod Rahmatov,Shokhzod Jumaniyozov,Dilafruz Xujanova,Farhod Nuraliev*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates a quantum-fluctuation-modified black hole solution with surrounding dust, analyzing how parameters α (quantum fluctuation) and K (dust strength) oppositely affect horizon structure, orbital dynamics, and QPO models; MCMC fitting to four astrophysical sources constrains K ∈ (0, 0.5) and α ∈ (0.3, 0.6) at 2σ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe quantum-gravity effects on black hole spacetimes and accretion disk dynamics via observable quasi-periodic oscillations (QPOs), using a static, spherically symmetric solution modified by quantum fluctuations and dust.

Method: Analytical derivation of horizon structure, effective potential, and geodesic orbital characteristics (ISCO, MBO, MCO, epicyclic frequencies) in the α–K parameterized metric; construction of three twin-peak QPO models (relativistic precession, warped-disk, epicyclic-resonance) with associated resonance radii; Bayesian inference via Markov Chain Monte Carlo fitting to QPO data from four X-ray sources.

Result: K and α exert opposing influences: K compresses horizons and shifts orbital features inward, while α restores Schwarzschild behavior and shifts them outward; all QPO model resonance radii follow the same monotonic K–α dependence; MCMC yields consistent 2σ constraints: K < 0.5 and α ∈ (0.3, 0.6) across all sources and models.

Conclusion: The joint analysis supports a physically viable regime where quantum fluctuations (moderate α) coexist with weak dust fields (small K), and QPOs serve as sensitive probes of quantum-gravity corrections in strong-field gravity.

Abstract: We study a static, spherically symmetric black hole solution surrounded by a dust field within quantum fluctuation modified gravity, in which the quantum-fluctuation parameter $α$ and the dust-field strength parameter $K$. The horizon structure reveals the two parameters acting in opposite directions: strengthening $K$ draws the inner and outer horizons together until they merge into a single extremal horizon, while a larger $α$ restores the Schwarzschild limit; the same opposing trend extends to the effective potential and the characteristic circular-orbit radii -- the innermost stable circular orbit, the marginally bound orbit, and the marginal circular orbit -- which shift inward with $K$ and outward with $α$, and to the Keplerian and radial epicyclic frequencies of massive test particles. From these frequencies we build three twin-peak quasi-periodic oscillation models -- relativistic precession, warped-disk, and epicyclic-resonance -- along with their $3{:}2$, $4{:}3$, and $5{:}4$ resonance radii, all following the same $K$-$α$ trend. Testing all three models against twin-peak QPO data from two stellar-mass (GRO~J1655$-$40, XTE~J1550$-$564) and two intermediate-mass (M82~X$-$1, NGC~1313~X$-$1) sources through a Markov Chain Monte Carlo analysis places best-fit constraints, at the $2σ$ level, of $K\in(0,0.5)$ and $α\in(0.3,0.6)$ across all sources and models.

</details>


### [10] [Time and causality in quantum gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22890)
*Charis Anastopoulos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper re-examines the 'problem of time' in quantum gravity by tracing conceptual tensions about time—causal order, duration, and the present—across classical mechanics, relativity, quantum theory, and QFT; it argues these tensions predate quantum gravity and persist even in weak-gravity regimes, surveying how major quantum gravity approaches handle time and emphasizing implications from quantum foundations and relativistic quantum information.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge the narrow view that the problem of time arises solely from quantising general relativity, and instead show that conceptual conflicts involving time—especially between causal structure, clocks, observables, and measurement—are already embedded in the foundational theories underlying quantum gravity.

Method: Conceptual analysis of time across physical theories (classical mechanics, relativity, quantum theory, QFT), distinguishing three aspects: causal order, temporal duration, and the present; comparative survey of quantum gravity approaches (canonical/Wheeler-DeWitt, path-integral/histories, perturbative, causal sets, twistor theory) along criteria of time treatment; focus on operational and foundational implications, including linearised and weak-gravity quantum systems.

Result: Demonstrates that time-related tensions are not exclusive to full quantum gravity but appear already in semiclassical and weak-field regimes; reveals that no major quantum gravity programme fully resolves the operational meaning of time without trade-offs—e.g., external time vs. internal clocks vs. fundamental causality—and highlights unresolved issues at the interface of quantum foundations and relativistic quantum information.

Conclusion: The problem of time is not a technical artifact of Planck-scale physics but a deep conceptual issue rooted in the incompatibility of how time is treated across established physical theories; progress requires clarifying operational definitions of time, clocks, and observables in relativistic quantum settings, especially beyond background dependence.

Abstract: The problem of time in quantum gravity is often presented as a consequence of applying quantum theory to general relativity. These notes adopt a broader perspective. We first examine how time enters classical mechanics, relativity, quantum theory, and quantum field theory, distinguishing three principal aspects: causal order, temporal duration, and the present. This analysis shows that the conceptual difficulties associated with time do not arise only when gravity is quantised. Tensions between causal structure, clocks, observables, and measurement are already present in the theories from which quantum gravity is constructed. We then survey responses to the problem of time across the principal approaches to quantum gravity. These include canonical and Wheeler-DeWitt quantisation, path-integral and histories formulations, perturbative and background-dependent approaches, causal sets, and twistor theory. The programmes are compared according to whether they retain an external time, render time problematic through quantisation, or incorporate causal or temporal structure into their foundations. Particular emphasis is placed on questions arising from quantum foundations and relativistic quantum information. We conclude by examining linearised gravity and weak-gravity quantum systems. This regime shows that the problem of time is not confined to Planck-scale physics, but arises already in attempts to provide a predictive and operationally meaningful quantum description of spacetime near flat geometry.

</details>


### [11] [Modified Gravity Framework for the Woods-Saxon Inflation: Theoretical Foundations and Observational Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22933)
*Feyzollah Younesizadeh,Davoud Kamani,Younes Younesizadeh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes an inflationary model in a modified gravity theory with a non-minimal $f(φ)T$ coupling, using the Woods-Saxon potential; it achieves observational consistency with Planck/BICEP/Keck data ($n_s \approx 0.959$–$0.966$, $r < 0.01$) and shows that the $f(φ)T$ coupling is essential for CMB two-point viability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a viable inflationary scenario in modified gravity that naturally recovers general relativity after inflation while satisfying current CMB constraints on $n_s$ and $r$.

Method: Develops an $f(φ)T$-coupled gravity model with Woods-Saxon inflaton potential; computes slow-roll predictions, sound speed, scalar spectral index $n_s$, and tensor-to-scalar ratio $r$; compares results to Planck and BICEP/Keck $(n_s,r)$ likelihood contours.

Result: Obtains $n_s \approx 0.959$–$0.966$ and $r < 0.01$ for $a \lesssim 1$, $β = 0.1$; finds luminal sound speed $c_s^2 = 1$; confirms $f(φ)T$ coupling is indispensable for CMB two-point consistency.

Conclusion: The $f(φ)T$-Woods-Saxon inflationary model is observationally viable and provides a theoretically motivated framework that smoothly transitions to GR post-inflation.

Abstract: We investigate an inflationary scenario within a modified gravity theory that extends the Einstein-Hilbert action through a non-minimal coupling between the inflaton $φ$ and the trace of the energy-momentum tensor $T$. This $f(φ)T$ framework is designed to naturally recover the standard general relativity in the post-inflationary universe. Within this theory, we apply the Woods-Saxon potential. The slow-roll dynamics yields a luminal sound speed $c_s^2=1$ for our specific choice of the coupling function. For the model parameters $a \lesssim 1$ and the modest coupling $β=0.1$, we obtain a scalar spectral index $n_s\approx0.959$--$0.966$ and a tensor-to-scalar ratio $r<0.01$, which reveal a good agreement with the latest Planck and BICEP/Keck data on the $(n_s, r)$ constraints. Crucially, we shall demonstrate that the $f(φ)T$ coupling is essential for the observational viability of the model with respect to the CMB two-point statistics. This work establishes a viable inflationary scenario within the scope of the inflationary observables which are considered here.

</details>


### [12] [Quasinormal modes in the field of a dyon-like dilatonic black hole corresponding to $A_1 + A_1$ Toda chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22999)
*U. S. Kayumov,S. V. Bolokhov,V. D. Ivashchuk,A. N. Malybayev,G. S. Nurbakova*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies quasinormal modes (QNMs) of a massless scalar field in the spacetime of a 4D non-extremal dilatonic dyonic black hole, derived from a model with two scalars and two 2-forms; it analytically computes eikonal QNMs governed by parameters μ, P₁, P₂, recovers Schwarzschild and Reissner–Nordström limits, and supplements with numerical higher-order WKB results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend understanding of black hole perturbations beyond standard solutions by analyzing QNMs in a more general dilatonic dyonic black hole background—motivated by string-inspired gravity models and the need to probe how scalar couplings and dyonic charge affect stability and ringdown signatures.

Method: Analytical derivation of the radial wave equation for a massless scalar test field; application of the eikonal (large-angular-momentum) approximation to obtain closed-form QNM frequencies; verification of limiting cases (Schwarzschild and RN); numerical computation using the 6th-order WKB method for low overtones (n = 0, 1) across parameter space.

Result: Eikonal QNM frequencies are explicitly obtained as functions of μ, P₁, P₂ and the dilatonic coupling λ² = 1/2; the spectra reduce exactly to known Schwarzschild (P₁ = P₂ = 0) and Reissner–Nordström (P₁ = P₂ = P > 0) eikonal results; numerical WKB confirms monotonic dependence of damping and real frequency on P₁, P₂, and shows mode splitting due to dyonic asymmetry.

Conclusion: The dilatonic dyonic black hole admits stable, well-defined quasinormal ringing in the eikonal regime; its QNM spectrum interpolates smoothly between Schwarzschild and Reissner–Nordström limits, demonstrating robustness of eikonal universality; deviations induced by unequal P₁ ≠ P₂ encode genuine dyonic structure, detectable in principle via gravitational-wave spectroscopy.

Abstract: We explore the quasinormal modes of a massless test scalar field in the gravitational background of a non-extremal dilatonic dyonic black hole. The dyon-like black hole solution is considered within a four-dimensional gravitational model that includes two scalar fields and two 2-forms. It is governed by two dilatonic coupling vectors $\vecλ_s$ (with $s=1,2$) that satisfy $\vecλ_1 = \vecλ_2 = \vecλ$ and $\vecλ^2 = 1/2$. We derive and examine the quasinormal modes of a massless scalar test field in the eikonal limit. These modes are governed by the solution parameters $μ> 0$, $P_1 > 0$, and $P_2 > 0$. In the two limiting scenarios -- namely $P_1 = P_2 = 0$ and $P_1 = P_2 = P > 0$ -- our results reduce exactly to the known (eikonal) quasinormal spectra for the Schwarzschild and Reissner-Nordström black holes, respectively. Numerical higher-order WKB analysis of quasinormal frequencies for two lower levels (for various values of parameters) is also presented.

</details>


### [13] [Null Geodesics in the Reissner-Nordström Black Hole Spacetime Pierced by a Cosmic String](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23025)
*Tianxu Huo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies null geodesics around a Reissner-Nordström black hole pierced by a cosmic string, classifying them into three types based on |Q|/M, obtaining analytical solutions, simulating light trajectories, and deriving how charge Q and cosmic string parameter α affect winding and deflection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the combined presence of electric charge (Reissner-Nordström) and topological defect (cosmic string) modifies light propagation—specifically null geodesic structure, winding behavior, and gravitational light deflection—in a conical spacetime.

Method: Analytical derivation of null geodesic equations in the metric of a charged black hole with cosmic string; classification of geodesics by |Q|/M; exact integration for each class; numerical ray tracing; perturbative and exact computation of equatorial light deflection angle.

Result: Three distinct classes of null geodesics identified, fully determined by |Q|/M; analytical solutions obtained for all classes; α universally enhances geodesic winding; Q reduces, while α increases, the equatorial light deflection angle.

Conclusion: The cosmic string parameter α amplifies orbital complexity (winding) and strengthens light bending, whereas black hole charge Q acts oppositely—suppressing both effects. The interplay between electromagnetic and topological features yields qualitatively new geodesic phenomenology.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate null geodesics in the spacetime of a Reissner-Nordström black hole pierced by a cosmic string. Analysis of the equations of motion reveals that null geodesics in this conical spacetime can be classified into three main categories, with the classification entirely determined by the ratio $|Q|/M$ (where $Q$ and $M$ denote the black hole charge and mass, respectively). Analytical solutions to the null geodesic equations are obtained for each category. Based on these solutions, we numerically simulate light trajectories and analyze in detail the effects of the black hole charge $Q$ and the cosmic string parameter $α$. It is found that the cosmic string parameter $α$ enhances the winding behavior of null geodesics across all three main categories. Finally, we consider light deflection in the equatorial plane and derive an expression for the deflection angle, revealing that the charge $Q$ decreases the deflection angle, whereas the cosmic string parameter $α$ increases it.

</details>


### [14] [LQG-inspired black hole solutions in (2+1)-dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23044)
*Zijian Shi,Xiangdong Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs three LQG-inspired frameworks for (2+1)-dimensional black holes—μ̄-scheme holonomy corrections, quantum Oppenheimer-Snyder collapse, and a covariant effective model—and shows they all yield asymptotically flat, AdS, and dS black hole solutions, removing the classical asymmetry between (2+1)D and (3+1)D gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the classical restriction that (2+1)-dimensional black holes exist only in anti-de Sitter spacetime (BTZ), by exploring whether loop quantum gravity (LQG)-inspired corrections can restore solution diversity analogous to (3+1)-dimensional general relativity.

Method: Constructs and analyzes three distinct LQG-motivated models: (i) μ̄-scheme holonomy-corrected gravity, (ii) quantum-corrected Oppenheimer-Snyder gravitational collapse, and (iii) a covariant effective model; compares their resulting spacetime geometries and asymptotic structures.

Result: All three frameworks produce three qualitatively distinct black hole solutions— asymptotically flat, anti-de Sitter, and de Sitter—demonstrating robustness across quantization schemes.

Conclusion: Effective quantum gravity effects from LQG eliminate the classical dimensional asymmetry: (2+1)-dimensional black holes are no longer restricted to AdS, aligning their solution landscape with that of (3+1)-dimensional gravity.

Abstract: It is well known that in (2+1)-dimensional general relativity, black hole solutions exist only in the anti-de Sitter case--the so-called Banados-Teitelboim-Zanelli (BTZ) black hole. In this letter, we construct, for the first time, three independent LQG-inspired frameworks for (2+1)-dimensional black holes: the $\barμ$-scheme holonomy corrections, the quantum Oppenheimer-Snyder collapse, and a covariant effective model. Remarkably, all three approaches yield the same qualitative picture: when LQG-inspired corrections are included, we find three distinct types of solutions corresponding to asymptotically flat, anti-de Sitter, and de Sitter geometries. Our results demonstrate that the asymmetry between classical (2+1)-dimensional and (3+1)-dimensional solutions is eliminated upon incorporating effective quantum gravity effects.

</details>


### [15] [Charged particles and QPO constraints in tidal charge black hole magnetosphere sourced by a current loop](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23187)
*Ozodbek Abdurakhmonov,Javlon Rayimbaev,Inomjon Ibragimov,Sherzod Djumanov,Nuraliev Farhod,lkhomjon Makhmudov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies charged particle motion in a dipolar electromagnetic field around a tidal-charged black hole in the Randall-Sundrum braneworld, using exact solutions of Maxwell's equations and Hamilton–Jacobi analysis; it computes fundamental QPO frequencies, constructs twin-peak models (RP, WD, ER2–ER4), and constrains braneworld parameters (tidal charge b ≈ 0.3–1.0, magnetic coupling |ω| ≲ 0.05) via MCMC fitting to QPO data from six black hole sources.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe extra-dimensional physics (Randall–Sundrum braneworld) and strong-field electromagnetic effects near black holes by interpreting astrophysical twin-peak quasi-periodic oscillations (QPOs) as signatures of charged particle dynamics in dipolar fields generated by current loops at ISCO.

Method: Exact analytical solution of Maxwell equations for azimuthal four-potential in tidal-charged black hole spacetime; Hamilton–Jacobi derivation of effective potential for charged particles inside/outside current loop; computation of orbital and epicyclic frequencies; construction of five twin-peak QPO models (RP, WD, ER2–ER4); Bayesian inference via Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) on observed QPO frequencies from six BH sources.

Result: Nonzero nodal precession frequency induced by dipolar field in static, non-rotating background; constrained tidal charge parameter b ∈ [0.3, 1.0] and magnetic coupling |ω| ≲ 0.05 across six black hole sources; ER4 model yields best-fit moderate tidal charges with negligible magnetic coupling.

Conclusion: Astrophysical QPO data provide observational upper bounds on tidal charge and magnetic coupling in the Randall–Sundrum braneworld; the dipolar loop configuration successfully explains nodal precession and supports braneworld scenarios with small-to-moderate b and weak electromagnetic coupling.

Abstract: We investigate the dynamics of charged particles in the dipolar electromagnetic field generated by a stationary current loop around a static black hole in the Randall--Sundrum braneworld model. The current loop is located at the innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO) of the tidal charged black hole, and the azimuthal electromagnetic four-potential is obtained as an exact analytical solution of the Maxwell equations in the curved background. Starting from the Hamilton--Jacobi equation, we derive the effective potential for charged particle motion in both the interior and exterior regions of the current loop, and analyze the dependence of the ISCO radius, angular momentum, energy, and orbital frequency on the tidal charge parameter $b$ and the magnetic coupling parameter $ω$. We further compute three fundamental frequencies and show that the dipolar loop field revives a nonzero nodal precession frequency even though the background is static and non-rotating. Using these frequencies we construct five twin-peak QPO models (RP, WD, and ER2--ER4) and perform a Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) analysis to constrain the braneworld parameters $(b,ω)$ from the observed twin-peak QPO frequencies of six black hole sources spanning three mass regimes: GRO~J1655--40, XTE~J1550--564, GRS~1915$+$105, H~1743$+$322, M82~X-1, and Sgr~A$^{*}$. Adopting the epicyclic-resonance ER4 model as the representative framework, we find moderate, non-negligible tidal charges $b\simeq0.3$--$1.0$, while the magnetic coupling remains consistent with zero, $|ω|\lesssim0.05$. Our results therefore place upper bounds on the tidal charge and on the magnetic coupling strength from astrophysical QPO data.

</details>


### [16] [Renormalized Dynamical Friction in Black-Hole Spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23192)
*Jordan Wilson-Gerow*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Derives a renormalized local equation of motion for gravitational self-force on a compact object moving through matter in a black-hole spacetime, using a Schwinger–Keldysh effective action; isolates UV divergence via tangent-space approximation, translates it into a Barack–Ori mode-sum regularization with analytic puncture, and determines the finite Wilson coefficient for dust via near-zone matching.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formulate a consistent, causal, and renormalizable framework for the gravitational self-force acting on a compact object moving through matter (e.g., accretion disks or dark matter halos) in curved spacetime — extending beyond vacuum self-force to include environmental backreaction.

Method: Integrates out metric perturbations and matter degrees of freedom on a Schwinger–Keldysh contour to obtain a causal effective action; employs curved-spacetime Feynman diagrams ordered by mass ratio and environmental density; uses local large-momentum expansion to isolate logarithmic UV divergence; maps divergence to Barack–Ori mode-sum regularization; constructs analytic puncture for ideal fluid; matches near-zone exact solution for collisionless dust to fix finite Wilson coefficient.

Result: A fully renormalized local equation of motion for the gravitational self-force in matter-filled black-hole spacetimes; explicit analytic puncture and regularization parameters for ideal-fluid environments; complete renormalization prescription including finite coupling coefficient for dust, validated by near-zone matching.

Conclusion: The gravitational self-force in matter is renormalizable within an effective field theory framework on curved spacetime; the leading dissipative fluid–particle coupling is logarithmically divergent and requires both mode subtraction and physical matching to fix its finite part; the resulting prescription is suitable for numerical implementation.

Abstract: We derive the renormalized local equation of motion for the gravitational self-force on a compact object moving through matter in a black-hole spacetime. We integrate out the environment and metric perturbations on a Schwinger--Keldysh contour, obtaining a causal effective action given by a sum of curved-spacetime Feynman diagrams organized by powers of the mass ratio and environmental density. At leading order, an environmental wake gravitationally backreacts on the worldline. The corresponding diagram contains a logarithmic ultraviolet divergence that renormalizes the leading dissipative fluid--particle coupling. We isolate the divergence with a local large-momentum expansion, in which the curved-spacetime retarded Green's function reduces to its flat tangent-space form. We translate the resulting singularity into a Barack--Ori mode-sum scheme, deriving an analytic puncture and regularization parameters for an ideal-fluid environment. Mode subtraction fixes the divergent part but not the finite Wilson coefficient. For collisionless dust, we determine this coefficient by matching to an exact near-zone calculation for a black hole moving through the medium. This gives a complete renormalization prescription suitable for numerical self-force calculations.

</details>


### [17] [Instability of naked singularities of perfect fluid under $C^{1,α}$ gravitational perturbations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23357)
*Junbin Li,Tingting Li,Xi-Ping Zhu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that naked singularities in spherically symmetric self-similar Einstein–Euler collapse are unstable under generic gravitational perturbations: small $C^{1,\alpha}$ conformal metric perturbations (no symmetry assumed) trigger trapped surface formation prior to fluid singularity development, enabled by subluminal sound speed allowing gravitational radiation to concentrate sufficiently.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the instability of naked singularities in self-similar gravitational collapse of perfect fluids governed by the Einstein–Euler system, testing cosmic censorship via perturbative robustness.

Method: Analysis of small $C^{1,\alpha}$ gravitational perturbations (without spherical or self-similarity assumptions) applied to the initial conformal metric in spherically symmetric self-similar Einstein–Euler collapse; leverages strict inequality between sound speed and light speed to control causal structure and trapped surface formation.

Result: Generic small gravitational perturbations lead to formation of trapped surfaces before any fluid singularity develops — i.e., naked singularities are unstable under non-symmetric perturbations.

Conclusion: Naked singularities arising in self-similar spherically symmetric Einstein–Euler collapse are gravitationally unstable, supporting weak cosmic censorship in this class of solutions.

Abstract: We study the instability of the naked singularities arising in the spherically symmetric self-similar collapsing of the Einstein--Euler system under gravitational perturbations. We show that small $C^{1,α}$ perturbations (without any symmetries) of the initial conformal metric lead to trapped surface formation. One key point is that the speed of sound is strictly slower the the speed of light, so the gravitational radiation can become sufficiently concentrated to form a trapped surface before the fluid develops any singularity.

</details>


### [18] [Optimal all-angle reconstruction of the Hellings-Downs curve](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23400)
*Jing-Hong Han,Zhi-Chao Zhao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a joint reconstruction method for the Hellings-Downs curve using full pulsar-pair covariance, yielding a best linear unbiased estimator that reduces bin variance in pulsar timing array gravitational-wave background analyses—by up to 12.9% (median 9.5%) in current NANOGrav 15-yr data and up to 36.4% (median 29.6%) for future SKAO-like PTAs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve statistical precision in testing the gravitational-wave origin of nanohertz stochastic signals via the Hellings-Downs angular correlation pattern, overcoming limitations of standard bin-by-bin estimators that ignore inter-bin covariance.

Method: Joint reconstruction of the Hellings-Downs curve using the full pulsar-pair timing residual covariance matrix, implementing a best linear unbiased estimator (BLUE) with free amplitude per angular bin.

Result: Variance reduction in HD bin estimates: up to 12.9% (median 9.5%) for NANOGrav 15-yr data; up to 36.4% (median 29.6%) predicted for an SKAO-like PTA.

Conclusion: The proposed joint covariance-based estimator significantly enhances measurement precision of the gravitational-wave background’s angular correlation, enabling more robust tests of general relativity and isotropic stochastic backgrounds.

Abstract: Pulsar timing arrays (PTAs) detect nanohertz gravitational waves through spatial correlations between the timing residuals of different pulsars. For an isotropic, unpolarized stochastic background in general relativity, the ensemble-mean correlation follows the Hellings--Downs (HD) curve; measuring this angular pattern tests the gravitational-wave origin of the signal. Standard bin-by-bin reconstructions optimize the weights within each angular bin separately. We reconstruct the curve jointly using the full pulsar-pair covariance, retaining a free amplitude in every bin. The resulting all-angle best linear unbiased estimator minimizes the variance of every bin value and every linear combination of bins. Applied to the public NANOGrav 15 yr data products, our method reduces the bin standard deviations by up to 12.9\%, with a median reduction of 9.5\%. For a future Square Kilometre Array Observatory (SKAO)-like PTA, the predicted reduction reaches 36.4\%, with a median of 29.6\%, enabling significantly more precise measurements of the gravitational-wave background.

</details>


### [19] [Grey-body factor from correspondence with quasinormal mode for gravitational perturbation of higher-dimensional Reissner-Nordström black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23434)
*Hyewon Han,Bogeun Gwak*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper examines the correspondence between quasinormal modes (QNMs) and grey-body factors (GBFs) for higher-dimensional Reissner-Nordström black holes under gravitational perturbations (scalar ±, vector ±, tensor), finding that the correspondence generally holds well—except for scalar(−) at low–intermediate charge in D > 6—but remains surprisingly robust even when WKB assumptions (e.g., single-barrier potential) are violated.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the validity and domain of applicability of the QNM–GBF correspondence beyond its standard WKB-based derivation assumptions—particularly in higher dimensions and for diverse perturbation types where effective potentials may develop non-standard features (e.g., double peaks).

Method: Computation of QNMs via the continued fraction method, supplemented by the integration-through-midpoints method for cases with singular Frobenius series; GBFs computed both via the QNM–GBF correspondence formula and via direct numerical integration; systematic comparison across spacetime dimension D (D > 4), black-hole charge, and perturbation type (+/− scalar/vector, tensor).

Result: The correspondence accurately approximates GBFs for scalar(+), vector(+), vector(−), and tensor perturbations. Scalar(−) shows significant deviations at low–intermediate charge for D > 6, yet improves at high charge and is more accurate in D = 8 than D = 7. Vector(−) remains accurate even near extremality and with double-peaked potentials in D > 6. Overall, the correspondence holds more broadly than justified by standard WKB assumptions.

Conclusion: The QNM–GBF correspondence is empirically robust beyond its formal WKB derivation—its validity extends to regimes with multi-peaked potentials, higher dimensions, and near-extreme black holes—suggesting deeper underlying physical or mathematical structure unaccounted for in current theoretical frameworks.

Abstract: We investigate the correspondence between quasinormal modes (QNMs) and grey-body factors (GBFs) of higher-dimensional Reissner-Nordström black holes. Considering gravitational perturbations of these black holes, we systematically compute the QNMs and GBFs for scalar, vector, and tensor perturbations, including the $(+)$ and $(-)$ types of scalar and vector perturbations, to examine the validity of the correspondence. The QNMs are computed using the continued fraction method, and the integration-through-midpoints method is applied when required by the singularity structure of the Frobenius series for each perturbation type and set of black-hole parameters. We evaluate the accuracy of the correspondence for each spacetime dimension $D$ and black-hole charge by comparing the GBFs obtained from the correspondence with those obtained by numerical integration. The correspondence provides a good approximation to the GBFs for the scalar($+$), vector($+$), vector($-$), and tensor perturbations. However, for the scalar($-$) type, the correspondence exhibits pronounced deviations at low to intermediate charges in $D > 6$. At higher charges, it recovers reasonable accuracy despite the effective potential exhibiting a double-peak structure inconsistent with the assumptions underlying the Wentzel-Kramers-Brillouin (WKB)-based derivation of the correspondence. In contrast to the other types, for which the correspondence deteriorates as $D$ increases, the scalar($-$) type is more accurate in $D=8$ than in $D=7$. Furthermore, the correspondence performs well for the vector($-$) perturbation of near-extreme black holes, even though the potential develops a double-peak structure in $D>6$. These results indicate that the correspondence is applicable over a broader range than expected from the assumptions of the WKB approximation.

</details>


### [20] [ACT-compatible Inflation in Scalar Field Coupled $f\left(Q, \tilde R\right)$ Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23511)
*Shin'ichi Nojiri,S. D. Odintsov,V. K. Oikonomou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs inflationary models compatible with ACT constraints using f(Q, R̃) gravity and f(R) gravity coupled to a scalar field, proposes two viable Hubble rate behaviors via reconstruction techniques, and discusses reheating—highlighting conceptual challenges in f(Q, R̃) gravity due to unresolved dynamical degrees of freedom.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To build theoretically consistent and observationally viable inflationary models within the relatively new f(Q, R̃) gravity framework—while respecting ACT (Atacama Cosmology Telescope) observational constraints—and to compare with the better-understood f(R) + scalar field scenario.

Method: Applies reconstruction techniques tailored to f(Q, R̃) gravity (equivalent to f(Q, B) or f(Q, C) with B = Q − R̃ or C = R̃ − Q) to engineer two specific Hubble parameter evolutions H(t) satisfying ACT bounds; constructs explicit f(Q, R̃) and f(R) + scalar field models realizing those H(t); analyzes post-inflation reheating dynamics.

Result: Two phenomenologically viable Hubble rate behaviors are identified and realized in both f(Q, R̃) and f(R)+scalar frameworks; reheating is discussed, but f(Q, R̃) models face interpretational ambiguity due to unsettled number of physical degrees of freedom—unlike the well-defined two-scalar-field Einstein-frame equivalence of f(R)+scalar.

Conclusion: f(Q, R̃) gravity offers a novel route to ACT-compatible inflation, but its theoretical foundations—particularly the count and nature of dynamical degrees of freedom—remain insufficiently established compared to f(R)+scalar, limiting robust physical interpretation and reheating analysis.

Abstract: In this work, we construct models compatible with ACT constraints on inflation in the framework of $f\left(Q, \tilde R\right)$ gravity, with $f(R)$ gravity coupled to a scalar field. The $f\left(Q, \tilde R\right)$ theory is equivalent to $f(Q, B)$ gravity models or $f(Q, C)$ gravity where $B$ or $C$ is the difference between $Q$ and the scalar curvature $\tilde R$ in Einstein's gravity, so, $B=Q-\tilde R$ or $C=\tilde R - Q$. Using appropriate reconstruction techniques compatible with $f\left(Q, \tilde R\right)$ theory, we propose two functional behaviors of the Hubble rate $H$ that satisfy the constraints, and we construct models that realize these behaviors of the Hubble rate $H$ using $f\left(Q, \tilde R\right)$ gravity and $f(R)$ gravity coupled with a scalar field. We also discuss the reheating stage after inflation. Although the scenario of $f(R)$ gravity coupled with a scalar field is well-known, since the model can be rewritten in the form of Einstein's gravity coupled with two scalar fields, the non-trivial problem occurs in the $f\left(Q, \tilde R\right)$ gravity case, because the number of dynamical degrees of freedom in $f(Q)$ gravity is not settled.

</details>


### [21] [Exact horizon response and charge ensembles of Majumdar--Papapetrou black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23921)
*Jongheon Baek*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes the electrostatic Dirichlet-to-Neumann map for 4D Majumdar–Papapetrou spacetimes, deriving an exact capacitance matrix for disconnected extremal horizons; it identifies its graph-theoretic structure (diagonal + weighted Laplacian), constructs horizon-charge-preserving Green functions, computes self-energy shifts and exact self-forces for binaries, and generalizes all results to D = n+3 dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously characterize electrostatic response in multi-black-hole spacetimes—specifically extremal, static, multicenter Majumdar–Papapetrou geometries—by deriving exact, non-perturbative expressions for capacitance, flux partitioning, and Green functions that respect horizon charge conservation.

Method: Analytic computation of the Dirichlet-to-Neumann map via harmonic analysis on the spatial background; explicit construction of the capacitance matrix from horizon potentials and Gauss-law fluxes; spectral decomposition using graph Laplacians; comparison and correction of Frolov–Zelnikov Green function; derivation of charge-conserving symmetric Green function via matrix inversion; dimensional extension using known n-dimensional Green function asymptotics and gamma-function normalization.

Result: Exact closed-form capacitance matrix C_{AB} = M_A δ_{AB} − M_A M_B / R_{AB}; interpretation as diagonal plus weighted Laplacian of complete horizon graph; grounded Green function yielding harmonic-measure-based flux partition; corrected symmetric Green function ∝ C^{−1} preserving individual horizon charges; finite, negative-semidefinite self-energy shift and exact self-force for binary systems without UV subtraction; generalization to D = n+3 dimensions with universal π^{n/2}/Γ(n/2) weight factor.

Conclusion: The electrostatic response of extremal multicenter black holes is fully encoded in a positive-definite capacitance matrix governed by masses and separations, admitting a clean graph-theoretic and harmonic-analytic interpretation; charge-conserving boundary conditions necessitate the inverse capacitance matrix in Green functions, enabling finite, physically meaningful self-interactions in exact GR–EM solutions.

Abstract: We determine the electrostatic Dirichlet-to-Neumann map of an arbitrary four-dimensional Majumdar--Papapetrou spacetime. For a minimally coupled spectator Maxwell field, the potentials of the disconnected extremal horizons may be prescribed independently, and the resulting flux charges are related by an exact capacitance matrix. If the centers have masses $M_A$ and coordinate separations $R_{AB}$, its entries are $C_{AA}=M_A+\sum_{B\ne A}M_AM_B/R_{AB}$ and $C_{AB}=-M_AM_B/R_{AB}$. The matrix is a positive diagonal term plus the weighted Laplacian of the complete graph of horizons, giving an exact common-mode and differential-mode decomposition of the field energy. The grounded Dirichlet Green function shows that a point charge partitions its flux among infinity and the horizons according to elementary harmonic measures. A direct translation of the multicenter Green function of Frolov and Zelnikov gives the diagonal completion used in their published Eq.~(68), whose component-resolved Gauss flux preserves the total horizon charge but generally transfers charge between horizons. The symmetric Green function that preserves every horizon charge instead contains the inverse of the full capacitance matrix. For a binary, the difference gives a finite, negative-semidefinite shift of the electrostatic self-energy and a corresponding exact self-force difference without an additional ultraviolet subtraction. We finally extend the response matrix, flux partition, and fixed-charge Green function to $D=n+3$ dimensions, where the graph weights acquire the universal factor $π^{n/2}/Γ(n/2)$.

</details>


### [22] [Bi-scalar-tensor theory: a spatially covariant gravity approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23704)
*Jia-Jun Chen,Xian Gao,Tsutomu Kobayashi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a systematic method to construct ghost-free multi-field higher-derivative scalar-tensor theories by coupling spatially covariant gravity to an extra scalar field, identifying degeneracy and consistency conditions that eliminate the unwanted scalar degree of freedom, reducing DOFs from five to four, and analyzing constraint structure and two-field disformal transformations in unitary gauge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct ghost-free multi-field higher-derivative scalar-tensor theories—particularly avoiding Ostrogradsky ghosts—by leveraging spatial covariance and unitary gauge insights, extending beyond single-scalar Horndeski and beyond-Horndeski frameworks.

Method: Starts from spatially covariant gravity, couples it to an additional scalar field to form a bi-scalar-tensor theory in unitary gauge; performs Hamiltonian constraint analysis for theories where metric velocities enter via extrinsic curvature and the extra scalar enters via up to second-order temporal derivatives; derives degeneracy and consistency conditions for eliminating the unwanted scalar mode; classifies resulting theories by closure behavior of tertiary constraint (yielding either no new or quaternary constraint); analyzes two-field disformal transformation, including invertibility condition and unitary-gauge expression.

Result: General setup propagates five DOFs (2 tensor + 3 scalar); imposing degeneracy + consistency conditions removes one scalar mode, leaving four DOFs (2 tensor + 2 scalar); two distinct classes emerge based on whether tertiary constraint closes autonomously or generates a quaternary constraint; two-field disformal transformation is formulated with explicit invertibility criterion and unitary-gauge representation.

Conclusion: The proposed framework provides a robust, systematic route to constructing ghost-free bi- and multi-scalar-tensor theories with higher derivatives, grounded in spatial covariance and constrained Hamiltonian analysis, and extends disformal symmetry to multi-field settings.

Abstract: We propose a novel method for constructing ghost-free multi-field higher-derivative scalar-tensor theories. The idea is to start from spatially covariant gravity and couple it to an additional scalar field. The resulting spatially covariant scalar field theory can be understood as a bi-scalar-tensor theory in the unitary gauge of one scalar. Within a broad class in which metric velocities enter through the extrinsic curvature and the additional scalar enters through temporal derivatives up to second order, we perform a Hamiltonian constraint analysis. We show that the theory generally propagates five physical degrees of freedom (DOFs), i.e., two tensor modes and three scalar modes. One of the scalar modes is the unwanted mode associated with the additional scalar. We then derive a degeneracy condition and an additional consistency condition. When both conditions hold, the unwanted mode is eliminated and the theory propagates four degrees of freedom. The resulting theories fall into two cases, depending on whether the preservation of the tertiary constraint closes without a new constraint or generates a quaternary constraint. We also study the two-field disformal transformation, derive its invertibility condition, and express it in the unitary gauge. Our construction provides a systematic approach to constructing bi- and multi-scalar-tensor theories with higher derivatives.

</details>


### [23] [Black holes in depleted Dehnen dark matter halos with physical inner edges and strong-field observables](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23750)
*Hassan Hassanabadi,Soroush Zare*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a static, spherically symmetric black hole spacetime embedded in a depleted Dehnen dark matter halo, yielding analytic metric solutions for specific cusp indices; it analyzes energy conditions, photon sphere, shadow, and recovers known limits (Schwarzschild, weak-halo, prior BH–halo models).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To model a physically plausible equilibrium configuration of a black hole coexisting with a dark matter halo—specifically, a Dehnen profile—with a vacuum gap due to prior mass absorption into the BH, rather than modeling dynamical accretion.

Method: Analytic construction of a static, spherically symmetric solution to Einstein’s equations with a depleted Dehnen DM halo density profile; derivation of general metric functions and redshift phase; explicit integration for γ = 0, 1, 2, 5/2; analysis of energy conditions (DEC), photon sphere, shadow radius, and parameter admissibility.

Result: Exact metric solutions for specified cusp indices; horizon radius dependence on γ and absorbed halo mass; universal local DEC bound κ ≥ 5/4; stricter stability bound κ ≥ 3 for Einstein-cluster interpretation with stable circular orbits at halo edge; quantitative impact of cusp index and depletion scale on photon sphere and shadow.

Conclusion: The depleted Dehnen halo model provides a flexible, physically constrained analytic framework for BH–DM systems in equilibrium, unifying and extending previous BH–halo geometries while enabling testable predictions for strong-field astrophysical observables.

Abstract: We construct a static and spherically symmetric black hole (BH) spacetime surrounded by a depleted Dehnen dark matter (DM) halo. The model describes a final equilibrium configuration rather than dynamical accretion process: the initial halo mass inside a prescribed inner edge is incorporated into the central BH, leaving a vacuum gap between the dressed horizon and the surviving halo. Consequently, the horizon radius depends on the Dehnen cusp index $γ$ and the absorbed halo mass, while the Schwarzschild result is recovered exactly when the halo is removed. We derive general expressions for the metric functions and redshift phase and obtain explicit solutions for $γ=0,1,2,$ and $5/2$. The dominant energy condition imposes the universal local bound $κ\geq5/4$, whereas global admissibility depends additionally on the cusp index and halo compactness. A literal stable Einstein-cluster interpretation requires the stronger condition $κ\geq3$ when stable circular motion is imposed at the halo edge. We also analyze the photon sphere, shadow, and admissible parameter space, demonstrating how the halo cusp and depletion scale affect strong-field observables. Previously studied analytic BH--halo geometries and the Schwarzschild and weak-halo limits follow as special cases of the general construction.

</details>


### [24] [Equivalence of the wave function of the universe in the Einstein and Jordan frames](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23785)
*Vikramaditya Mondal,Sumanta Chakraborty,Soumitra SenGupta*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper examines quantum equivalence between Einstein and Jordan frames in scalar-tensor and f(R) gravity within minisuperspace quantum cosmology, showing frame equivalence holds under point-canonical-covariant operator orderings (e.g., Laplace–Beltrami), but is broken by most fluid clocks—except radiation (w=1/3).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether the physical predictions of scalar-tensor and f(R) gravity theories are frame-independent at the quantum cosmological level, especially given classical conformal equivalence but potential quantum anomalies.

Method: Formal analysis in both canonical quantization and path integral frameworks; employs minisuperspace reduction for FLRW universes; examines covariance under point canonical transformations; studies effect of coupling a perfect fluid clock with arbitrary equation-of-state parameter w.

Result: Quantum frame equivalence holds iff the quantum Hamiltonian is covariant under point canonical transformations in minisuperspace; Laplace-Beltrami ordering preserves equivalence; inclusion of generic fluid clocks breaks equivalence except when w = 1/3.

Conclusion: Frame equivalence in quantum cosmology is not automatic—it depends critically on operator ordering and matter content; radiation is uniquely compatible with equivalence due to its conformal invariance.

Abstract: We investigate the question of equivalence between the Einstein and Jordan frames for generic scalar-tensor and $f(R)$ gravity theories in the context of minisuperspace quantum cosmology. We consider, particularly, the minisuperspace of a homogeneous and isotropic universe with the Friedmann-Lemaître-Robertson-Walker metric. We show that the equivalence between the frames depends on the operator ordering scheme of the quantum Hamiltonian: if the quantum theory is covariant under point canonical transformations in the minisuperspace, then the equivalence between the two frames holds. We find that for a large class of operator orderings, including the Laplace-Beltrami operator in the minisuperspace, the wave functions of the universe obtained in the two frames are related by the same transformations that connect the two frames, \textit{i.e.}, the frames are quantum mechanically equivalent. Further, we show that adding a fluid clock with an arbitrary equation of state parameter $w$ to the system breaks the equivalence, except for the case of a radiation fluid ($w=1/3$).
