All our arguments are presented at a formal level in both canonical and path integral approaches, and for any scalar-tensor, $f(R)$ theories without assuming any particular model.

</details>


### [25] [Signatures in the local interstellar medium of electromagnetic radiation produced by neutron star gravitational waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23865)
*Preston Jones,Pragati Pradhan,Douglas Singleton*

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The long-standing mystery of the astrophysical origin of Voyager-detected ~kHz plasma oscillations—persisting even after entering the local interstellar medium—and the lack of a viable theoretical mechanism linking neutron stars to such frequencies.

Method: Theoretical analysis applying the recently developed framework of electromagnetic radiation co-produced in gravitational wave backgrounds, specifically evaluating its applicability to neutron star sources and consistency with observed ~kHz oscillation frequencies.

Result: The study finds that the co-production mechanism can plausibly generate electromagnetic signals in the few-kHz range from neutron stars embedded in astrophysically realistic gravitational wave backgrounds.

Conclusion: Neutron stars remain viable candidates for the Voyager oscillations if electromagnetic emission arises not solely via conventional magnetospheric processes, but through co-production with gravitational waves—a mechanism that naturally yields the observed frequency band.

Abstract: The Voyager spacecraft have been detecting plasma oscillations of a few kHz for over 40 years. However, there is still no widely accepted hypothesis for the astrophysical source of the oscillations. Having entered the local interstellar medium, these detections have persisted, adding to the challenge of identifying the astrophysical source. Neutron stars were one of the earliest hypothesized candidates of the electromagnetic radiation responsible for the detected plasma oscillations, but no theoretical mechanism could be identified to explain the frequency of the oscillations. We explore the recently developed theory of co-production of electromagnetic radiation in gravitational wave backgrounds as a possible source of these low-frequency oscillations and investigate whether neutron stars could be the astrophysical source.

</details>


### [26] [Soliton solution of a gravitating chiral-quark soliton model: dynamical fermion and Dirac-sea in general relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24159)
*Ryoma Mizutani,Daiki Niiyama,Nobuyuki Sawado*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends the chiral-quark soliton model (CQSM) to static spherically symmetric curved spacetime coupled with Einstein gravity, solving the Einstein–Dirac system self-consistently; it reveals that the Dirac sea suppresses gravitational coupling and can induce negative ADM mass for large dynamical fermion mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate gravitational effects on fermion dynamical mass and extend the CQSM—originally a hadronic model from large-N_c QCD—to include full general relativistic backreaction in static spherical symmetry.

Method: Self-consistent numerical solution of the coupled Einstein–Dirac equations with a nonlinear chiral field; regularization of the Dirac sea via one-fermion-loop effective action; spectral flow analysis of fermion eigenenergies and ADM mass.

Result: Successful self-consistent solution showing Dirac-sea-induced suppression of energy-momentum tensor's gravitational impact; emergence of an 'inactive point' where gravity decouples despite localized matter; spectral flow of fermion energies and ADM mass; onset of negative ADM mass for large dynamical mass M ∼ ⟨ψ̄ψ⟩.

Conclusion: The Dirac sea fundamentally modifies gravitational coupling in chiral fermionic systems: it dilutes local energy-momentum contributions and can yield unphysical (negative) ADM mass, signaling limitations or necessary refinements of the semiclassical treatment in strongly coupled Einstein–Dirac solitons.

Abstract: In this paper, we study Einstein-Dirac system based on a Dirac fermion coupled with a nonlinear chiral field on static spherically symmetric spacetime. Gravitational effects on the dynamical mass of fermions are examined. The chiral-quark soliton model (CQSM) originally was a model of hadron inspired by the low-energy regime of large-$N_\textrm{c}$ QCD, realizing localized fermions with the full inclusion of the Dirac-sea, which is derived from a regularized one-fermion loop. We extend the CQSM in spherically symmetric curved spacetime and coupled with the Einstein gravity. We successfully solve the CQSM and the Einstein equation self-consistently, and obtain the spectral flow of the fermion energies and also of the ADM mass. The Dirac-sea dilutes the effect of the energy-momentum tensor, which induces an inactive point for the impact of gravity even when the localizing matter exists. The Dirac-sea effect becomes dominant for larger dynamical mass $M\sim \langle\barψψ\rangle$, leading to the emergence of the negative ADM mass.

</details>


### [27] [Stationary Rotating Geometries Associated with Nonsingular Pulsating Collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.28850)
*A. D. Souza,F. A. Brito,J. Spinelly*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a rotating black hole solution by extending a singularity-avoiding static geometry (inspired by LQC/braneworld models) via the Newman–Janis algorithm; it recovers Kerr/Schwarzschild limits as quantum corrections vanish, and performs extended thermodynamic analysis including Davies singularities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the GR singularity problem in black holes by incorporating high-density quantum/braneworld corrections, while preserving realistic astrophysical features like rotation and thermodynamics.

Method: Applies the Azreg-Aïnou variant of the Newman–Janis algorithm to rotate a static, singularity-free exterior geometry (Gao–Lu–Shen–Faraoni model) coupled to a bouncing comoving interior; analyzes horizon structure, surface gravity, Hawking temperature, and extended thermodynamics including state-space curvature and Davies points.

Result: A novel stationary, axisymmetric rotating spacetime metric is derived; its horizons, angular velocity, surface gravity, and Hawking temperature reduce smoothly to Kerr and Schwarzschild limits as the correction parameter l → 0; thermal response reveals Davies-type singularities and yields an extended thermodynamic state-space relation.

Conclusion: The rotating extension successfully embeds singularity avoidance into a physically viable black-hole-like geometry with consistent rotational and thermodynamic properties, supporting effective high-curvature corrections as a pathway toward quantum gravity phenomenology.

Abstract: Since the first exact solutions of General Relativity (GR) were obtained, it became clear that the theory predicts a new class of compact objects: black holes. The Hawking--Penrose singularity theorems show that, under appropriate assumptions, gravitational collapse in GR leads to singular behavior. Motivated by the expectation that such regimes require physics beyond classical GR, effective approaches inspired by Loop Quantum Cosmology (LQC) and braneworld scenarios introduce high-density corrections. In this work we consider the static exterior geometry introduced by Gao, Lu, Shen, and Faraoni, associated with a comoving interior model in which the singularity is avoided through a sequence of collapse and bounce phases. Using the Newman--Janis algorithm in the Azreg-Aïnou formulation, we construct a stationary rotating exterior extension of this geometry. The resulting line element describes a stationary axisymmetric rotating spacetime whose horizon geometry, angular velocity, surface gravity, and Hawking temperature recover the Kerr and Schwarzschild limits when the quantum/braneworld correction becomes negligible ($l\to 0$). We further analyze the thermal response at fixed rotational state-space parameter, identify Davies-type singular points, and formulate an extended thermodynamic state-space relation.

</details>


### [28] [Signatures of Novikov-Thorne accretion disks around rotating black holes in semiclassical gravity with trace anomaly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24273)
*Yuxiang Zuo,Shiyang Hu,Dan Li,Chen Deng,Guansheng He,Wenbin Lin*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how semiclassical gravity with type-A trace anomaly affects thermal radiation from Novikov-Thorne accretion disks around rotating black holes, finding that quantum backreaction (via a coupling parameter) suppresses emission—especially for high-spin black holes—by shifting stable orbits outward and reducing radiative efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe quantum gravitational effects on black hole astrophysics by examining how semiclassical corrections (specifically type-A trace anomaly) modify accretion disk observables, offering a pathway to observationally constrain the coupling parameter and test semiclassical gravity against general relativity.

Method: Analytical and numerical modeling of Novikov-Thorne thin accretion disks around rotating black holes in semiclassical gravity with type-A trace anomaly; computation of energy flux density, temperature profile, and thermal luminosity across parameter space (spin, coupling strength).

Result: The coupling parameter suppresses disk emission: lower peak energy flux density, lower peak temperature, reduced thermal luminosity, and shifted (lowered) peak frequencies; suppression intensifies with black hole spin; stable circular orbits move outward, increasing specific orbital energy and decreasing radiative efficiency.

Conclusion: Thermal disk spectra provide an astrophysically viable probe to distinguish semiclassical gravity from general relativity and to observationally constrain the trace anomaly coupling parameter, particularly for high-spin black holes.

Abstract: Semiclassical gravity provides a promising framework to address the black hole singularity problem through quantum backreaction characterized by a coupling parameter, making it essential to thoroughly explore the accretion properties around such objects. In this paper, within the framework of semiclassical gravity with type-A trace anomaly, we investigate the thermal radiative signatures of Novikov-Thorne accretion disks around rotating black holes across diverse parameter spaces, focusing on the energy flux density, temperature profile, and thermal luminosity. Our findings demonstrate that the introduction of the coupling parameter suppresses disk emission, leading to lower peak values of both energy flux density and temperature, as well as lower peak values and corresponding peak frequencies in thermal luminosity compared to their Kerr counterparts in general relativity. In particular, this discrepancy becomes remarkably pronounced when the black hole possesses a high spin. Mechanistically, increasing the coupling parameter reinforces the effective gravitational field strength of the central compact object, which pushes the equatorial stable circular orbits outward and increases the specific energy of the orbiting matter, thereby reducing the radiative efficiency and weakening the overall disk emission. This suppression mechanism offers a practical astrophysical window to distinguish semiclassical gravity from general relativity and to place observational constraints on the coupling parameter via thermal disk spectra.

</details>


### [29] [A novel phenomenological parametrization of dynamical dark energy via matter-gravity coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24361)
*Hrishikesh Chakrabarty,Daniele Malafarina*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a novel parametrization for deviations from ΛCDM using the Markov-Mukhanov action with non-minimal matter-gravity coupling, yielding dynamical Λ and G; it constructs a phenomenological model generating dynamical dark energy naturally at low densities and applies it to interpret DESI’s phantom-crossing hints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a theoretically grounded, flexible parametrization for beyond-ΛCDM cosmologies that incorporates dynamical dark energy as an emergent feature of modified gravity—specifically, non-minimal matter-gravity coupling—rather than an ad hoc fluid component.

Method: Constructs a cosmological model based on the Markov-Mukhanov action with non-minimal coupling; derives effective dynamical Λ and G; develops a simple phenomenological realization; confronts model predictions with cosmological observations (e.g., DESI) via parameter inference.

Result: Demonstrates that dynamical dark energy arises naturally at low densities; obtains observational constraints on model parameters; shows consistency with tentative DESI evidence for phantom crossing (w < −1 transition).

Conclusion: Non-minimal matter-gravity coupling offers a compelling theoretical origin for dynamical dark energy and phantom behavior; the proposed framework bridges fundamental gravity modifications and observable late-time cosmology, enabling testable alternatives to ΛCDM.

Abstract: We develop a new parametrization for generic departures from the $Λ$CDM model based on the Markov-Mukhanov action with non-minimal matter-gravity coupling. In this approach the cosmological constant $Λ$ and the gravitational coupling $G$ become dynamical with their values determined by the explicit form of the matter-gravity coupling. We use the framework to build a simple phenomenological model where dynamical dark energy is naturally obtained as a consequence of the departure of the theory from classical General Relativity at low densities. We show how the model's free parameters can be constrained from observational data and interpret the tentative observations of `phantom crossing' by the DESI collaboration in view of the proposed formalism.

</details>


### [30] [Joule Thomson expansion of symmergent black holes in extended phase space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24400)
*Saheb Soroushfar,Sayyed Mehrab Ramezani*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies Joule-Thomson expansion of symmergent black holes in extended thermodynamic phase space, finding that the inversion curve is independent of the symmergent parameter α̂, while isenthalpic curves retain α̂-dependence; results admit holographic interpretation in asymptotically AdS-like spacetimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how symmergent gravity—a framework generating Einstein gravity and Standard Model fields from emergent symmetries—affects black hole thermodynamics, specifically the Joule-Thomson (JT) expansion, within an extended phase-space formalism where pressure is a thermodynamic variable.

Method: Extended phase-space thermodynamics applied to symmergent black holes; derivation of JT coefficient, inversion curve, and isenthalpic curves using symmergent entropy with parameter α̂; analysis of thermodynamic regimes (cooling vs. heating); holographic interpretation via asymptotically AdS-like geometry.

Result: The JT coefficient depends only on 8πPr₊², yielding an α̂-independent inversion curve P_i = (π/2) I_i²; isenthalpic curves explicitly depend on α̂; explicit expressions for inversion pressure and temperature obtained; cooling (P > P_i) and heating (P < P_i) regimes identified; holographic duality interpretation established for the isenthalpic process.

Conclusion: Symmergent parameters modify thermodynamic trajectories (e.g., isenthalpic curves) but leave the JT inversion boundary invariant; the framework consistently extends black hole thermodynamics to symmergent gravity and supports a dual field-theoretic interpretation in AdS/CFT-like settings.

Abstract: We investigate the Joule Thomson expansion of a symmergent black hole within an extended phase-space formalism in which the thermodynamic pressure is introduced as an independent variable. The symmergent entropy contains the parameter alpha-hat, which modifies the enthalpy and thermodynamic volume relative to their standard forms. We show that the Joule Thomson coefficient depends only on the combination 8piP*r_+^2, leading to an inversion curve P_i = (pi/2) * I_i^2 that is independent of alpha-hat. The isenthalpic curves, however, retain a parametric dependence on alpha-hat, indicating that the symmergent sector affects the thermodynamic trajectories but not the inversion boundary itself. The inversion pressure and temperature are derived explicitly, and the cooling regime P > P_i is distinguished from the heating regime P < P_i. Since the effective geometry is asymptotically AdS-like for positive pressure, the results also admit a holographic reading, in which the Joule Thomson expansion corresponds to an isenthalpic process in the dual field theory. The results provide a consistent thermodynamic framework for symmergent black holes and clarify the role of the symmergent parameters in the Joule Thomson process.

</details>


### [31] [Do gravitational waves assist the genuine tripartite entanglement harvesting?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24428)
*Xiang-Yue Yu,Xiao-Li Huang,Shu-Min Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how gravitational waves affect genuine tripartite entanglement harvested by three Unruh-DeWitt detectors coupled to a scalar field, revealing nonmonotonic, frequency- and separation-dependent responses—more sensitive than bipartite entanglement—suggesting its utility as a quantum probe of gravitational wave-induced spacetime perturbations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether genuine tripartite entanglement can serve as a more sensitive quantum probe of gravitational wave-induced spacetime perturbations compared to bipartite entanglement.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of entanglement harvesting by three linearly arranged Unruh-DeWitt detectors locally coupled to a massless scalar field in a gravitational wave background; quantification of genuine tripartite entanglement (e.g., via convex roof extended negativity or similar measures) and comparison with bipartite entanglement.

Result: Gravitational waves induce a nonmonotonic response in genuine tripartite entanglement: successive suppression–enhancement (resonance-like)–suppression with increasing GW frequency; nonmonotonic dependence on detector separation featuring local enhancement peaks absent in bipartite case; higher sensitivity to GW parameters than bipartite entanglement.

Conclusion: Genuine tripartite entanglement is a more sensitive quantum probe of gravitational wave perturbations than bipartite entanglement, due to its richer, nonmonotonic parametric dependencies—offering potential for novel quantum-gravitational sensing schemes.

Abstract: We investigate the harvesting of genuine tripartite entanglement by three linearly arranged Unruh-DeWitt detectors locally coupled to a massless scalar field in a gravitational wave background. We show that gravitational waves play a dual role in entanglement harvesting, either enhancing or suppressing the harvested entanglement depending on the system parameters. In particular, genuine tripartite entanglement exhibits a pronounced nonmonotonic response to the gravitational wave frequency, characterized by successive suppression, enhancement resembling resonance, and renewed suppression, while approaching its corresponding value in Minkowski spacetime in the regime of high frequencies. Its dependence on the detector separation is also nonmonotonic, displaying local enhancement peaks that are absent in the corresponding bipartite entanglement behavior. Compared with bipartite entanglement, genuine tripartite entanglement therefore exhibits a higher sensitivity to gravitational wave perturbations in both the frequency and spatial domains. These results suggest that genuine tripartite entanglement may provide a sensitive quantum probe of spacetime perturbations induced by gravitational waves.

</details>


### [32] [How Universal Is the Black Hole Zeroth Law?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24648)
*Rajes Ghosh,Sudipta Sarkar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper shows that the black hole zeroth law—constancy of surface gravity on a stationary Killing horizon—is not universally enforced across gravitational theories; instead, different theories realize it via distinct horizon-projection components of their field equations (e.g., mixed null-transverse in GR and Lovelock theories, null-null in generalized quadratic gravity), enabling the first exact, non-perturbative proof of the zeroth law in broad higher-curvature gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The status of the black hole zeroth law beyond general relativity is poorly understood; prior proofs rely on specific assumptions or perturbative frameworks, leaving open whether a universal mechanism enforces surface gravity constancy in modified gravity.

Method: Analysis of horizon-projected field equations across gravitational theories—including general relativity, f(R), Lovelock, and generalized quadratic gravity—to identify which component (e.g., mixed, null-null) enforces constancy of surface gravity on stationary Killing horizons; leverages geometric constraints of stationarity and Killing horizons without perturbative expansion.

Result: The zeroth law holds exactly in generalized quadratic gravity, with its validity enforced by the null-null component of the field equations—not the mixed component as in GR/Lovelock theories—yielding the first non-perturbative, exact proof in generic higher-curvature gravity.

Conclusion: The zeroth law is not governed by a universal mechanism but serves as a sharp diagnostic to classify gravitational theories by the horizon-projection component responsible for surface gravity constancy, revealing deep connections between horizon thermodynamics and the algebraic structure of field equations.

Abstract: The black hole zeroth law asserts the thermal equilibrium of a stationary Killing horizon, yet its status beyond general relativity remains limited. We demonstrate that its validity is not governed by a universal mechanism: distinct gravitational theories enforce constancy of the surface gravity through different horizon-projections of their field equations. In general relativity, $f(R)$-gravity, and Lovelock theories, the zeroth law is known to follow from the mixed (null-transverse) horizon equations. By contrast, we show that in non-Lovelock theories the zeroth law is enforced by qualitatively different mechanisms. In generalized quadratic gravity, for instance, the null-null component of the field equations dictates its validity. Exploiting this novel diagnostic, we obtain the first exact proof of the zeroth law in the broader landscape of higher-curvature gravity, without relying on a perturbative expansion around general relativity. Our result establishes the zeroth law as a sharp probe of the underlying thermodynamic structure of gravity, and points toward a classification of modified theories according to the mechanism responsible for its validity.

</details>


### [33] [Formalizing a unified dynamical system framework for $f(R)$ and $f(Q)$ gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24733)
*Saikat Chakraborty*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a unified dynamical systems framework for f(R) and coincident gauge f(Q) gravity using dimensionless variables—cosmographic parameters, dark energy equation of state w and its derivatives, and theory-space parameters—without auxiliary variables; closure is achieved via specifying the theory, cosmological evolution, or w(z) evolution; key findings include distinct dynamical behaviors: GR is a past attractor in f(Q) but not in f(R), and heteroclinic trajectories to de Sitter are generic in f(Q) but initial-condition-sensitive in f(R).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of traditional Hubble-normalized formalisms in analyzing higher-order or non-metric gravity theories like f(R) and f(Q), and to enable direct, variable-minimal comparison of their cosmological dynamics.

Method: Constructs a unified autonomous dynamical system using three equivalent, auxiliary-variable-free closures: (1) theory specification (e.g., Hu-Sawicki f(R), f(Q) = -2Λ + Q + β√(-Q)), (2) prescribed cosmographic evolution (e.g., j(z) = 1), and (3) prescribed w(z) evolution (e.g., w(z) = -1); analyzes phase-space structure, fixed points, stability, and heteroclinic orbits.

Result: f(Q) gravity generically admits heteroclinic trajectories from GR-matter domination to late-time de Sitter, with GR as a past attractor; f(R) exhibits similar trajectories only under fine-tuned initial conditions and lacks GR as a past attractor; both theories can kinematically (j=1) and dynamically (w=-1) mimic ΛCDM, but their underlying phase-space structures differ fundamentally.

Conclusion: The unified formulation reveals intrinsic dynamical distinctions between f(R) and f(Q) gravity: f(Q) possesses greater structural robustness and GR-as-attractor behavior, suggesting it may be more naturally aligned with standard cosmological history than f(R) within this class of modifications.

Abstract: Going beyond the traditional Hubble-normalized framework, we formalize a unified dynamical system formulation for $f(R)$ and coincident gauge $f(Q)$ gravity using three distinct sets of dimensionless variables: (1) kinematical cosmographic parameters, (2) the dark energy equation of state parameter $w$ (with its $N=\ln a$ derivatives) and matter density abundance $Ω_m$, and (3) theory space parameters $\{m_i\}$ characterizing the shape of the theory function. Our formulation is free of auxiliary variables, closing via three equivalent ways: (1) specifying a theory, (2) requiring a specific cosmological evolution, or (3) requiring a specific dark energy equation of state evolution.
  Each closure strategy is illustrated with explicit examples. The theory closure approach studies a case of Hu-Sawicki $f(R)$ and $f(Q)=-2Λ+Q+β\sqrt{-Q}$ models, where traditional formalisms fail. A heteroclinic trajectory from a GR-matter-dominated epoch to a late-time de-Sitter epoch is general for $f(Q)$, but sensitive to initial conditions for $f(R)$. The cosmographic and equation-of-state closure approaches compare $f(R)$ and $f(Q)$ gravities kinematically equivalent to $Λ$CDM ($j(z)=1$) and dynamically equivalent to $Λ$CDM ($w(z)=-1$). In both, GR acts as a cosmological past attractor for $f(Q)$ gravity, but not for $f(R)$.

</details>


### [34] [Constraining the geometry of rotating black holes with eikonal QNMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24824)
*Ciro De Simone,Sebastian H. Völkel,Kostas D. Kokkotas*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper compares metric-specific (global parametrization) and metric-agnostic (local light-ring quantities) Bayesian models for inferring black hole properties from quasinormal modes, showing that uniform priors on global parameters induce skewed priors on local metric quantities, but multi-parameter analyses yield robust—though degenerate—constraints consistent with Kerr for both GR-compatible and GW250114 data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess how theory-dependent modeling choices—specifically global metric parametrizations versus local light-ring descriptors—affect inference of black hole properties from quasinormal modes in gravitational-wave ringdowns, especially under potential deviations from general relativity.

Method: Connects a calibrated eikonal method (linking QNM shifts to local metric properties at the light ring) with rotating black hole metrics; applies Bayesian inference to compare: (i) a global Konoplya-Rezzolla-Zhidenko (KRZ) parametrization model, and (ii) a local model using metric functions and their radial derivatives at the light ring; performs one-parameter and simultaneous multi-parameter analyses; maps priors between parameterizations; validates on synthetic injections and GW250114 ringdown posteriors.

Result: Uniform priors on KRZ coefficients induce nonuniform, skewed priors on local light-ring quantities; one-parameter analyses fail to recover injected local deviations and bias global reconstruction in the KRZ case; multi-parameter analyses yield consistent constraints but exhibit strong degeneracies and divergent marginalized posteriors; both models are consistent with Kerr when applied to GW250114.

Conclusion: Local, metric-agnostic modeling provides more interpretable and prior-robust inference of light-ring physics, while global parametrizations risk misleading reconstructions unless multi-parameter flexibility and careful prior calibration are employed; the framework confirms consistency of current ringdown data with Kerr in general relativity.

Abstract: Quasinormal modes of black holes provide a central avenue for confronting general relativity with gravitational-wave observations from the ringdown stage of compact binary coalescence. However, inferring black-hole properties or the background spacetime requires theory-dependent input. In this work, we connect a calibrated eikonal method, which relates quasinormal mode shifts to local metric properties near the light ring, with the underlying rotating black hole metric. Using Bayesian inference, we compare a metric-specific model based on the global Konoplya-Rezzolla-Zhidenko parametrization with a metric-agnostic approach based on local values and radial derivatives of the metric functions at the light ring. For each model, we perform both one-parameter and simultaneous multi-parameter analyses and quantify how their priors map onto the same local quantities. We find that uniform priors on the metric-specific coefficients can induce strongly nonuniform and skewed priors on these local metric quantities. For an injection compatible with general relativity, both models remain consistent with Kerr and yield similar posteriors for the orbital frequency and Lyapunov exponent despite their different induced priors. For injections representing deviations from general relativity, one-parameter analyses can fail to recover the injected local deviations and, in the metric-specific case, can bias the global metric reconstruction. The multi-parameter analyses of both models yield consistent constraints, suggesting sufficient robustness. However, they introduce substantial degeneracies and differences in marginalized parameters, demonstrating practical limitations when considering more flexible models. Finally, we apply the framework to approximate posterior information from the ringdown analysis of GW250114 and find that both models are consistent with the Kerr metric in general relativity.

</details>


### [35] [Boson Star Factory: Past, Present, and Future](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24913)
*Romain Gervalle,Jorge Filipe Mónico Delgado,Etevaldo dos Santos Costa Filho,Carlos Herdeiro,Víctor Jaramillo,Eugen Radu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper compares three numerical methods (finite differences, finite elements, spectral methods) for solving the Einstein-Klein-Gordon equations for non-spherical boson stars—specifically rotating and static dipolar configurations—and establishes high-precision reference solutions; it also demonstrates physics-informed neural networks for a rotating Q-ball.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Lack of systematic comparison among independent numerical implementations for constructing non-spherical boson stars, despite their relevance as horizonless compact objects in General Relativity.

Method: Numerical solution of nonlinear elliptic PDEs using three independent codes: FIDISOL/CADSOL (finite differences), FreeFem (finite elements), and Kadath (spectral methods); cross-validation via global observables and runtime benchmarking; supplementary demonstration of physics-informed neural networks for rotating Q-balls.

Result: All three solvers agree on global observables to ~8 significant digits; precise benchmark values for physical quantities (e.g., mass, angular momentum, field amplitude) are provided; runtime comparisons performed on identical hardware; PI-NN successfully constructs a rotating Q-ball solution.

Conclusion: The study establishes robust, high-accuracy reference data for non-spherical boson star configurations and validates methodological consistency across distinct numerical paradigms, facilitating future studies and code verification.

Abstract: Boson stars are self-gravitating solitons of the Einstein-Klein-Gordon equations for a massive complex scalar field, and arguably the simplest horizonless compact objects arising in General Relativity. Beyond spherical symmetry, their construction requires solving a system of nonlinear elliptic partial differential equations, a difficult task per se and that each group has traditionally addressed with its own numerical implementation. A systematic comparison of different methods applied to the same physical model has so far been lacking. In this work, we carry out such a comparison for the two simplest non-spherical configurations: rotating boson stars and static dipolar boson stars. We employ three independent codes, based respectively on finite differences (FIDISOL/CADSOL), finite elements (FreeFem), and spectral methods (Kadath), and assess their accuracy through different diagnostics. The three solvers agree on the global observables to typically eight significant digits. We further compare the runtime of each code on identical hardware and provide precise benchmark values of the relevant physical quantities, intended to serve as reference data for future numerical studies of boson stars. As a further proof of concept, we also present the use of physics-informed neural networks to construct a rotating Q-ball solution.

</details>


### [36] [Observational Features of Nonsingular Rotating Black Holes in the Dark-Energy Dominated Universe](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24926)
*Ramon Torres*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how an evolving dark energy, modeled as a curvature-dependent cosmological term, affects the shadows of nonsingular rotating black holes—contrasting them with Kerr and Kerr–de Sitter geometries—and identifies shadow area and vertical diameter as key observables to constrain the effective regularization scale of the running cosmological term.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent DESI BAO, CMB, and SN Ia data hint at time-evolving dark energy; quantum field theory in curved spacetime and quantum gravity approaches with running couplings further motivate a curvature-dependent cosmological term.

Method: The authors formulate null geodesics using a super-Hamiltonian in phase space with a Mino-type parameter; compute black hole shadows via backward ray tracing from a zero-angular-momentum observer; define and analyze standard shadow observables (size, shape, displacement), focusing on area and vertical diameter.

Result: Shadow area and vertical diameter exhibit clear, distinguishable signatures of the running cosmological term; joint analysis of multiple strong-field observables across black holes of varying mass and spin enables tight constraints—or potential measurement—of the effective regularization scale and statistical discrimination from Kerr/Kerr-dS.

Conclusion: Nonsingular rotating black hole shadows in a dark-energy-dominated universe with a curvature-dependent cosmological term provide a novel, observationally viable probe of dark energy evolution and quantum-gravity-motivated modifications to Λ, complementing cosmological probes.

Abstract: Recent DESI baryon acoustic oscillation measurements, combined with cosmic microwave background and type-Ia supernova data, suggest that dark energy may evolve with cosmic time. Along a cosmological background, an evolving dark-energy density can be expressed locally as a function of a monotonic curvature invariant, motivating an effective curvature-dependent cosmological term. Moreover, quantum field theory in curved spacetime and approaches to quantum gravity with running gravitational couplings provide complementary motivations for exploring such a term.
  Here we study how the phenomenology of nonsingular rotating black holes embedded in a Universe dominated by dark energy can differ substantially from that of the Kerr and Kerr--de Sitter geometries. The problem is directly formulated in phase space using a null geodesic super-Hamiltonian and a Mino-type parameter. The shadows of the black holes are calculated by backward ray tracing from a zero-angular-momentum observer. We define and analyze a comprehensive set of standard shadow observables, characterizing the size, shape, and displacement of the shadow. We show that the area of the shadow and its vertical diameter provide particularly clear signatures of a running cosmological term. In this way, joint analyses of multiple strong-field observables and of several black holes with different masses and spins could provide a realistic route to tightly constraining, or potentially measuring, the effective regularization scale behind the running of the cosmological term and statistically distinguishing the non-singular black hole geometry from Kerr/Kerr-dS solutions.

</details>


### [37] [Analytical estimates of core optics stray light noise and design requirements for ground based gravitational wave detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25003)
*M. Andrés-Carcasona,M. Evans*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces an analytical framework to translate detector sensitivity requirements into stray light constraints for the core optics of ground-based gravitational wave interferometers, enabling early-stage conservative design specifications before full optical layout is finalized.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish conservative, pre-layout stray light requirements for core optics in ground-based gravitational wave interferometers—critical for mitigating noise from scattered and ghost light that could degrade detector sensitivity.

Method: Derives analytical estimates for multiple scattering regimes (e.g., surface scatter, bulk scatter), extends analysis to planar baffle arrays, and formulates analogous requirements for deterministic ghost beams intercepted by beam dumps.

Result: Obtains compact, physics-based expressions for stray light budgeting; successfully applies the framework to Cosmic Explorer to derive concrete, sensitivity-driven stray light requirements for next-generation detectors.

Conclusion: The framework provides a practical, analytical toolset for early stray light budgeting and design trade-offs in current and future gravitational wave detectors, bridging sensitivity targets and optical hardware specifications.

Abstract: We present an analytical framework to translate detector sensitivity into stray light requirements for the core optics region of ground based gravitational wave interferometers. We derive analytical estimates for several relevant scattering regimes, extend the treatment to collections of planar baffles, and obtain an analogous requirement for deterministic ghost beams intercepted by beam dumps. The framework is intended to provide conservative design requirements before a complete optical layout is available. We apply it to Cosmic Explorer to show how these estimates can be used to derive requirements for next generation detectors. The resulting expressions provide a compact set of tools for stray light budgeting and early design studies in current and future gravitational wave detectors.

</details>

### [38] [First-Principles Determination of the QCD Contribution to the Axion-Photon Coupling Using Domain-Wall Fermions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22698)
*Tian Lin,Hao-Yang Cheng,Xu Feng,Lu-Chang Jin,Chuan Liu,Qi-Yuan Luo*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper presents a high-precision lattice QCD calculation of the QCD contribution $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}$ to the axion-photon coupling, resolving prior discrepancies between NLO chiral perturbation theory predictions; using domain-wall fermions and anomalous Ward identities on near-physical $N_f=2+1$ ensembles with continuum extrapolation, it obtains $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD} = 1.965(35)$, reconciles tensions with ChPT, and provides a first-principles benchmark.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve puzzling ~8% discrepancies among next-to-leading-order chiral perturbation theory (NLO ChPT) predictions for the model-independent QCD contribution $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}$ to the axion-photon coupling — a quantity critical for axion searches and testing the strong CP problem solution — via a precise, first-principles lattice QCD determination.

Method: Lattice QCD computation of pseudoscalar-to-two-photon three-point functions using domain-wall fermions on two near-physical $N_f=2+1$ ensembles; employs anomalous Ward identities to separate $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}$ into the exact chiral anomaly term and a light-quark-mass-suppressed correction; performs continuum extrapolation; method is complementary to prior background-field approaches.

Result: $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}^{\rm IS} = 1.619(30)$ (isospin-symmetric), $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}^{\rm IB} = 0.347(22)$ (isospin-breaking), total $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD} = 1.965(35)$; identifies and resolves sources of apparent tension with published NLO ChPT results.

Conclusion: The lattice result provides the first robust, first-principles benchmark for $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}$, confirms consistency with properly interpreted NLO ChPT, and validates the use of domain-wall fermions and Ward identities for precision anomalous couplings in QCD.

Abstract: The axion-photon coupling, crucial for experimental axion searches and tests of the strong CP solution, receives a substantial model-independent contribution $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}$ from QCD dynamics. Next-to-leading-order chiral perturbation theory (NLO ChPT) in different frameworks has yielded puzzling discrepancies of up to $8\%$, motivating precise first-principles calculations. We present an independent lattice QCD determination using a method complementary to the recent background-field calculation. Computing pseudoscalar-to-two-photon three-point functions and exploiting anomalous Ward identities, we separate $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}$ into an exact anomaly contribution and a light-quark-mass-suppressed correction. The latter is computed using domain-wall fermions, whose excellent chiral symmetry strongly suppresses discretization effects. Working on two near-physical $N_f=2+1$ ensembles with continuum extrapolation, we obtain $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}^{\rm IS}=1.619(30)$ (isospin-symmetric), $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}^{\rm IB}=0.347(22)$ (isospin-breaking), and $\mathcal C_{\rm QCD}=1.965(35)$ in total. While direct comparisons with published NLO ChPT predictions reveal apparent tensions, we identify their sources and show that the ChPT results can be reconciled with our lattice determination. Our result provides a first-principles benchmark for the QCD contribution to the axion-photon coupling and a quantitative test of competing ChPT descriptions.

</details>


### [39] [Flowed quark field renormalization in lattice QCD: A Ward-identity approach and its validation using quark bilinears](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23379)
*Mei-Qi Gao,Long-cheng Gui,Jun Hua,Jian Liang,Jun Shi*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper introduces a non-perturbative Ward-identity-based method to compute the flowed quark field renormalization factor $Z_χ$, using vector-current normalization and ratios of flowed/unflowed meson two-point functions—avoiding costly ringed diagrams. It is validated across multiple channels and lattice ensembles, showing consistency with established RI/MOM and RI/SMOM schemes and convergence toward the continuum limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome computational expense and numerical noise of the conventional 'ringed' prescription for flowed quark field renormalization in lattice QCD, especially for fermionic operators subject to gradient flow.

Method: A Ward-identity prescription based on vector-current conservation; constructs $\mathring{Z}_χ^{V}(t_f,a)$ from ratios of flowed and unflowed meson two-point functions, normalized via the conserved vector current; applied on five $2+1$-flavor clover ensembles with varying lattice spacings.

Result: $\mathring{Z}_χ^{V}(t_f,a)$ is determined and cross-validated in pseudoscalar, scalar, axial-vector, and tensor channels; renormalized matrix elements agree with RI/MOM and RI/SMOM after sequential continuum and zero-flow-time extrapolations; discretization-dependent deviations diminish at finer lattice spacings.

Conclusion: The proposed Ward-identity prescription is viable, systematically validated, and provides a robust, non-perturbative framework for renormalizing flowed fermionic operators in future high-precision lattice QCD calculations.

Abstract: We present a non-perturbative Ward-identity prescription for determining the flowed quark field renormalization factor $Z_χ$, avoiding the computational difficulties of the conventional ringed prescription. The method is based on vector-current normalization and ratios of flowed and unflowed meson two-point functions. We determine the resulting $\mathring{Z}_χ^{V}(t_f,a)$ on five $2+1$-flavor clover ensembles and validate it in the pseudoscalar, scalar, axial-vector, and tensor channels. Renormalized matrix elements obtained through sequential continuum and zero-flow-time extrapolations agree with independent RI/MOM and RI/SMOM determinations. The finite-lattice-spacing bilinear renormalization factors show differences that decrease toward finer lattices, reflecting the different discretization effects of the renormalization methods. The cross-channel agreement demonstrates the viability of the proposed prescription; together, the method and its systematic validation establish a robust foundation for the non-perturbative renormalization of flowed fermionic operators in future lattice calculations.

</details>

### [40] [Coherent deeply virtual Compton scattering on helium-4 beyond the leading twist approximation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22405)
*V. Martínez-Fernández,B. Pire,P. Sznajder,J. Wagner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes deeply virtual Compton scattering (DVCS) on helium-4 to probe its quark-gluon structure and produce the first 3D tomographic image of quarks and gluons in ^4He, incorporating leading-twist, twist-3, and twist-4 contributions and emphasizing NLO corrections for JLab kinematics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To access the quark and gluon structure of light nuclei and enable three-dimensional nuclear tomography via coherent hard exclusive reactions.

Method: Theoretical analysis of deeply virtual Compton scattering (DVCS) on helium-4, including leading-twist contributions as well as kinematical twist-3 and twist-4 effects; numerical estimation of cross sections and asymmetries for JLab-relevant kinematics; inclusion of next-to-leading-order (NLO) QCD corrections.

Result: Quantitative predictions for DVCS cross sections and asymmetries in JLab kinematics; identification of significant contributions from NLO and kinematical twist-3/4 terms; derivation of the first tomographic image of quark and gluon distributions in the helium-4 nucleus.

Conclusion: Coherent DVCS on ^4He, when treated beyond leading twist and including NLO corrections, provides a viable pathway to nuclear 3D tomography; the extracted tomographic image represents the first such visualization of quark and gluon spatial and momentum structure in a light nucleus.

Abstract: Coherent hard exclusive reactions on light nuclei offer access to their quark and gluon structure and provide a framework for three-dimensional nuclear tomography. We analyze deeply virtual Compton scattering on a helium-4 target, including leading-twist contributions as well as kinematical twist-3 and twist-4 effects. We present numerical estimates of cross sections and asymmetries for kinematic regimes relevant to JLab experiments. We emphasize the importance of next to leading order (NLO) and kinematical twist 3 and 4 contributions to interpret data in the JLab kinematics. We deduce a first tomographic image of quarks and gluons in the helium-4 nucleus.

</details>


### [41] [Resonant Gravitational Production of Chiral Dark Photons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22423)
*Leah Jenks,Marc Kamionkowski,Edward W. Kolb*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates chiral, parity-odd couplings between a massive vector field (dark photon) and the inflaton during inflation, identifying two resonant production mechanisms—tachyonic instability and parametric resonance—that enhance dark-photon abundance, broaden viable dark matter parameter space, yield chiral (helicity-biased) populations with parity-violating signatures, and encode reheating history in the number density spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how parity-odd couplings of vector fields to the inflaton during inflation can resonantly amplify gravitational particle production, thereby generating viable dark-photon dark matter and yielding observable chiral/parity-violating imprints.

Method: Analytical and semi-numerical analysis of quantum production of a massive vector field coupled via a pseudoscalar (inflaton) interaction during slow-roll inflation; classification into tachyonic vs. parametric resonance regimes based on coupling strength and mass; helicity decomposition; computation of mode functions, occupation numbers, and late-time number density spectra; incorporation of post-inflationary reheating dynamics.

Result: Two distinct production regimes are identified; transverse vector modes dominate over longitudinal ones due to chiral coupling; tachyonic regime yields maximally chiral (single-helicity) dark photons; viable dark-photon DM parameter space is significantly extended; number density spectrum carries characteristic features encoding reheating temperature and duration.

Conclusion: Chiral inflaton–vector couplings during inflation provide a robust, observationally distinctive mechanism for generating dark-photon dark matter, with intrinsic chirality and reheating-dependent spectral features offering new avenues for probing early-universe physics.

Abstract: Vector fields with a chiral, parity-odd coupling to a pseudoscalar field are known to undergo resonant amplification. In this work, we study this phenomenon in the context of cosmological gravitational particle production during early-universe inflation. We consider a massive vector (dark photon) field with a parity-odd coupling to the inflaton. There are two regimes of production, delineated by the relative size of the coupling and the dark photon mass, arising from a tachyonic instability, and a parametric resonance, respectively. As a result of the coupling, the transverse modes, subdominant in both the minimally and nonminimally coupled theories, can dominate the total late-time abundance. The viable parameter space for dark-photon dark matter is thus widened. Furthermore, in the tachyonic case, the production is dominated by a single helicity mode, leading to a population of chiral dark photons that can lead to potential parity-violating observational signatures. We further explore the dependence of the production on the reheating history, and find that information about reheating is encoded in the number density spectrum.

</details>


### [42] [Tackling the noisy truncated moment problem with Gaussian Processes: An application to parton distribution functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23196)
*Rohith Karur*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper addresses the noisy truncated Hausdorff moment problem for reconstructing parton distribution functions (PDFs) using Gaussian Process (GP)-based Bayesian inference, introducing novel bias-correction and adaptive covariance kernels, and validating robust performance across diverse phenomenological PDF datasets — with direct applicability to lattice QCD moment data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome ill-posedness and noise sensitivity in reconstructing PDFs from truncated, noisy moment data — a core challenge in hadronic physics where direct functional reconstruction from moments is unstable and conventional methods lack principled uncertainty quantification.

Method: Bayesian regression with Gaussian Processes: includes kernel design (novel x-adaptive kernels), analytical posterior characterization, Monte Carlo hyperparameter sampling, bias correction for posterior mean, and validation via multiple metrics; grounded in analysis of the Hausdorff moment problem’s identifiability limits across PDF classes.

Result: Robust PDF reconstruction from moderate numbers of moments across 5 diverse phenomenological datasets (valence, gluon, sea quark, pion/nucleon); novel tools resolve persistent bias and scale-mismatch issues unaddressed by standard GP or deterministic inversion methods.

Conclusion: The proposed GP framework provides a flexible, statistically rigorous, and practically effective solution to the noisy truncated Hausdorff moment problem for PDF reconstruction, enabling reliable inference from lattice QCD moments and offering generalizable methodology for ill-posed inverse problems in theoretical physics.

Abstract: In this work, we study the noisy truncated Hausdorff moment problem in the context of recovering parton distribution functions (PDFs) from their moments. We start by reviewing basics of the Hausdorff moment problem and analyze how the relation between the amount of moment data provided and the ability for $f(x)$ to be reconstructed varies across different classes of parton distribution functions; we then turn to using Bayesian methods centered around Gaussian Processes (GPs) to solve the moment problems. Standard aspects of Bayesian regression such as covariance kernel choice, analytical characterization of the posterior, hyperparameter sampling with Monte Carlo methods, and validation metrics are detailed. In addition to this, powerful novel tools, such as a method to correct for mean bias in posterior distributions, and a simple class of covariance kernels whose $x$ correlation length is learned during sampling are introduced.
  Finally, we test our framework on 5 datasets from valence, gluon, and sea quark phenomenological pion and nucleon PDF datasets, selected to reflect diversity in PDF behavior. We find that our GP framework robustly reconstructs PDFs given a moderate number of moments. Additionally, the novel tools developed in this work resolve persistent issues that would be difficult to address via conventional means. The presented framework can be readily applied to moments obtained via lattice Quantum Chromodynamics (LQCD) to reconstruct PDFs from theoretical first principles.

</details>


### [43] [Gravitational Waves from Long Strings and Loops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22430)
*Christos Litos,Sarunas Verner,Wei Xue,Fengwei Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the gravitational wave (GW) spectrum from a global cosmic string network in the scaling regime, splitting it into infrared (analytically derived via unequal-time correlator of stress-energy tensor in the unconnected segment model generalized to loops) and ultraviolet (data-driven, calibrated to lattice simulations) components; the spectrum exhibits distinct power-law behaviors (k and k³ in IR, plateau with log tilt in UV), with turnover set by the axion mass; constraints and forecasts on axion parameter space (fa, ma) are provided using GW observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To predict the gravitational wave spectrum from global cosmic strings—relevant for axion-like models—and bridge analytical modeling (infrared) with numerical simulation results (ultraviolet) to enable observational tests of axion physics.

Method: Analytical derivation of the infrared GW spectrum using the unequal-time correlator of the string stress-energy tensor within the generalized unconnected segment model including loops; development of a data-driven method to connect this correlator formalism to instantaneous radiation power spectra from lattice simulations for the ultraviolet regime; spectral matching at the correlation length scale; confrontation with GW detector sensitivities.

Result: The predicted GW energy density spectrum Ω_GW(k) rises as ∝ k and k³ in the infrared, flattens to a plateau with mild logarithmic tilt in the ultraviolet; turnover frequency is set by the Goldstone boson (axion) mass m_a; quantitative constraints and projections in the (f_a, m_a) plane are obtained using current (e.g., LIGO, PTA) and future (e.g., LISA, Einstein Telescope) GW data.

Conclusion: Global cosmic strings produce a distinctive, calculable GW spectrum whose shape and amplitude encode axion parameters; combining analytical theory and simulation-informed modeling enables robust GW-based probes of high-scale symmetry breaking and ultralight scalar physics.

Abstract: We compute the gravitational wave spectrum produced by a global cosmic string network in the scaling regime. The spectrum naturally divides at the string correlation length into infrared and ultraviolet parts. In the infrared, we derive the spectrum analytically from the unequal-time correlator of the string stress-energy tensor within the unconnected segment model, which we generalize for the first time to string loops. The loop contribution can be comparable to that of long strings, depending on the loop evolution parameters. In the ultraviolet, where structure below the correlation length dominates the source, we develop a data-driven method that connects this correlator formalism to the instantaneous radiation power spectrum measured in existing lattice simulations. The predicted spectrum rises with frequency as $Ω_{\rm GW}\propto k$ and $k^{3}$ in the infrared, and flattens into a plateau with a mild logarithmic tilt in the ultraviolet. The turnover frequency between the two is set by the mass of the Goldstone boson, the axion. Confronting the predicted spectrum with current data and projected sensitivities, we map out current constraints and future probes in the plane of the symmetry-breaking scale~$f_a$ and the axion mass~$m_a$.

</details>


### [44] [Spectroscopic parameters of $B_c$ meson](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24536)
*Sinem Küçükyılmaz,Halil Mutuk*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the $B_c$ meson using a nonrelativistic quark model, computing its mass spectrum (including $S$-, $P$-, and $D$-wave excited states), decay constants (with QCD corrections), radiative widths (E1/M1), and Regge trajectories; it predicts inverted fine-structure ordering due to OGE–confinement competition, suppressed M1 widths from small hyperfine splittings, and identifies $B_c^*$ mass and $P$-wave fine structure as critical experimental tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe spin-dependent dynamics in the $B_c$ system — a unique heavy-heavy meson — by computing spectroscopic and decay observables inaccessible to pure lattice or perturbative QCD, and to guide experimental searches for excited states.

Method: Nonrelativistic quark model with phenomenological confining + one-gluon-exchange potential; includes one-loop QCD corrections to decay constants; computes E1/M1 transition widths via dipole matrix elements; constructs radial Regge trajectories from calculated masses.

Result: Good agreement for ground and first radial excitation masses with data; decay constants consistent with relativistic models and predict $\mathcal{B}(B_c \to \tau\nu_\tau)$; E1 cascade $D \to P \to S$ favored for detecting $D$-wave states; inverted $P$- and $D$-wave fine structure due to OGE–confinement interplay; M1 widths strongly suppressed; near-linear radial Regge slopes decreasing with $L$.

Conclusion: The $B_c$ system offers a clean laboratory for testing spin-dependent QCD forces; precise measurement of $B_c^*$ mass and $P$-wave multiplet fine structure is essential to discriminate between competing spin-orbit mechanisms.

Abstract: We investigate the spectroscopic and decay properties of the $B_c$ meson within a nonrelativistic quark model. We calculate the mass spectrum of radially and orbitally excited states in the $S$-, $P$-, and $D$-wave sectors, the pseudoscalar and vector decay constants with one-loop QCD corrections, the E1 and M1 radiative widths, and radial Regge trajectories. The ground state and its first radial excitation agree well with experiment. The predicted decay constants are in line with relativistic model estimates and yield a corresponding prediction for the leptonic decay $B_c\toτν_τ$. The E1 transition pattern is governed primarily by radial-wave-function overlaps, and the dominant transitions of the lowest $D$-wave multiplet suggest the $D\to P\to S$ radiative cascade as a promising pathway to the unobserved $D$-wave states. The model predicts an inverted fine-structure ordering in the $P$- and $D$-wave multiplets, reflecting the competition between the one-gluon-exchange (OGE) and confinement contributions to the spin--orbit interaction. The allowed M1 widths are strongly suppressed by the small predicted hyperfine splittings, highlighting the importance of a direct $B_c^*$ mass measurement. The radial Regge trajectories are nearly linear across all considered families, with slopes decreasing as the orbital angular momentum increases. These results identify the $B_c^*$ mass and the fine-structure ordering of the lowest $P$-wave multiplet as the key measurements for testing the spin-dependent dynamics of the $B_c$ system.

</details>


### [45] [Non-Perturbative Modulus Decay and Multi-Component Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22438)
*Gordon Kane,Leia Price,Luis Rufino,Scott Watson,Fred Adams*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the dynamical robustness of non-thermal cosmological histories—particularly modulus-dominated eras—against non-perturbative decay, using an effective field theory from G₂ compactifications. It finds Wino-like fermion production strongly suppressed, but axion production via a modulus-dependent kinetic term exhibits narrow instability bands with growth rates exceeding Hubble expansion; however, nonlinear effects (backreaction, rescattering, expansion, higher-order operators) prevent definitive conclusions without full nonlinear simulation. Thus, standard cosmology remains robust against Wino/gauge preheating, but axion channel may significantly modify dark matter composition and dark radiation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether the standard non-thermal cosmological scenario—featuring coherent scalar oscillations, early matter domination by a modulus condensate, and perturbative gravitationally suppressed decays—is dynamically stable when non-perturbative (preheating) decay channels are included.

Method: Applies Floquet analysis within an effective field theory framework derived from G₂ compactifications of string theory to study parametric resonance and instability bands for Wino-like fermions and axions coupled to an oscillating modulus; assesses relevance of nonlinear effects (backreaction, cosmic expansion, rescattering, higher-dimension operators).

Result: Non-perturbative Wino production is strongly suppressed; axion production exhibits narrow instability bands with Floquet exponents > Hubble rate, but linear analysis is insufficient to quantify energy transfer due to dominant nonlinear dynamics.

Conclusion: The conventional modulus-dominated cosmology is robust against Wino and gauge-field preheating, but axion preheating remains a potentially significant, nonlinearly sensitive channel that could alter dark matter relic abundances (Wino/axion split) and enhance dark radiation.

Abstract: Non-thermal cosmological histories rest on the assumption that scalars displaced during inflation undergo coherent oscillations, dominate the energy density of the universe before Big Bang Nucleosynthesis, and decay perturbatively through gravitationally suppressed interactions. In addition, usually a single dark matter component is assumed. This early matter-dominated era is the basis for the usual predictions of non-thermal WIMP production, axion dark matter, entropy generation, and dark radiation. This paper examines whether this picture is dynamically robust against non-perturbative decay of the modulus condensate. In this work, we study non-perturbative particle production from oscillating moduli using the effective field theory appropriate to $G_2$ compactifications. We find that non-perturbative production of Wino-like fermions is strongly suppressed in the relevant parameter regime. In contrast, a modulus-dependent axion kinetic term admits narrow instability bands with growth rates that can exceed the Hubble rate. A linear Floquet analysis alone, however, cannot determine whether these bands significantly deplete the modulus condensate, since cosmic expansion, backreaction, rescattering, and higher-order operators in the effective theory can become important. As a result, the conventional modulus-dominated cosmology remains robust against Wino and gauge-field preheating, while the axion channel provides a potentially important modification that requires nonlinear study. If sufficiently efficient, axion production can alter the division of dark matter between Winos and axions and enhance the dark-radiation abundance.

</details>


### [46] [Interpreting the LZ 248 keV Event using Dark QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24988)
*Francesco Sannino,Jessica Turner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes a composite inelastic dark matter model to explain a nuclear-recoil candidate observed by LZ, using a dimension-ten operator in an SU(3)_D confining theory, with endothermic scattering mediated by a leptophobic vector boson; a benchmark with 1 TeV dark baryon mass, 300 keV splitting, and cross section 6.5×10⁻⁴³ cm² fits LZ’s 90% CL and evades solar neutrino/dijet constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) Collaboration's observed nuclear-recoil candidate (248 ± 23_stat ± 23_sys keV) within a theoretically consistent and phenomenologically viable dark matter framework beyond standard WIMP paradigms.

Method: Constructs a composite inelastic dark matter scenario where the lightest dark baryon—arising from a confining SU(3)_D gauge theory—is split into two Majorana states via a dimension-ten effective operator; models endothermic scattering off nuclei mediated by a leptophobic vector boson coupled to baryon number; computes the expected recoil spectrum and compares with LZ sensitivity and published confidence intervals; checks consistency with solar neutrino flux limits and LHC dijet resonance bounds.

Result: A benchmark point with dark-baryon mass 1 TeV, mass splitting 300 keV, and nucleon cross section 6.5×10⁻⁴³ cm² lies within LZ’s two-sided 90% confidence interval; the corresponding mediator-to-coupling ratio is fixed as M_V/g_B ≈ 9.9 TeV, and the model satisfies existing solar neutrino and dijet experimental constraints.

Conclusion: Composite inelastic dark matter with baryon-number-coupled leptophobic vector mediation provides a viable and testable explanation for the LZ nuclear-recoil candidate, demonstrating compatibility with current direct detection limits and complementary collider/astrophysical constraints.

Abstract: The LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) Collaboration has reported a nuclear-recoil candidate with reconstructed energy $248\pm23_{\rm stat}\pm23_{\rm sys}\,\mathrm{keV}$. We investigate an interpretation in terms of composite inelastic dark matter, in which a dimension-ten operator splits the lightest baryon of a confining $SU(3)_{\rm D}$ theory into two Majorana states. Endothermic scattering is mediated by a leptophobic vector coupled to baryon number. For a dark-baryon mass of $1\,\mathrm{TeV}$ and a splitting of $300\,\mathrm{keV}$, a reference nucleon cross section of $6.5\times10^{-43}\,\mathrm{cm^2}$ lies within LZ's published two-sided 90\% confidence interval. This benchmark fixes $M_V/g_B\simeq9.9\,\mathrm{TeV}$, where $M_V$ and $g_B$ are the mediator mass and baryon-number gauge coupling, and is compatible with solar neutrino and dijet constraints.

Abstract: We investigate whether adding $t$, the positive magnitude of the squared nuclear four-momentum transfer, enables boosted decision trees (BDTs) to improve invisible-dark-boson selection relative to optimized rectangular cuts at the Electron-Ion Collider. We model coherent exclusive scalar and vector production at generator level in electron-gold collisions at 18 GeV by 100 GeV per nucleon. Both methods use identical weighted samples, inputs, preselection, and optimization objectives. Using only electron information, the BDT provided no consistent advantage over optimized cuts for signal selection across 11 masses for each boson type. When both methods also use $t$, the BDT distinguishes signal from background slightly better than optimized cuts at 10 GeV for both boson types. These results motivate further investigation of machine learning in EIC dark-boson searches through exclusive processes where $t$ can be reconstructed.

### [48] [Heavy-quark spin precession as a magnetic field chronometer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22492)
*Dushmanta Sahu,Captain R. Singh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes heavy-quark spin precession as a novel probe to quantify the strength and lifetime of magnetic fields in relativistic heavy-ion collisions, using polarized charm and bottom quarks evolving via Larmor precession in the quark–gluon plasma; it predicts measurable, charge-dependent polarization splittings in Λc/Λ̄c and Λb/Λ̄b baryons, with distinct pT and mass dependencies enabling discrimination of magnetic-field lifetime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The lifetime of the ultra-strong magnetic field generated in relativistic heavy-ion collisions is poorly constrained, yet crucial for understanding electromagnetic effects on QGP dynamics and chiral phenomena.

Method: The authors model Larmor precession of initially polarized charm and bottom quarks propagating through an expanding QGP medium under realistic time-dependent magnetic fields; they compute accumulated precession phases before hadronization and project resulting baryon polarization observables (Λc+, Λ̄c−, Λb0, Λ̄b0), including transverse-longitudinal mixing.
