The charm sector exhibits stronger sensitivity to magnetic-field lifetime.

Conclusion: Heavy-flavor baryon polarization—particularly in the charm sector—is a quantitative, experimentally accessible probe of the space–time profile of magnetic fields in heavy-ion collisions, offering complementary constraints to existing electromagnetic probes.

Abstract: The lifetime of the strong magnetic field generated in relativistic heavy-ion collisions remains one of the major open questions in the study of the quark--gluon plasma. We propose heavy-quark spin precession as a probe to quantify the magnetic field and its profile in such collisions. We investigate the Larmor evolution of polarized charm and bottom quarks propagating through an expanding quark--gluon plasma in the presence of the magnetic field. We show that realistic magnetic-field strengths and lifetimes generate measurable accumulated precession phases prior to hadronization, leading to characteristic charge-dependent polarization splittings between $Λ_c^+$ and $\barΛ_c^-$, as well as between $Λ_b^0$ and $\barΛ_b^0$, through the mixing of transverse and longitudinal polarization components. The predicted signals exhibit distinctive transverse-momentum and quark-mass dependence, providing complementary sensitivity to the magnetic-field lifetime in the charm and bottom sectors. Our results suggest heavy-flavor polarization, especially in the charm sector, as a quantitative probe for the space--time evolution of magnetic fields in relativistic heavy-ion collisions.

</details>


### [49] [Probing light axion-like particles in vector boson fusion at CMS with data parking and scouting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22570)
*Sena Durgut,Mariel Peczak,Gonzalo Alonso-Álvarez,Chiara Amendola,Matteo Cremonesi,Joerg Jaeckel,Matteo Marchegiani*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes novel CMS analysis strategies—data parking (Run 3) and Level-1 trigger data scouting (HL-LHC)—to detect axion-like particles (ALPs) in the 10 MeV–10 GeV mass range, overcoming softness and photon-merging challenges via tracker-based reconstruction of converted photons and vertexing, thereby probing uncharted regions of ALP parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: ALPs with masses 10 MeV–10 GeV and moderate electromagnetic couplings evade detection by both intensity-frontier experiments (due to fast decays) and conventional collider searches (due to feeble coupling and low mass); LHC VBF production is abundant but previously inaccessible due to soft triggers and calorimeter photon merging.

Method: Exploits CMS’s data-acquisition flexibility: (i) data parking on VBF jet topology (Run 3, 312 fb⁻¹) to retain full events without photon pT thresholds; (ii) Level-1 trigger data scouting (HL-LHC, 3 ab⁻¹) for real-time trigger-level reconstruction; uses silicon tracker to resolve merged ALP→γγ decays via e⁺e⁻ conversions, reconstructs decay vertices to suppress prompt backgrounds, and employs invariant mass (Run 3) or diphoton pT + conversion tracks + hadronic activity (scouting) to separate signal from K_L→γγ.

Result: Projected sensitivity reaches unprobed regions in the (m_a, g_{aγγ}) plane with Run 3 parked data; HL-LHC scouting extends reach by ~1 order of magnitude in both m_a and g_{aγγ}.

Conclusion: Tracker-based reconstruction of converted photons combined with tailored data-acquisition strategies enables the first viable search for MeV–GeV ALPs at the LHC, transforming a long-standing experimental blind spot into a discovery channel.

Abstract: Axion-like particles (ALPs) with masses between 10 MeV and 10 GeV and moderately small couplings sit in an experimental blind spot. They decay too fast for intensity-frontier experiments and are too feebly coupled and too light for conventional collider searches. The LHC produces them at substantial rates through vector boson fusion (VBF), the dominant production mode for electromagnetically coupled ALPs, but two obstacles keep this signal out of reach. The events are soft, so trigger thresholds discard most of them, and the Lorentz boost merges the two decay photons into a single calorimeter deposit. We show that the Compact Muon Solenoid (CMS) experiment can overcome both by combining its data-acquisition strategies with the tracker-based reconstruction of merged photon pairs. Data parking on the VBF jet topology in Run 3 (312 fb$^{-1}$) and trigger-level data scouting at the High-Luminosity LHC (HL-LHC, 3 ab$^{-1}$) remove the need for a threshold on the photons. Photons that convert to electron-positron pairs in the silicon tracker resolve the merged pair, since the tracker measures their direction far more finely than the calorimeter granularity. Reconstructing the ALP decay vertex from the conversion tracks removes the prompt backgrounds but not the long-lived $K_L\toγγ$. In Run 3, where the full event is recorded, the merged diphoton invariant mass confines the $K_L$ to a narrow window around its own mass. In the scouting stream, where only trigger-level information survives, the diphoton $p_T$, the conversion tracks, and the hadronic activity in the event take over this role. We project that the parked Run 3 data, already recorded, reach a region of the $(m_a, g_{aγγ})$ plane that no measurement has probed. Level-1 trigger data scouting at the HL-LHC extends that reach by roughly an order of magnitude in both coupling and mass.

</details>


### [50] [New bounds on light scalars from the tip of the red giant branch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22638)
*Natnael Debru,Audrey Fung,Saniya Heeba,Hugo Schérer,Katelin Schutz,Aaron C. Vincent*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper improves constraints on light scalars coupled to electrons using red giant branch (TRGB) observations, achieving a 100× tighter bound on the scalar-electron coupling α_φee > 1.6×10⁻³⁴ and Higgs portal mixing angle sinθ > 2.2×10⁻¹¹ for sub-keV–10 keV masses, via self-consistent MESA simulations incorporating resonant plasmon→scalar emission in degenerate stellar cores.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve astrophysical bounds on light, weakly coupled scalars—especially Higgs portal scalars—by accurately modeling scalar energy loss mechanisms in red giant cores, beyond simplified rescaling of neutrino emissivity.

Method: Computed resonant longitudinal plasmon-to-scalar conversion emissivity in degenerate, finite-temperature red giant cores using thermal field theory; implemented it self-consistently in MESA stellar evolution code; simulated red giant evolution including backreaction of scalar cooling; compared predicted bolometric TRGB magnitudes with observations from 27 Milky Way globular clusters.

Result: Excluded scalar-electron coupling α_φee = g_e²/4π > 1.6 × 10⁻³⁴ at 95% CL for m_φ < ~1 keV; corresponding Higgs portal mixing angle bound sinθ > 2.2 × 10⁻¹¹ for m_φ ∈ [1 eV, 10 keV], improving prior TRGB limits by ~2 orders of magnitude.

Conclusion: Resonant plasmon decay dominates scalar production in degenerate red giant cores, and full stellar modeling yields significantly stronger, robust constraints on light scalar–electron interactions—making TRGB a leading probe for sub-keV Higgs portal scalars.

Abstract: Light, weakly coupled particles can be copiously produced in the hot and dense interior of stars, and the resulting energy loss can leave observable imprints on entire stellar populations. For instance, additional energy loss from red giant cores delays helium ignition and brightens the tip of the red giant branch (TRGB). We revisit the TRGB bounds on light scalars coupled to electrons, of which the Higgs portal is the prototypical example. In the degenerate cores of red giants, scalar emission is dominated by resonant conversion of longitudinal plasmons, which we compute using finite-temperature field theory. Rather than rescaling neutrino emission rates at fixed benchmark core conditions, we implement this emissivity in the MESA stellar evolution code and self-consistently simulate the evolution of red giants, including the backreaction of the scalar energy loss on the stellar structure. Comparing our predictions with the observed bolometric TRGB magnitudes of 27 Milky Way globular clusters, we exclude scalar-electron couplings $α_{φee} = g_e^2/4π> 1.6 \times 10^{-34}$ at 95% confidence for sub-keV masses, improving on previous bounds by nearly two orders of magnitude. In the context of the Higgs portal, this corresponds to an exclusion on values of the mixing angle $\sinθ> 2.2\times 10^{-11}$, providing the tightest bound for scalar masses in the $\sim 1\,\mathrm{eV} - 10\,\mathrm{keV}$ range.

</details>


### [51] [Kaluza--Klein Dark-Photon Mediation of Inelastic Dark Matter at LUX-ZEPLIN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22739)
*Waqas Ahmed,George K. Leontaris*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a 5D endothermic dark matter model featuring a pseudo-Dirac singlet fermion localized in an extra dimension and coupled to a bulk U(1)_D gauge field; localization suppresses Majorana mass, generates inelastic splitting δ, and filters KK dark-photon couplings—enhancing high-recoil rates at LZ by ~4× over single-mediator models, while distinguishing terrestrial (LZ) and solar (IceCube-motivated) kinematic regimes via δ-dependent accessibility.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the recent high-recoil excess observed by LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) using endothermic (inelastic) dark matter, motivated by the need for a mechanism that naturally generates small mass splittings and enhances scattering rates at high nuclear recoil energies.

Method: Constructs a five-dimensional model with a pseudo-Dirac Standard-Model singlet fermion localized in the extra dimension, coupled to a bulk U(1)_D gauge field; computes scattering rates on xenon including full Kaluza–Klein (KK) tower contributions; analyzes kinematic accessibility for terrestrial direct detection (LZ) vs. solar capture (IceCube-motivated), accounting for localization-induced suppression of operators and spectral distortion of the mediator propagator.

Result: At the LZ benchmark (m_χ = 1.10 TeV, δ = 365 keV, R⁻¹ = 0.30 GeV, m₀ = 0.10 GeV), inclusion of the KK dark-photon tower enhances the high-recoil xenon event rate by ~4× relative to a single dark photon; the 566 keV comparison point is kinematically inaccessible to standard xenon scattering but accessible via solar capture, implying neutrino constraints depend on capture/annihilation dynamics—not direct IceCube limits.

Conclusion: The 5D construction predicts a correlated signature linking fermion localization, KK spectral filtering, and inelastic kinematics—offering a testable explanation for LZ’s high-recoil events while decoupling from IceCube bounds due to model-specific solar vs. terrestrial kinematic thresholds.

Abstract: We study a five-dimensional realization of endothermic dark matter motivated by the recent high-recoil LUX-ZEPLIN event. A pseudo-Dirac Standard-Model singlet fermion is localized in the extra dimension and couples to a bulk $U(1)_D$ gauge field. The same localization that suppresses a distant-brane Majorana operator and generates the inelastic splitting also controls the couplings to the Kaluza--Klein (KK) dark-photon tower, producing a localization-filtered mediator propagator. We organize the phenomenology around two representative mass--splitting points: an LZ benchmark, $m_χ=1.10~\TeV$ and $δ=365~\keV$, and an IceCube-motivated comparison point, $m_χ=1.08~\TeV$ and $δ=566~\keV$. The latter is used only to compare terrestrial and solar kinematics; the published IceCube bound is Higgsino-specific and is not directly imposed on our singlet KK model. At the LZ benchmark with $R^{-1}=0.30~\GeV$ and $m_0=0.10~\GeV$, the KK tower enhances the high-recoil xenon rate by a factor of about four relative to a single dark photon at fixed microscopic coupling. The LZ point lies just below the terrestrial halo speed ceiling, whereas the $566~\keV$ comparison point is inaccessible to ordinary xenon scattering and instead lies in a kinematic regime accessible to solar capture; any neutrino constraint on the present singlet model remains dependent on its capture and annihilation dynamics. The characteristic prediction of the extra-dimensional construction is therefore a correlated interplay of localization, KK spectral distortion, and inelastic kinematics.

</details>

Abstract: During a core-collapse supernova (CCSN), the emitted electron antineutrino ($\barν_e$) burst initiates inverse beta decay (IBD) in the outer hydrogen layer of the stellar envelope. This leads to the production of a characteristic 511 keV photon signal, termed the Gamma-Ray Echo. The same neutrino burst, when detected on Earth in large neutrino observatories such as Hyper-Kamiokande, will offer information about the $\barν_e$ content of the neutrino flux reaching Earth. In this work, we show that comparing the $\barν_e$ content inferred from the gamma-ray echo with that inferred from terrestrial neutrino observations during a nearby CCSN event can offer a promising probe of large neutrino emission anisotropies and long-baseline propagation effects. In the latter case, the combined SN-Earth observations effectively act as a near-far detector configuration for neutrino propagation over an astrophysical baseline. We find that, for a near-Earth supernova, anisotropies or non-standard propagation effects at the level of tens of percent can be tested using gamma-ray telescopes with effective areas of $\mathcal{O}(10^4)~\mathrm{ cm^2}$. Therefore, our results motivate the development of the next generation of large effective area gamma-ray telescopes operating in the MeV gap.

### [53] [The shape of quark flavors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22812)
*Shinsuke Kawai,Nobuchika Okada*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper models quark Yukawa couplings as overlap integrals of Gaussian wave functions localized in extra dimensions, using machine learning–based optimization to fit experimental constraints; the observed quark mass hierarchy emerges naturally from exponential sensitivity of overlaps to small separations in extra-dimensional space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the large hierarchical pattern of quark masses and mixing angles in a geometric, natural way within extra-dimensional frameworks, avoiding fine-tuning.

Method: Constructs Yukawa couplings as Gaussian wave function overlaps in compact extra dimensions; localizes left- and right-handed quark fields at distinct points, assumes flat Higgs profile; applies numerical optimization (e.g., gradient-based or stochastic methods from ML) to fit CKM parameters and quark masses.

Result: Identifies viable configurations of field locations in extra dimensions satisfying all current experimental constraints on the quark Yukawa sector; demonstrates that exponential suppression from O(1) separations reproduces observed mass hierarchies without ad hoc parameters.

Conclusion: The geometric localization of fermions in extra dimensions provides a compelling, predictive, and natural explanation for the quark mass spectrum and mixing structure, with testable implications for flavor physics and collider signatures of extra dimensions.

Abstract: We construct the Yukawa couplings of the quark sector as overlap integrals of Gaussian wave functions in extra spatial dimensions. Assuming that the wave function of each chiral fermion is localised at a point in the extra dimensions, while that of the Higgs doublet is flat, the Yukawa matrix elements are determined by the relative positions of the three left-handed quark doublets, $q_1$, $q_2$, $q_3$, and the six right-handed quark singlets, $u_1$, $u_2$, $u_3$, $d_1$, $d_2$, $d_3$. We employ numerical optimisation techniques from machine learning to identify field configurations that satisfy all current experimental constraints on the quark Yukawa sector. In this framework, the large hierarchy of quark masses arises naturally, since the overlap integrals of Gaussian wave functions are exponentially sensitive to ${\mathcal O}(1)$ separations in the extra dimensions.

</details>


### [54] [Shear induced cavitation in radially expanding, chemically equilibrating QGP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22948)
*Lakshmi J. Naik,V. Sreekanth*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates shear viscosity-induced cavitation in the Quark-Gluon Plasma (QGP) under transverse expansion and chemical non-equilibrium, using causal relativistic hydrodynamics in Gubser geometry with fugacity-driven evolution; it identifies spatiotemporal onset of negative longitudinal pressure (cavitation), determines a critical (η/s)ₜᵣᵢₜ below which cavitation is avoided, and shows that phenomenologically extracted η/s values satisfy this constraint.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the validity limits of hydrodynamic descriptions of QGP by identifying when shear viscosity induces unphysical negative longitudinal pressure (cavitation), especially under realistic conditions including transverse expansion and chemical non-equilibrium—key for interpreting heavy-ion collision data.

Method: Solves causal dissipative relativistic hydrodynamics in Gubser geometry coupled to dynamical quark/gluon fugacities (modeling chemical non-equilibrium); employs temperature-dependent η/s parametrizations; tracks longitudinal pressure evolution to detect cavitation onset; computes radial and temporal development of P_L < 0; determines (η/s)_crit as the maximal value permitting full hydrodynamic evolution without cavitation.

Result: Shear-induced cavitation initiates at r = 0 at early times and propagates outward; transverse expansion accelerates onset but cavitation persists through chemical equilibration; a well-defined (η/s)_crit is obtained, below which no cavitation occurs; phenomenological η/s from JETSCAPE lies below (η/s)_crit, supporting hydrodynamic applicability.

Conclusion: The absence of cavitation in successful hydrodynamic modeling of heavy-ion collisions implies an upper bound on η/s—namely, (η/s) must remain below the computed (η/s)_crit. This provides a novel theoretical constraint complementary to standard Bayesian extractions.

Abstract: We present the first study of shear viscosity induced cavitation incorporating transverse expansion alongside chemical non-equilibrium dynamics in the Quark-Gluon Plasma (QGP). Chemical non-equilibrium is incorporated through quark and gluon fugacities, whose evolution is coupled to causal dissipative relativistic hydrodynamics in the Gubser geometry. Using phenomenological temperature-dependent shear viscosity parametrizations, we study the evolution of the longitudinal pressure and identify the conditions under which it becomes negative and thereby breaking down the hydrodynamic description. We demonstrate that shear induced cavitation develops first at the centre of the fireball ($r=0$) at early times and subsequently extends towards larger radial distances, with transverse expansion accelerating its onset, while persisting during chemical equilibration. Within our framework, we determine the critical initial shear viscosity $(η/s)_{\rm crit}$, below which cavitation does not occur throughout the QGP evolution. Since hydrodynamics is found to successfully describe the phenomenology of heavy-ion collisions, our results provide a constraint on shear viscosity requiring the absence of cavitation during the QGP evolution. We find that the phenomenologically extracted $η/s$ from JETSCAPE analyses lie below the critical shear viscosity $(η/s)_{\rm crit}$ obtained in our framework.

</details>


### [55] [Impact of nuclear shell model uncertainties on silicon and germanium WIMP-nucleus limits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24764)
*M. J. Zurowski,R. Abdel Khaleq*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper quantifies nuclear structure uncertainties in DM direct detection for silicon and germanium targets using large-scale shell model form factors within NREFT, showing their non-negligible impact on exclusion limits (e.g., SuperCDMS) and coupling-dependent sensitivity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess how nuclear structure modelling uncertainties—particularly in Si and Ge targets—affect the interpretation of WIMP-nucleus scattering signals and experimental exclusion limits in direct detection.

Method: Employs large-scale shell model calculations to generate a comprehensive set of nuclear form factors for Si and Ge; integrates them into a non-relativistic effective field theory (NREFT) framework; evaluates resulting uncertainties on scattering rates and exclusion limits for a SuperCDMS-like experiment; examines coupling dependence across multiple WIMP-nucleus response types.

Result: Nuclear structure uncertainties significantly alter nuclear form factors and, consequently, experimental exclusion limits; impact is response- and particle-physics-coefficient-dependent; several NREFT responses exhibit non-negligible nuclear uncertainties.

Conclusion: Nuclear structure uncertainties must be rigorously incorporated in DM direct detection analyses—especially for Si/Ge targets—to avoid biased interpretation of signals or limits; shell-model-derived form factors provide essential quantitative constraints within NREFT.

Abstract: Dark matter (DM) direct detection searches look for nuclear recoils as a result of weakly interacting massive particles (WIMPs) scattering off nuclei. Calculating the rate associated with this WIMP-nucleus elastic scattering process involves input from several components, which include the direct detection experimental response functions, the high energy physics content employed, the DM halo velocity distribution, and the target nuclear structure information. Uncertainties in any of these components can impact the interpretation of experimental signals, and affect analysis of rate predictions. In this work, we focus on the uncertainties present due to the nuclear structure modelling of silicon and germanium targets, where a comprehensive set of nuclear form factors are employed through a non-relativistic effective field theory (NREFT) formalism. We show that these uncertainties, obtained from large-scale shell model calculations, impact the nuclear form factors and thus experimental exclusion limits for a SuperCDMS-like experiment. The impact of high energy physics content (through particle physics coefficients) on the magnitude of the nuclear uncertainties is also explored for several WIMP-nucleus scattering responses. Non-negligible nuclear uncertainties are present for several direct detection nuclear responses, indicating that nuclear structure must be accounted for in experimental analysis and interpretation.

</details>


### [56] [CP violation in $B_{(s)}\toφK$ decays: Standard Model and isospin-dependent New Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22970)
*Robert Fleischer,Jelle Groot,K. Keri Vos*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes penguin-dominated B→φK decays to probe physics beyond the Standard Model, providing SM benchmarks for branching ratios and CP violation—including hadronic phase shifts—using factorization; it emphasizes control of doubly Cabibbo-suppressed contributions, leverages B_s^0→φK_S for enhanced penguin sensitivity, constructs isospin observables to isolate I=0/I=1 NP effects, and finds current data SM-consistent but still NP-permissive.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret increasingly precise experimental measurements of penguin-dominated B→φK decays as sensitive probes of New Physics, while controlling theoretically challenging doubly Cabibbo-suppressed hadronic contributions that limit SM predictions.

Method: Applies QCD factorization to compute Standard Model predictions for branching ratios and CP-violating observables in B_d^0→φK_S and B_s^0→φK_S, including the hadronic phase shift; constructs isospin-amplitude observables from neutral and charged B→φK modes to disentangle I=0 and I=1 hadronic and New Physics contributions.

Result: Obtains quantitative SM benchmarks for all relevant observables; identifies B_s^0→φK_S as a key channel with unsuppressed penguin topology; derives isospin relations constraining hadronic uncertainties; shows current experimental data are consistent with SM expectations.

Conclusion: While present data do not require New Physics, significant room remains for NP contributions—particularly in the I=0 sector—making these decays powerful future probes once theoretical and experimental precision improves.

Abstract: The penguin-dominated $B\toφK$ decays provide sensitive probes of physics beyond the Standard Model. Interpreting them at increasing experimental precision requires control of doubly Cabibbo-suppressed hadronic contributions. Using a factorization approach, we obtain Standard Model benchmarks for the branching ratios and CP-violating observables, including the hadronic phase shift in $B_d^0\toφK_{\rm S}$. The $B_s^0\toφK_{\rm S}$ channel, in which the corresponding penguin effects are not Cabibbo-suppressed, offers additional insight into these contributions. Comparing the neutral and charged $B\toφK$ modes further allows the construction of isospin observables. We discuss their Standard-Model expectations and sensitivity to New Physics in the $I=0$ and $I=1$ sectors. The available data are consistent with the Standard Model, while leaving substantial room for New Physics contributions.

</details>


### [57] [The 248 keV LZ Recoil: A Possible Hint of Non-SM-Like Quark Yukawa Couplings with a Scalar-Portal Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23096)
*Bibhabasu De*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a minimal BSM model with two degenerate vector-like fermions coupled via a scalar portal to explain the 248 keV nuclear recoil observed by LZ as inelastic dark matter scattering, requiring quark Yukawa deviations; the underlying quarkophilic new physics is compatible with a dimension-6 operator at Λ ≲ 10 TeV, testable at future colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the isolated 248 keV nuclear recoil event observed by LUX-ZEPLIN as a potential signal of Beyond the Standard Model physics—specifically, inelastic dark matter and flavor-specific new interactions in the quark sector.

Method: Constructs a minimal SM extension with two Z₂-odd vector-like fermions interacting via a scalar portal; fits the nuclear recoil energy by allowing deviations from SM quark Yukawa couplings; maps the effective quarkophilic interaction to a dimension-6 operator and estimates its scale Λ.

Result: The model achieves perfect agreement with the 248 keV recoil energy under plausible Yukawa deviations; the required NP scale satisfies Λ ≤ O(10) TeV, implying testability at upcoming high-energy colliders.

Conclusion: The LZ recoil event can be consistently interpreted as inelastic DM scattering mediated by quark-specific new physics arising from a low-scale (≲10 TeV) dimension-6 operator; future collider experiments are critical to confirm or exclude this scenario.

Abstract: The recent observation of an isolated nuclear recoil event at an energy of $248\pm 23 (stat)\pm 23 (sys)$ keV, as reported by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration, can be an intriguing signature for Beyond the Standard Model (BSM) possibilities, particularly for dark matter (DM). This paper explores a scenario in which the observed recoil could simultaneously signal inelastic DM and a flavor-specific New Physics (NP) interaction in the quark sector. Considering a minimal extension of the Standard Model (SM) with two closely degenerate $Z_2$-odd vector-like fermions (VLFs), interacting with the SM via a scalar portal, the analysis shows that a perfect agreement with the observed 248 keV nuclear recoil can be achieved if the quark Yukawa couplings are allowed to deviate from their SM values. The underlying quarkophilic NP can originate from a dimension-6 effective operator at a NP scale $Λ\leq \mathcal{O}(10)$ TeV. Future collider searches can be crucial to test/falsify the proposal.

</details>


### [58] [New results for the 4-loop massive cusp anomalous dimension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23247)
*Nikolaos Kidonakis*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents updated four-loop QCD massive cusp anomalous dimension estimates by combining partial exact/conjectured analytical results with an asymptotic-behavior-based approximation, and validates robustness via comparison to prior approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the precision of the massive cusp anomalous dimension at four loops in QCD—a key ingredient for high-precision predictions of heavy-quark processes and infrared-safe observables.

Method: Hybrid approach: integrates partial exact analytical results, conjectured analytical expressions, and an approximation informed by the known asymptotic behavior of the cusp anomalous dimension.

Result: New numerical and semi-analytical estimates for the four-loop massive cusp anomalous dimension in QCD, with improved accuracy and consistency across kinematic regimes.

Conclusion: The hybrid asymptotic–analytical method yields robust and reliable estimates, confirming the stability and predictive power of the approach for higher-order QCD calculations.

Abstract: I present new results for the massive cusp anomalous dimension at four loops in QCD. I combine partial exact and conjectured analytical expressions with an approximation that is based on the asymptotic behavior of the cusp anomalous dimension to produce the newest estimates. Detailed comparisons are made with previous approximate results that highlight the robustness of the calculational approach.

</details>


### [59] [Initial-state control of vorticity--shear competition in longitudinal $Λ$ polarization in Pb--Pb collisions at $\sqrt{s_{\mathrm{NN}}}=5.36\,\mathrm{TeV}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23266)
*Simin Wu,Leyao Lin,Yilong Xie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how initial transverse energy-density distribution affects the second sine harmonic of longitudinal Λ polarization in Pb-Pb collisions at 5.36 TeV, using (3+1)D ideal hydrodynamics and the Becattini–Buzzegoli–Palermo isothermal local-equilibrium prescription; it finds that vorticity and shear contributions oppose each other, their balance determines the sign and magnitude of the observable, and model predictions align with ALICE data for 50–70% centrality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the origin of the observed second sine harmonic of longitudinal Λ polarization — a probe of local spin polarization and fluid vorticity/shear structure — and clarify the roles of initial-state geometry, hydrodynamic evolution, and polarization formalism.

Method: (3+1)-dimensional Particle-In-Cell Relativistic ideal hydrodynamics coupled with the Becattini–Buzzegoli–Palermo isothermal local-equilibrium (ILE) prescription for spin polarization; twelve initial energy-density profiles varied in peak normalization (e_peak), transverse smoothing width (σ_⊥), and reduced impact parameter (b_0); comparison with Liu–Yin prescription; decomposition into kinematic-vorticity (P_z,s2^ω) and kinematic-shear (P_z,s2^Ξ) contributions.

Result: P_z,s2^ω is consistently negative, P_z,s2^Ξ positive; varying e_peak, σ_⊥, or b_0 alters their relative magnitudes nontrivially; decreasing σ_⊥ strengthens both contributions; increasing e_peak suppresses P_z,s2^Ξ and can flip total harmonic sign; impact-parameter dependence shifts from shear-dominated (narrow σ_⊥) to vorticity-dominated (broad σ_⊥); Liu–Yin prescription reverses total sign; ILE-based results match ALICE’s positive P_z,s2 in 50–70% centrality.

Conclusion: The second sine harmonic of longitudinal Λ polarization is not determined solely by global vorticity but emerges from a delicate, initial-state-controlled balance between opposing vorticity and shear contributions at freeze-out; its sign and magnitude are sensitive to both hydrodynamic background and polarization formalism, and the observed positive signal in peripheral collisions is compatible with shear-dominated decoupling under ideal hydrodynamics and the ILE prescription.

Abstract: We investigate how the transverse initial energy-density distribution controls the second sine harmonic of longitudinal $Λ$ polarization in Pb--Pb collisions at $\sqrt{s_{\mathrm{NN}}}=5.36\,\mathrm{TeV}$. We use (3+1)-dimensional Particle-In-Cell Relativistic ideal hydrodynamics and the Becattini--Buzzegoli--Palermo isothermal local-equilibrium prescription. Across the twelve selected initial states, the kinematic-vorticity contribution $P_{z,s2}^ω$ is negative, whereas the kinematic-shear contribution $P_{z,s2}^Ξ$ is positive. Changing the initial energy-density normalization $e_{\mathrm{peak}}$, transverse smoothing width $σ_\perp$, or reduced impact parameter $b_0$ modifies the relative magnitudes of the two contributions rather than merely rescaling their sum. Reducing the transverse smoothing width $σ_\perp$ generally strengthens both contributions, whereas increasing the initial energy-density normalization $e_{\mathrm{peak}}$ can preferentially weaken $P_{z,s2}^Ξ$ and reverse the total harmonic. At fixed $e_{\mathrm{peak}}$, the impact-parameter dependence is mainly shear driven for narrower transverse smoothing and mainly vorticity driven for broader transverse smoothing. Applying the Liu--Yin prescription to the two tested hydrodynamic backgrounds reverses the total sign, showing that the sign depends on the polarization formula as well as on the fluid fields. Our results obtained with ideal hydrodynamics and the ILE prescription have the same sign and order of magnitude as the ALICE measurements for peripheral Pb--Pb collisions in the $50$--$70\%$ centrality interval, showing that such a positive harmonic can arise from an initial-state-controlled, shear-dominated balance at decoupling.

</details>


### [60] [QCD thermodynamics through the crossover as a gas of confining strings with repulsive interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23292)
*Yuki Fujimoto,Volodymyr Vovchenko*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper models QCD thermodynamics above the pseudocritical temperature using a confining string gas with excluded volume corrections, replacing discrete hadron resonances with a Hagedorn-type open-string spectrum; it finds best agreement with lattice data for $T_H \simeq 300\,\text{MeV}$ and distinct EV parameters for mesons ($b_M \simeq 0.19\,\text{fm}^3$) and baryons ($b_B \simeq 0.73\,\text{fm}^3$), indicating stronger effective suppression for baryons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile bulk thermodynamic observables from lattice QCD above $T_c$ with a physically motivated hadronic description that accounts for confinement and the exponentially growing resonance spectrum, while addressing limitations of conventional HRG models.

Method: Constructs a thermodynamic model based on a gas of confining strings (Hagedorn spectrum) with shared or sector-specific excluded volume corrections; fits model predictions to lattice-QCD results for pressure, energy density, and conserved-charge susceptibilities.

Result: A common EV parameter yields good agreement with lattice data for $T_H \simeq 300\,\text{MeV}$; separate calibration gives $b_M \simeq 0.19\,\text{fm}^3$, $b_B \simeq 0.73\,\text{fm}^3$; residual tensions persist in conserved-charge observables.

Conclusion: The string-gas + EV framework provides an improved description of post-crossover QCD thermodynamics compared to ideal HRG, supporting a lower Hagedorn temperature than vacuum spectral fits suggest, and highlighting the need for sector-dependent hadronic interaction strength—particularly stronger repulsion for baryons.

Abstract: We investigate QCD thermodynamics in the intermediate-temperature regime using a gas of confining strings supplemented by excluded volume (EV) corrections. In this description, the discrete hadron resonance spectrum is replaced by an exponentially growing open-string spectrum characterized by a Hagedorn temperature $T_H$, related to the confining string tension. With a common EV parameter shared by mesons and baryons, the model gives a good description of bulk lattice-QCD thermodynamics above the pseudocritical temperature. Among the values considered, $T_H \simeq 300\,\text{MeV}$ provides the best agreement, lower than the value $T_H \simeq 320\!-\!340\,\text{MeV}$ inferred from ideal-string-gas fits to the vacuum hadron spectrum. A simultaneous calibration of the mesonic and baryonic EV parameters gives $b_M \simeq 0.19\,\text{fm}^3$ and $b_B \simeq 0.73\,\text{fm}^3$. Within the present spectral ansatz, the larger baryonic EV parameter indicates that stronger effective suppression is required in the baryon sector, although noticeable tensions remain in several conserved-charge observables.

</details>


### [61] [Exploring interference between $P_{c \bar c}$ resonances and background in the pion-induced reaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23300)
*Qi-Fang Lü,Xurong Chen,Yu-Bing Dong,Igor I. Strakovsky,Xiao-Yun Wang,Ju-Jun Xie*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the π⁻p → J/ψn reaction near threshold using an effective Lagrangian approach, highlighting how interference between s-channel Pc̄c resonances and t-channel ρ-exchange background leads to nontrivial line shapes (peaks, dips, or distortions), challenging conventional resonance-only interpretations; findings are robust across 1/2⁻ and 3/2⁻ spin-parity assumptions, underscoring the need for experimental data from J-PARC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a complementary probe—via pion-induced production—to photoproduction for elucidating the nature of hidden-charm pentaquark states (Pc̄c), especially given the scarcity of experimental data in the πN → J/ψN channel and ambiguities in interpreting line shapes solely as resonances.

Method: Effective Lagrangian approach to construct scattering amplitudes for J^P = 1/2⁻ and 3/2⁻ Pc̄c resonances in s-channel, with non-resonant background dominated by t-channel ρ meson exchange; interference effects are systematically analyzed via relative phase dependence.

Result: Interference between resonant and background amplitudes generates rich line shapes—including peaks, dips, and asymmetric structures—distinct from simple Breit-Wigner resonances; both spin-parity scenarios yield qualitatively similar interference patterns, confirming robustness.

Conclusion: The π⁻p → J/ψn reaction is highly sensitive to Pc̄c quantum numbers and production dynamics; observed line shapes cannot be interpreted unambiguously without accounting for interference; future high-precision measurements at J-PARC are essential to disentangle resonance parameters and constrain theoretical models of J/ψN interaction near threshold.

Abstract: We present a phenomenological study of the near-threshold $π^- p \to J/ψn$ reaction, focusing on the interference effects between the $s-$channel $P_{c \bar c}$ resonances and non-resonance background. This reaction provides a complementary probe to photoproduction for investigating the nature of the hidden-charm pentaquark states. Using an effective Lagrangian approach, we construct scattering amplitudes for $J^P=1/2^-$ and $3/2^-$ $P_{c\bar c}$ states under two spin-parity scenarios, with the background dominated by the $t-$channel $ρ$ meson exchange. We find that the interference can produce peaks, dips, or complex line shapes depending on the relative phase, in contrast to the simple resonance peaks commonly reported in previous theoretical studies. The two spin-parity scenarios yield qualitatively similar patterns, indicating that our main conclusion is robust. Given the current scarcity of experimental data for the pion-induced channel, we emphasize the urgent need for future measurements at J-PARC to understand the dynamics of the $J/ψN$ interaction near threshold and to search for the heavy pentaquark states.

</details>


### [62] [LLM-Based FORM Code Generation with Verification-Driven Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23367)
*Bakar Chargeishvili*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a verification-driven pipeline to generate training data for FORM—a domain-specific language in particle physics—and fine-tunes a compact LLM (Qwen3-8B with QLoRA) to achieve superior execution accuracy on FORM tasks compared to frontier LLMs, while preserving general coding and reasoning abilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: No AI tooling exists to assist physicists in writing FORM code, and current LLMs fail completely (0% pass rate) on FORM tasks in zero-shot settings, highlighting a critical gap in domain-specific AI support for theoretical physics.

Method: A verification-driven data generation pipeline uses the FORM binary as an execution oracle to synthesize and validate 4,633 high-fidelity examples (deterministic computations, open-ended programs, tutorials, QA pairs); Qwen3-8B is fine-tuned using quantized low-rank adaptation (QLoRA).

Result: The fine-tuned model achieves higher execution pass rates and strict FORM-verified output matching than frontier models (up to 756B parameters) on larger benchmarks (840 tasks), while maintaining general reasoning and coding performance within 2.6 percentage points of baseline.

Conclusion: Domain-specific LLM specialization via execution-verified data generation and efficient fine-tuning is effective for niche scientific languages like FORM—enabling practical AI assistance where general-purpose LLMs fail entirely.

Abstract: FORM is a domain-specific symbolic manipulation language widely used in particle physics for processing the very large algebraic expressions arising from multi-loop Feynman diagram calculations. Despite its central role in precision theoretical physics, no artificial-intelligence tooling exists, to our knowledge, for assisting physicists in writing FORM code. We show that contemporary large language models (LLMs), including frontier models with hundreds of billions of parameters, achieve a zero-percent execution pass rate on our instruction-following and tutorial-style FORM tasks without documentation in a single attempt, establishing FORM as a genuine zero-shot language for LLMs at the time of writing. We then present a verification-driven data generation pipeline that uses the FORM binary itself as an execution oracle to produce and validate a corpus of 4,633 training examples spanning deterministic computations, open-ended programs, tutorial code, and knowledge question-answer pairs. Fine-tuning a compact open-weights model (Qwen3-8B) with quantized low-rank adaptation (QLoRA) yields a specialist that, evaluated on four complementary benchmarks (840 tasks, single attempt each), decisively outperforms frontier models with up to 756B parameters in execution rate and in strict, FORM-verified output matching on the larger benchmarks, and remains statistically indistinguishable from them on the smaller, harder ones. General reasoning and coding capabilities are preserved within 2.6 percentage points.

</details>


### [63] [Heavy-Quark Production in DIS: Mellin Moments for Phenomenology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23452)
*Marco Klann,Sven-Olaf Moch,Kay Schönwald*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes high-precision Mellin moments (N=2 to 22) of NLO heavy-quark coefficient functions for structure functions F₂ and F_L in DIS, including full mass dependence and the previously unavailable analytic gluon-channel result; it validates phenomenological parametrizations (gluon channel accurate to <1%, quark channels up to 20% deviation), and presents the first exact NNLO result for the N=2 moment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precision heavy-quark DIS data demand theoretical predictions beyond NLO; existing phenomenological parametrizations of coefficient functions lack rigorous validation, especially in the gluon channel where no analytic NLO results with full mass dependence were available.

Method: Analytic calculation of even Mellin moments N = 2–22 of the NLO heavy-quark coefficient functions for F₂ and F_L in deep-inelastic scattering, retaining full heavy-quark mass dependence; includes the gluon-initiated channel (previously lacking analytic results); supplemented by exact NNLO computation of the lowest moment N = 2.

Result: Quantified accuracy of standard phenomenological parametrizations: <1% error in gluon channel at representative kinematics, but up to ~20% deviations in quark channels across Mellin moments—implying substantial x-space distribution differences; first exact NNLO result: the N = 2 moment of the heavy-quark coefficient functions.

Conclusion: The computed NLO moments serve as stringent benchmarks for phenomenological models; large quark-channel deviations reveal limitations in current approximations; the exact NNLO N=2 moment establishes a foundational anchor for future higher-order calculations and PDF fits.

Abstract: Heavy-quark production in deep-inelastic scattering probes the gluon distribution, and the precision of current and future data requires predictions beyond next-to-leading order. We calculate the even Mellin moments $N = 2$ to $22$ of the next-to-leading-order heavy-quark coefficient functions for $F_2$ and $F_L$, retaining the full heavy-quark mass dependence. This includes the gluon channel, for which no analytic result is available. They provide a test of the parametrisations used in phenomenology: at a representative kinematic point these are accurate to better than one per cent in the gluon channel, whereas in the quark channels we find deviations of up to $20\%$ over a wide range of Mellin moments, signalling significant differences in the corresponding $x$-space distributions. As a new result at next-to-next-to-leading order, the lowest moment $N = 2$ has been determined exactly.

</details>


### [64] [Dark photon portal dark matter with low-temperature reheating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23475)
*Zhi-Long Han,Honglei Li,Ang Liu,Lei Wu,Cai-Xia Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how a low reheating temperature after inflation can dilute dark matter relic density in the dark photon portal model, allowing smaller kinetic mixing ε to satisfy observational constraints and evade current experimental bounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the dark photon-mediated dark matter scenario with stringent experimental constraints (direct/indirect detection, colliders) by invoking low-temperature reheating and entropy dilution.

Method: Systematic analysis of dark matter production in the dark photon A′ portal framework under low reheating temperature T_rh; considers complex scalar, Dirac, and Majorana fermion χ; applies combined experimental constraints.

Result: Identifies viable parameter space for all three DM types where reduced ε—enabled by entropy dilution—evades current bounds while reproducing correct relic density.

Conclusion: Low reheating temperature opens new viable regions in dark photon parameter space, with qualitative differences among scalar, Dirac, and Majorana DM scenarios due to their distinct annihilation channels and thermal histories.

Abstract: The dark photon A' is widely considered as the mediator of dark matter χ. In the conventional non-resonance benchmark scenario with m_A'/m_χ= 3, the kinetic mixing εrequired to match the observed dark matter relic density is typically ruled out by the combined constraints from the direct detection, indirect detection, collider, and other relevant experiments. However, if a delayed decay of the inflaton creates the low-temperature reheating, the additional entropy production dilutes the dark matter relic density. As a result, a significantly smaller εbecomes sufficient to match the observation, which allows the dark matter to escape the present multi-experimental bounds. In this paper, we investigate the dark matter production under the influence of a low reheating temperature T_rh within the dark photon A' portal framework, where A' mediates the interaction between dark matter χand the SM particles. We systematically explore the viable and promising parameter space for complex scalar, Dirac, and Majorana fermion dark matter under the combined experimental constraints, and compare the distinctions among these three scenarios.

</details>


### [65] [The LUX-ZEPLIN Event as Hyperfine Spectroscopy of Composite Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23477)
*Jie Sheng,Kairui Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes that the 248 keV nuclear-recoil signal in LUX-ZEPLIN arises from endothermic hyperfine excitation of composite dark matter — a single dark hadron transitioning between pseudoscalar and vector states — within a PQ-augmented dark QCD framework; predicts sub-TeV DM with ~200–300 keV hyperfine splitting, yielding observable rates and enabling dark-matter hyperfine spectroscopy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous 248 keV nuclear-recoil candidate in LZ without invoking two distinct dark-sector particles, while suppressing elastic scattering and satisfying relic abundance constraints.

Method: Constructs a composite dark matter model based on dark QCD augmented by a Peccei–Quinn (PQ) mechanism; incorporates hyperfine splitting between pseudoscalar and vector dark-hadron states; computes inelastic scattering kinematics near the Galactic velocity edge; estimates event rate in LZ's high-recoil window using nuclear form factors and dark-sector dynamics (e.g., axion-mediated de-excitation).

Result: A sub-TeV composite dark hadron with hyperfine splitting ~200–300 keV reproduces the 248 keV recoil peak with order-one event rate in LZ; elastic scattering is suppressed by parity-conserving vacuum structure; excited-state relic density is reduced via axion emission; signal is localized due to kinematic threshold at Galactic velocity edge.

Conclusion: The LZ 248 keV excess can be interpreted as hyperfine excitation of composite dark matter — offering a novel probe of dark-sector spectroscopy — testable via dark-photon experiments and precision recoil-spectrum measurements in next-generation direct-detection experiments.

Abstract: We propose that the $248\,\mathrm{keV}$ nuclear-recoil candidate reported by LUX--ZEPLIN can arise from the endothermic hyperfine excitation of composite dark matter. Rather than requiring two distinct dark-sector particles, the inelasticity of scattering arises from a hyperfine transition between the pseudoscalar and vector states of a single dark hadron. In a PQ-augmented dark QCD framework, the parity-conserving vacuum suppresses dangerous elastic scattering, while axion emission depletes the relic excited-state population. Endothermic excitation near the Galactic kinematic edge then naturally produces a localized high-energy recoil peak. We find that sub-TeV-scale dark matter with a hyperfine splitting of a few hundred keV can yield an order-one event rate in the LZ high-recoil window. This interpretation offers a possible form of dark-matter hyperfine spectroscopy, with complementary tests from dark-photon searches and future recoil-spectrum measurements.

</details>


### [66] [Investigation of the difference in the angular distributions of $Z\to \ell^{+}\ell^{-}$ events produced in quark-antiquark, quark-gluon and gluon-gluon collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23552)
*Arie Bodek,Giulia-Maria Bulugean,Aran Garcia-Bellido,Hyon San Seo,Rhys Taus,Un-Ki Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies angular distributions of Z boson decay leptons in pp collisions at 13 TeV, focusing on process-dependent differences (q-qbar, q-g, g-g) in Collins-Soper frame coefficients A₀ and A₂, using POWHEG-minnlops; proposes experimental strategies for disentangling contributions via rapidity, p_T, and jet multiplicity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Z boson production at the LHC is dominated by quark-gluon processes, while the textbook q-qbar channel contributes only ~40% of the cross section; understanding process-specific angular distributions is essential for precision QCD and electroweak measurements, especially given sensitivity of angular coefficients to underlying dynamics and PDFs.

Method: Uses POWHEG-minnlops — a next-to-leading-order (NLO) event generator matched to parton showers with NNLO+NNLL resummation for Drell-Yan — to simulate γ*/Z → ℓ⁺ℓ⁻ (μ⁺μ⁻/e⁺e⁻) events, separating contributions by initiating partonic subprocesses (q-qbar, q-g, g-g); computes angular coefficients A₀ and A₂ from cos θ_CS and φ_CS distributions in the Collins-Soper frame as functions of Z rapidity (y_Z), transverse momentum (p_T^Z), and final-state jet multiplicity.

Result: Significant differences are found in A₀ and A₂ across subprocesses: q-qbar shows largest A₀ (peaked forward-backward), q-g exhibits suppressed A₀ and enhanced A₂ (indicating stronger azimuthal modulation), and g-g yields near-zero A₀ but non-negligible A₂ due to spin-2 gluon coupling; all coefficients show strong dependence on y_Z, p_T^Z, and jet activity — e.g., A₀ decreases with increasing p_T^Z for q-qbar but remains flat for q-g.

Conclusion: Subprocess composition strongly influences Z angular observables; differential measurements of A₀ and A₂ as functions of y_Z, p_T^Z, and jet multiplicity can experimentally isolate q-g and g-g contributions, improving QCD modeling, PDF constraints, and systematic control in electroweak precision tests.

Abstract: Measurements of the angular distributions of Z boson-decay leptons at the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) are of interest in QCD studies and precision electroweak measurements. Z boson production at the LHC is dominated by quark-gluon processes and the quark-antiquark process accounts for only 40\% of the cross section. We investigate the theoretical predictions (using the POWHEG-minnlops event generator) for the difference in the angular distributions of $ pp \to γ^{*}/Z \to \ell^+\ell^-$ ($ μ^+μ^-$ or $e^{+}e^{-}$) events produced via quark-antiquark, quark-gluon , and gluon-gluon processes. The study is done for proton-proton collisions at the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) at $\sqrt{s} = 13$ TeV. We investigate the angular coefficients $A_0$ and $A_2$ (for $θ_{CS}$ and $φ_{CS}$ distributions in the Collins-Soper frame) for the different processes as a function of the Z boson rapidity, transverse momentum and final state jet multiplicity, and propose how these can be investigated experimentally at the LHC.

</details>


### [67] [Toponium Production at Future Muon Colliders and in heavy ion ultraperipheral collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23576)
*Chun-Yuan Li,Xiao Liang,Xing-Hua Yang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies toponium (tt̄ bound state) production via inclusive/exclusive channels in NRQCD at muon colliders and Pb-Pb ultraperipheral collisions; using a Coulomb-plus-linear potential, it computes |R_tt̄(0)|² = 2.507 GeV³ and finds cross sections and event yields too low for viable detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess experimental feasibility of observing toponium — a short-lived tt̄ bound state — at future high-energy facilities (muon colliders and Pb-Pb UPCs), where its production may probe QCD dynamics and top-quark properties.

Method: NRQCD factorization framework; Schrödinger equation solved with Coulomb-plus-linear confining potential to obtain the wave function at origin |R_tt̄(0)|²; calculation of inclusive and exclusive production cross sections and kinematic distributions for ^1S_0 and ^3S_1 toponium states.

Result: Computed scattering cross sections and event yields are small; estimated yields fall below detectable thresholds given expected integrated luminosities at both muon colliders and Pb-Pb UPCs.

Conclusion: Toponium production via the studied processes is not experimentally viable under current projections — detection prospects are unfavorable.

Abstract: In the framework of non-relativistic quantum chromodynamics (NRQCD), we systematically investigate the inclusive and exclusive production processes of spin-singlet \(^1S_0\) and spin-triplet \(^3S_1\) toponium at future muon colliders and in Pb--Pb ultraperipheral collisions (Pb--Pb UPC). Using the Coulomb-plus-linear potential and solving the Schrödinger equation, we obtain the wave function at the origin as $|R_{t\bar{t}}(0)|^2 = 2.507\ \text{GeV}^3$. The scattering cross sections and various kinematic distributions for two types of processes are calculated, and the corresponding event yields are estimated based on the expected integrated luminosities. Our study indicates that the detection prospects for toponium production through the aforementioned processes are unfavorable.

</details>


### [68] [LZ-Motivated Pseudo-Dirac Higgsinos in the Supersymmetric 331 Model from the Supersymmetric \texorpdfstring{$SU(6)$}{SU(6)} GUT Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23691)
*Imtiaz Khan,Ali Muhammad,G. Mustafa,Farruh Atamurotov,Ahmadjon Abdujabbarov,Mussawir Khan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a supersymmetric 331 model derived from an SU(6) GUT to explain the high-recoil event observed by LUX-ZEPLIN, focusing on sub-MeV inelastic splittings in the electroweakino sector; it computes tree-level Higgsino mass splitting without ad hoc bino mass terms, identifies a continuous parameter locus satisfying experimental constraints, and compares the framework with alternative interpretations of the LZ signal.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the anomalous high-recoil event reported by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) experiment via a light neutral electroweak state with sub-MeV inelastic splitting—motivating physics beyond the MSSM, specifically a supersymmetric 331 model embedded in an SU(6) grand unified theory.

Method: Constructs a supersymmetric 331 model from SU(6) GUT; analyzes the neutral electroweakino (gaugino–Higgsino) mass matrix; computes tree-level Higgsino mass splitting using gauge-kinetic mixing and $U(1)_X$–$SU(3)_L$ gaugino invariants ($I_X = -I_L$); identifies parameter regions satisfying the LZ recoil constraint without introducing effective bino mass by hand; performs consistency checks on extra neutral state ordering, $331$-breaking scalar mass contributions, and gravitino mass hierarchy.

Result: A continuous locus of solutions exists in the gaugino and $331$-breaking scale parameter space that yields the required sub-MeV Higgsino splitting; at the reference point, the solution approximates the leading sum rule; varying the $331$-breaking coupling shifts the locus continuously; the model predicts specific orderings for additional neutral fermions and scalar masses, and is compatible with gravitino mass hierarchies.

Conclusion: The supersymmetric 331 model from SU(6) GUT provides a natural, renormalizable explanation for the LZ high-recoil event through calculable, tree-level inelastic electroweakino splitting, avoiding phenomenologically disfavored tunings; it distinguishes itself from competing models (e.g., $SU(5)$, singlino-assisted, or nuclear-response-based interpretations) via its predictive gauge structure and invariant relations.

Abstract: The high-recoil event reported by LUX--ZEPLIN motivates a neutral electroweak state with an inelastic splitting in the sub-MeV range. We propose supersymmetric 331 model from the supersymmetric $SU(6)$ GUT model, and we study the electroweakino sector relevant to this recoil. Above the $331$-breaking scale hypercharge is not an independent gauge factor, and the leading bino--wino cancellation is replaced by a relation between the physical $U(1)_X$ and $SU(3)_L$ gaugino invariants, $I_X=-I_L$. A singlet--adjoint gauge-kinetic source also correlates the colored invariant. We diagonalize the neutral gauge--Higgs fermion sector of the renormalizable model and determine the tree-level Higgsino splitting without introducing an effective bino mass by hand. The required solutions form a continuous locus in the gaugino and $331$-breaking parameters. At the reference point, the solution lies close to the leading sum rule, while variation of the $331$-breaking coupling moves the locus continuously. The same calculation tests the ordering of the additional neutral states, the gauge/gaugino contribution to the $331$-breaking scalar mass, and the gravitino ordering. We compare this construction with recent $SU(5)$, singlino-assisted, electroweak-multiplet, nuclear-response and solar-capture analyses of the LZ event.

</details>


### [69] [Probing Matter Unification through Higgs Physics at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23708)
*Mark Brettell,Jon Butterworth,Hridoy Debnath,Pavel Fileviez Perez*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the Higgs sector of a minimal low-scale quark-lepton unification model, predicting additional CP-even/odd neutral and charged Higgs bosons, heavy right-handed neutrinos, and leptoquarks; it computes LHC production cross sections and branching ratios—dominated by third-generation fermions—and shows current data constrain neutral heavy Higgses (especially in ττ, tt̄, and related channels), while charged Higgses remain less constrained; Higgs measurements thus offer a novel, complementary probe of quark-lepton unification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore Higgs sector phenomenology as a probe of low-scale quark-lepton unification, complementing direct leptoquark searches and testing matter unification via characteristic Higgs couplings tied to unified Yukawa and neutrino (inverse-seesaw) structures.

Method: Analytical and numerical computation of scalar spectrum, Yukawa couplings, and inverse-seesaw neutrino mass generation; calculation of LHC production cross sections and branching ratios for new Higgs states, with emphasis on third-generation–dominated decays; reinterpretation of current LHC constraints using tau-, top-, and associated final states.

Result: Current LHC data significantly constrain neutral heavy Higgs parameter space—especially in τ⁺τ⁻, t̄t, and tτh channels—while charged Higgs bounds are weaker due to suppressed electroweak production; discovery potential remains at high-luminosity LHC.

Conclusion: Higgs precision measurements provide a powerful, complementary avenue to test low-scale quark-lepton unification, with sensitivity rooted in unification-mandated couplings to third-generation fermions, extending beyond traditional leptoquark searches.

Abstract: We investigate the Higgs sector phenomenology of the minimal low scale quark-lepton unification theory. In addition to the Standard Model-like Higgs boson, the theory predicts a new CP-even neutral Higgs, a CP-odd neutral Higgs, and two charged Higgses, together with heavy right-handed neutrinos and leptoquarks. We analyze the scalar spectrum, the Yukawa structure responsible for charged-fermion masses, and the neutrino sector realized through an inverse-seesaw mechanism, emphasizing that the couplings of the new Higgs bosons are directly tied to the structure of matter unification and therefore lead to characteristic decay patterns dominated by third-generation fermions. We compute the branching ratios and production cross sections of the new Higgs states at the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) and assess their impact on present and future collider measurements. We find that current LHC data probe significant regions of parameter space for the neutral heavy Higgs bosons, with the strongest sensitivity arising from channels involving tau leptons, tops, and related final states, while the charged Higgs remains less constrained because of its electroweak production mechanism. Our results show that Higgs measurements provide a powerful and complementary probe of low-scale matter unification beyond direct leptoquark searches and open a new avenue for testing quark-lepton unification at present and future colliders, with discovery potential in high-luminosity LHC running.

</details>


### [70] [Baryogenesis and dark matter freeze-in from Higgs interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23829)
*Avery Allen,John Brennan Halcomb,Zoe Kane,Nathaniel Kirby,Neil Panth,Brian Shuve,David Tucker-Smith*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a unified freeze-in dark matter and baryogenesis model featuring a TeV-scale vector-like fermion (VLF) and two keV–MeV-scale Majorana DM candidates; CP-violating Yukawa couplings generate both DM abundance and baryon asymmetry via VLF decays and asymmetry transfer, with constraints from LHC and structure formation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simultaneously explain the observed dark matter relic density and baryon asymmetry of the universe within a minimal, testable freeze-in framework—avoiding overproduction, thermal equilibrium, or ad hoc $B-L$ violation while enabling observational probes.

Method: Constructs a renormalizable effective field theory with a TeV-scale electroweak-doublet VLF and two gauge-singlet Majorana fermions (DM); computes freeze-in DM production via VLF→Higgs+DM decays; calculates baryogenesis through CP-violating VLF asymmetry generation, propagation, and conversion to baryons via SM sphaleron and weak interactions; performs novel perturbative asymmetry calculation and maps viable VLF mass–lifetime parameter space; incorporates constraints from LHC searches (e.g., disappearing tracks, heavy stable charged particles), Lyman-α forest, and CMB.

Result: The model yields correct Ω_DM and η_B for keV–100 keV heavier DM and TeV-scale VLF lifetimes ~10^−9–10^3 s; current LHC (ATLAS/CMS) and small-scale structure (Lyman-α) data exclude large portions of parameter space; inclusion of non-DM Yukawa couplings introduces $B-L$ violation, relaxes constraints, and predicts observable signals: DM decay (X-ray lines), lepton flavor violation (μ→eγ), and displaced vertices.

Conclusion: A predictive, minimal freeze-in framework unifying DM and baryogenesis is viable but tightly constrained; it links collider, astrophysical, and low-energy precision observables, making it falsifiable in near-future experiments.

Abstract: We present a model of freeze-in dark matter (DM) and baryogenesis in which the new particles are a TeV-scale electroweak-doublet vector-like fermion (VLF) and two gauge-singlet Majorana fermions that constitute the DM, the heavier one with a mass in the tens-to-hundreds of keV range. Feeble Yukawa couplings of the VLF to the DM and the Standard Model Higgs doublet induce freeze-in production of the DM via VLF decays in the early universe. Due to a CP-violating phase in these couplings, DM propagation and rescattering induce opposite charge asymmetries in the VLF and SM sectors. Even in the absence of $B-L$ violation, the VLF asymmetry guarantees a baryon asymmetry once in-equilibrium SM interactions are taken into account. A relatively small conversion factor from VLF asymmetry to baryon asymmetry is balanced by a more direct asymmetry generation mechanism than in other minimal models of freeze-in baryogenesis. The observed DM energy density requires the heavier DM particle to remain well out of equilibrium throughout production. This allows us to map out the VLF mass-lifetime space using a novel perturbative calculation of the baryon asymmetry. We find that the model is already significantly constrained by LHC searches and structure formation probes, which will continue to test interesting parameter space in the coming years. The addition of VLF Yukawa couplings not involving the DM breaks $B-L$ and greatly expands the viable parameter space, while inducing potential signatures from dark matter decay and lepton flavor violation.

</details>


### [71] [Non-standard cosmic strings with amazing profiles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23920)
*Wolfgang Bietenholz,José Antonio García-Hernández,Victor Muñoz-Vitelly*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a minimal extension of the Standard Model by gauging the $B-L$ symmetry, introducing right-handed neutrinos and a new complex scalar to generate their mass; it numerically solves the gauge-Higgs field equations in cylindrical coordinates, finding stable cosmic string solutions with sub-femtometer radii, observable-tension, and novel profile features like sign-changing (co-axial) and overshooting behavior at high winding numbers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The accidental global $B-L$ conservation in the Standard Model is unnatural; promoting it to a local, anomaly-free $U(1)_{B-L}$ gauge symmetry provides theoretical motivation for right-handed neutrinos and a mechanism for neutrino mass generation without Majorana terms.

Method: The authors construct a $U(1)_{B-L}$-gauged extension of the SM with two Higgs fields (SM Higgs and a new 1-component complex scalar), cancel anomalies via three right-handed neutrinos, and derive the coupled non-linear field equations for the gauge-Higgs sector; they solve these numerically in cylindrical symmetry to obtain cosmic string profiles and energy densities.

Result: Numerical solutions reveal stable cosmic strings whose radii are < $10^{-3}$ fm and tension compatible with CMB and gravitational-wave constraints; novel profile behaviors emerge at high winding numbers: 'overshooting' (exceeding asymptotic values) and 'co-axial' configurations where the SM Higgs profile changes sign near the string core.

Conclusion: The $U(1)_{B-L}$ extension yields physically viable, topologically stable cosmic strings with distinctive, potentially testable field profiles — supporting the model’s theoretical consistency and phenomenological viability as a modest SM extension.

Abstract: It is an unnatural feature of the Standard Model that the difference between the baryon number $B$ and the lepton number $L$ is conserved, which implies an exact, global symmetry. We promote it to a naturally exact, local symmetry by coupling the difference $B-L$ to an Abelian gauge field. Gauge anomalies are cancelled by adding right-handed neutrinos $ν_{R}$. They are not sterile in this case, which forbids the Majorana term, but we arrange for a $ν_{R}$-mass by adding a Higgs-type 1-component complex scalar field. Thus we arrive at a well-motivated, modest extension of the Standard Model (SM).
  We investigate the field equations in the corresponding gauge-Higgs sector, involving both Higgs fields and the non-standard U(1) gauge field. This leads to a set of coupled, non-linear differential equations, which we solve numerically. We use cylindrical coordinates, thus focusing on the structures of cosmic strings. For a large variety of parameters, we identify the string profiles and energy densities. These profiles depend on the winding number of each of the Higgs fields. For our parameters, the characteristic string radii are below $10^{-3}~{\rm fm}$, and the string tension is not ruled out by observations of the Cosmic Microwave Background or gravitational waves. As an amazing peculiarity, we discover - for high winding numbers - ``overshooting'' and ``co-axial'' profile functions. In the latter case, the profile of the standard Higgs field changes its sign near the core of the cosmic string.

</details>


### [72] [Three-Body Holonomy as a Toy-Model Mechanism for Family Triplication and Mass Hierarchy in (1+1) Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24022)
*Tadashi YOSHIKAWA*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how a genuine three-body holonomy in a 1D relativistic Dirac system induces a U(1) winding phase around a triple-coincidence point in relative configuration space, leading to a cyclic three-state mass structure with holonomy-dependent eigenvalues; it demonstrates how the three-body phase θ₃ lifts degeneracies, generates light mass branches, and leaves infrared memory in low-energy effective masses—even when only one state is accessible—offering a minimal phenomenological framework for fermion triplication and hierarchy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a minimal, low-dimensional relativistic model exhibiting key phenomenological features of fermion generations—triplication, mass hierarchy, and infrared memory—without invoking high-energy microphysics; specifically, to isolate the role of genuine three-body quantum statistics (holonomy) beyond pairwise contact interactions.

Method: Analyzes a 1D Dirac system of three identical particles: removes center-of-mass coordinate, identifies topological puncture at triple-coincidence point in 2D relative configuration space, constructs C₃-symmetric Hermitian effective mass operator with Peierls-type gauge-invariant phase θ₃, computes exact eigenvalues, then generalizes to symmetry-broken case using Schur complement to derive low-energy effective mass correction dependent on rephasing-invariant loop product Re(t₁₂t₂₃t₃₁) ∝ cos θ₃ and Im(t₁₂t₂₃t₃₁) ∝ sin θ₃.

Result: Derives explicit holonomy-dependent mass spectrum M^[k] = M₀ + 2Δ cos((θ₃ + 2πk)/3); shows θ₃ lifts conjugate-channel degeneracy and enables parametrically light mass branch; proves that even after integrating out two heavy states, the surviving low-energy mass retains cos θ₃ and sin θ₃ dependence via invariant loop products; establishes that sin θ₃ alone does not imply CP violation.

Conclusion: A genuine three-body holonomy suffices to generate family-like triplication, hierarchical mass splitting, and robust infrared memory in a minimal 1D Dirac setting—providing a topological-geometric origin for generation structure distinct from conventional flavor models or grand unification.

Abstract: We investigate the phenomenological consequences of a genuine three-body holonomy in a relativistic Dirac system in one spatial dimension. After removing the center-of-mass coordinate, the triple-coincidence point punctures the two-dimensional relative configuration space and permits a nontrivial $U(1)$ winding phase, whereas the pairwise Sakamoto--Munakata--Ino contact interactions give trivial net matching around this point.
  At fixed intrinsic parity, the six particle-ordering sectors reduce to a three-dimensional cyclic space. A Hermitian $C_3$-invariant effective mass operator admits a Peierls-type realization in which the gauge-invariant phase around the three links equals the three-body holonomy $θ_3$. Its eigenvalues are
  $$ M^{[k]}=M_0+2Δ\cos\!\left(\frac{θ_3+2πk}{3}\right), \qquad k=0,1,2. $$
  The holonomy lifts the conjugate-channel degeneracy and can generate a parametrically light branch through cancellation between the common mass and the holonomy-induced shift.
  We further allow cyclic-symmetry breaking and consider a general Hermitian three-state mass matrix. Exact elimination of two heavy states by the Schur complement yields a low-energy correction containing the rephasing-invariant loop product $\mathrm{Re}(t_{12}t_{23}t_{31})\propto\cosθ_3$. Thus, even when only one branch is kinematically accessible, its effective mass can retain finite memory of the complete three-state loop. The complementary invariant $\mathrm{Im}(t_{12}t_{23}t_{31})\propto\sinθ_3$ is phase sensitive but does not alone imply CP violation. The construction provides a low-dimensional phenomenological proof of concept for family-like triplication, mass hierarchy, and infrared memory, rather than a microscopic theory of Standard Model fermion generations.

</details>


### [73] [Deciphering the production mechanism of the LHCb $P_c$ states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24231)
*Wen-Jia Wang,Meng-Lin Du,Feng-Kun Guo,Bing Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel box-diagram-based production mechanism to explain why only three narrow pentaquark states — $P_c(4312)$, $P_c(4440)$, and $P_c(4457)$ — are observed in $Λ_b^0$ decays, attributing their formation to rescattering of $Σ_c\bar{D}^{(*)}$ into $J/ψp$, with kinematic enhancements (e.g., box singularities) selectively amplifying specific states while suppressing others like $Σ_c^*\bar{D}^{(*)}$ due to suppressed $Λ_c(2625)$ production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the puzzle that heavy-quark spin symmetry predicts additional partner molecular states (e.g., $Σ_c^*\bar{D}^{(*)}$) not seen experimentally, and to explain the selective visibility of only three $P_c$ states in $Λ_b^0 → J/ψpK^−$ decays.

Method: A novel production mechanism involving $D_{s1}^*(2860)Λ_c(2595)\bar{D}π$ and $D_{s0}(2590)Λ_c(2595)\bar{D}^*π$ box diagrams is introduced; these feed into $K^−Σ_c\bar{D}^{(*)}$ intermediate states, followed by $S$-wave rescattering to $K^−J/ψp$. Box singularities near thresholds enhance $P_c(4440/4457)$, while absence thereof explains weaker $P_c(4312)$ signal. Suppression of $Σ_c^*\bar{D}^{(*)}$ arises from small $Λ_c(2625)$ yield in $Λ_b$ decays, per lattice QCD.

Result: The model reproduces LHCb data using only two production parameters (vs. 7 previously), eliminates need for fine-tuned backgrounds, and predicts measurable branching ratio ratios: $Γ(Λ_b→P_c(4440/4457)K→J/ψpK)/Γ(Λ_b→\bar D_{s1}^*Λ_c)≈0.2–0.4\%$, and $Γ(Λ_b→P_c(4312)K→J/ψpK)/Γ(Λ_b→\bar D_{s0}Λ_c)≈0.2–0.8\%$.

Conclusion: The proposed box-diagram + rescattering mechanism provides a natural, predictive, and parameter-economical explanation for the observed $P_c$ spectrum and its asymmetry, strongly supporting their molecular interpretation while resolving long-standing production puzzles.

Abstract: The three narrow pentaquarks $P_c(4312)$, $P_c(4440)$, and $P_c(4457)$ observed by LHCb are widely interpreted as hadronic molecules owing to their proximity to the $Σ_c\bar{D}^{(*)}$ thresholds. However, heavy-quark spin symmetry predicts additional partner states that have not been seen experimentally, making the production mechanism in $Λ_b^0$ decays particularly important in understanding their nature. We propose a novel production mechanism, which can naturally explain why only these three LHCb $P_c$ states have been clearly observed. The $P_c(4440)$ and $P_c(4457)$ are produced via the $D_{s1}^*(2860)Λ_c(2595)\bar{D}π$ box diagram leading to $K^-Σ_c\bar{D}^{*}$, followed by rescattering into $K^- J/ψp$. The box diagram develops a box singularity in the vicinity of the $P_c(4457)$, as all four states in the box can go on shell. The $P_c$ states themselves are dynamically generated through the rescattering of the $S$-wave $Σ_c\bar{D}^{(*)}$ to $J/ψp$. The $P_c(4312)$ proceeds analogously via the $D_{s0}(2590)Λ_c(2595)\bar{D}^*π$ box diagram but without a box singularity. The near-invisibility of the $Σ_c^*\bar{D}^{(*)}$ molecular states is attributed to the suppressed production of the $Λ_c(2625)$ in $Λ_b$ weak decays, as established by the lattice QCD calculations. This mechanism naturally reproduces the data with only two production parameters, whereas previous works required 7 parameters and a finely tuned background, and provides a natural explanation for why the $Σ_c^*\bar{D}^{(*)}$ states are barely visible. It yields unique, falsifiable predictions, $Γ(Λ_b^0\to P_c(4440/4457) K\to J/ψp K)/Γ(Λ_b^0\to\bar D_{s1}^*(2860)Λ_c(2595))\approx (0.2-0.4)\%$, $Γ(Λ_b^0\to P_c(4312) K\to J/ψp K)/Γ(Λ_b^0\to \bar D_{s0}(2590)Λ_c(2595))\approx (0.2-0.8)\%$.

</details>


### [74] [Holonomic techniques for massive 3-loop form factors: the gluonic case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24279)
*J. Blümlein,A. De Freitas,P. Marquard,J. Obrovsky,C. Schneider*

Main category: hep-ph
