TL;DR: This paper computes massive three-loop form factors for various currents (vector, axial-vector, scalar, pseudoscalar) in the gluonic sector using advanced symbolic methods, yielding exact analytic expansions around s=0 and s=±∞ in terms of multiple zeta values (MZVs) and new constants, with full analytic continuation across the real s-axis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend precision collider phenomenology beyond the quarkonic case to the more complex gluonic sector by computing massive three-loop form factors—key ingredients for high-accuracy QCD predictions at colliders.

Method: Large-moment method combined with automated guessing, large-scale PSLQ integer-relation searches, and solving record-sized linear recurrences and differential equations using advanced computer algebra systems.

Result: Exact analytic series expansion around s=0 in terms of MZVs; first complete analytic expansion around s=±∞ involving MZVs and three new constants from higher-weight number spaces; reliable analytic continuation over the entire real s-axis.

Conclusion: The results constitute a landmark benchmark for modern symbolic computation and provide essential high-precision input for gluonic contributions in three-loop QCD calculations relevant to collider physics.

Abstract: Massive three-loop form factors for vector, axial-vector, scalar, and pseudoscalar currents are vital for precision collider phenomenology. Extending the quarkonic framework to the more complex gluonic case, in Ref. (arXiv:2609.22034 [hep-ph]) we computed these contributions widely using advanced computer algebra, automated guessing, and large-scale PSLQ searches. Here, we outline the core strategy of the large-moment method and illustrate its main challenges, including solving record-sized recurrences and differential equations. Our high-precision evaluation provides an exact analytic representation around $s=0$ in terms of MZVs. We achieved a complete analytic series expansion around $s=\pm\infty$ for the first time, which depends on MZVs and three constants out of higher number spaces, along with reliable analytic continuations across $s \in (-\infty, \infty)$, serving as a landmark benchmark for modern symbolic computation.

</details>


### [75] [Multitask Jet Analysis with Vision-Language Models: A Physics-Informed Four-Panel Representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24334)
*Lu Zhang,Rachik Soualah,Abbes Amira*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores using Vision-Language Models (VLMs) — adapted via LoRA — on physics-informed jet images for unified jet tagging (QCD, Higgs, W/Z, top), attribute prediction, and cross-panel consistency; achieves strong performance (e.g., 75.05% Macro recall) and robust transfer to simulated and real collider data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The bottleneck in high-energy physics event analysis stems from massive collider data and the need for accurate, structured jet object identification; conventional ML methods lack a unified, interpretable, and scalable interface — motivating exploration of VLMs on rasterized, multi-panel jet representations encoding key physics features.

Method: Converts JetClass jets into 224×224 RGB images with four physics-encoded panels: (1) p_T flow, (2) particle composition (charged/neutral hadrons, EM), (3) impact-parameter significance + displaced-track multiplicity (lifetime), and (4) signed-track p_T density + p_T^k-weighted jet-charge asymmetry; adapts four open VLMs using LoRA for three tasks: (1) 4-class jet classification, (2) 6-field attribute regression/prediction, and (3) replaced-panel localization (cross-panel consistency); evaluates on 24k balanced test jets with ablations and transfer to JetClass-II and real-data Aspen Open Jets.

Result: Gemma4-E4B achieves 75.05% Task 1 Macro recall (74.87% F₁), 93.31% Task 2 field-mean accuracy, and 99.88%/99.75% binary/localization recall; ablation shows impact-parameter lifetime signature dominates heavy-flavor tagging, while jet charge separates W/Z; budget reallocation improves Zqq recall by +9.83 points; transfer to JetClass-II and Aspen Open Jets maintains >70% Task 1 recall and >92% on all Task 2/3 metrics with only 3k target jets.

Conclusion: Rasterizing jet observables — energy flow, species, displacement, and jet charge — into multi-panel images, combined with parameter-efficient VLM adaptation, enables a single instruction-conditioned interface for robust, interpretable, and transferable jet analysis across simulation and real collider data.

Abstract: The enormous recorded data at high-energy physics (HEP) colliders make the accurate identification of physics objects a bottleneck in disentangling event topologies, where machine learning has become a standard tool for jet tagging. In this work, we examine whether Vision-Language Models (VLMs) can provide a common interface for structured jet analysis from a single physics-informed image. Each JetClass jet becomes a 224x224 RGB image with four panels encoding p_T flow of all constituents; charged-hadron, neutral-hadron, and electromagnetic composition; p_T-weighted impact-parameter significances with displaced-track multiplicity; and signed-track p_T densities with local p_T^k-weighted jet-charge asymmetry. Four open VLMs are adapted with low-rank adaptation (LoRA) for class classification across QCD, Higgs, W/Z, and top jets, six-field attribute prediction, and cross-panel consistency with replaced-panel localization, evaluated on 24000 balanced JetClass test jets. Ablations identify the impact-parameter lifetime signature of heavy-flavor tagging as dominant, with jet charge separating the nearly mass-degenerate hadronic W and Z. Zero-shot recall stays near chance (3.33%-10.57%), once adapted, Task 1 Macro recall grows monotonically with training size, and Gemma4-E4B reaches 75.05% (74.87% F_1), 93.31% Task 2 field-mean, and 99.88%/99.75% binary/localization recall. Reallocating budget toward W/Z jets raises Zqq recall by up to 9.83 points at near-constant Macro recall. Transfer to broader-topology JetClass-II and real-data Aspen Open Jets (probing the simulation-to-data gap) is retained: with 3000 target jets, Task 1 recall exceeds 70% on JetClass-II and every Task 2/3 metric exceeds 92%. Thus rasterizing energy flow, species, displacement, and jet charge, with parameter-efficient adaptation, supports jet analysis through one instruction-conditioned interface for future collider data.

</details>


### [76] [Probing Massive Invisible Dark Photons in Higgs Decays via Gluon--Gluon Fusion at ATLAS Run 3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24527)
*Waqas Ahmed,Ammara Ahmad,Shabbar Raza*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: ATLAS reinterprets its Higgs-to-photon-plus-missing-energy search to constrain massive invisible dark photons (γD) in the 30–70 GeV mass range, finding weakening sensitivity beyond ~40 GeV due to falling signal acceptance; limits on BR(H→γγD) range from ~1.6% to ~3.8% at 95% CL.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend ATLAS's existing limit on H→γ+MET (interpreted for massless dark photon γD) to the physically relevant case of a massive, detector-invisible dark photon, which alters kinematics and acceptance.

Method: Reinterpretation of the ATLAS Run 3 (135 fb⁻¹, √s = 13.6 TeV) H→γ+ETmiss search using signal acceptance corrections as a function of m_γD (0–70 GeV); normalization to published ggF-driven massless-γD yield; simplified frequentist CLs limit calculation.

Result: Signal acceptance drops from ~1.0 at m_γD ≤ 40 GeV to 0.888 (50 GeV), 0.736 (60 GeV), and 0.524 (70 GeV); expected (observed) 95% CL limits on BR(H→γγD) rise from 1.64% (1.76%) at 30 GeV to 3.50% (3.75%) at 70 GeV.

Conclusion: The ATLAS analysis remains sensitive to massive invisible dark photons up to 70 GeV, but with quantified degradation in exclusion power above ~40 GeV due to reduced pT and mT spectra efficiency; this reinterpretation provides the first direct experimental constraints on H→γγD for m_γD > 0.

Abstract: The ATLAS Collaboration has searched for Higgs-boson decays into a photon and missing transverse momentum using $135~\mathrm{fb}^{-1}$ of Run~3 proton--proton collision data at $\sqrt{s}=13.6~\mathrm{TeV}$. For the benchmark decay $H\toγ\gD$ with a massless invisible dark photon, ATLAS reports observed (expected) 95\% confidence-level limits of $1.4\%$ ($1.2\%$) on the branching fraction. We reinterpret this search for a massive detector-invisible dark photon with $0\leq m_{\gD}\leq70~\gev$, focusing on Higgs production through gluon--gluon fusion. \audit{The relative signal acceptance, normalized to the massless benchmark, remains nearly unchanged up to $m_{\gD}\simeq40~\gev$, but decreases to $0.888$, $0.736$, and $0.524$ at $50$, $60$, and $70~\gev$, respectively, as the photon-$p_T$ and transverse-mass spectra move toward the analysis thresholds.} Using the published massless gluon--gluon-fusion yield for normalization, we obtain simplified expected (observed) 95\% confidence-level limits on $\BR(H\toγ\gD)$ from $1.64\%$ ($1.76\%$) at $m_{\gD}=30~\gev$ to $3.50\%$ ($3.75\%$) at $70~\gev$. These results extend the ATLAS interpretation to massive invisible dark photons and quantify the resulting loss of sensitivity at large dark-photon mass.

</details>


### [77] [Probing axion-like particle couplings to leptons via the $γγjj$ signal at the FCC-ee](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24566)
*Hua-Ying Zhang,Chong-Xing Yue*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper projects FCC-ee sensitivity to axion-like particles (ALPs) via e⁺e⁻ → γγjj at √s = 91 GeV and 150 ab⁻¹, analyzing three chiral coupling scenarios (c_R=0, c_L=0, c_R=c_L) with unpolarized and polarized beams; polarized beams significantly improve limits on effective ALP–lepton couplings, yielding sub-0.02 GeV⁻¹ sensitivities across distinct ALP mass windows, complementary to other future collider probes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe axion-like particle (ALP) couplings to leptons—specifically ALP–neutrino and ALP–charged lepton interactions—using the high-luminosity, precision environment of FCC-ee, where existing LHC and low-energy experiments have limited reach.

Method: Monte Carlo simulation of e⁺e⁻ → γγjj at √s = 91 GeV with integrated luminosity ℒ = 150 ab⁻¹; analysis of three chiral ALP–lepton coupling configurations (c_R = 0, c_L = 0, c_R = c_L); comparison of sensitivity between unpolarized and longitudinally polarized beams (P_e⁻ = +80%, P_e⁺ = −80%); derivation of projected 95% CL lower limits on effective couplings g_aℓℓ/f_a and g_aνν/f_a.

Result: Polarized beams enhance sensitivity: for c_R = 0 (probing g_aνν/f_a), limit ≈ 0.0105 GeV⁻¹ (10–24 GeV m_a); for c_L = 0 (probing g_aℓℓ/f_a), limit ≈ 0.0174 GeV⁻¹ (10–27 GeV); for c_R = c_L (probing g_aνν/f_a), limit ≈ 0.00394 GeV⁻¹ (45–80 GeV). All limits are complementary to other future collider channels.

Conclusion: FCC-ee with beam polarization offers uniquely competitive sensitivity to ALP–lepton couplings in specific mass ranges, making it a powerful probe of ALP physics beyond current experimental reach—especially for neutrino-coupled ALPs inaccessible to hadron colliders.

Abstract: We study the sensitivity of the Future Circular Collider in electron-positron mode (FCC-ee) to the axion-like particle (ALP) couplings with leptons via the process $e^{+}e^{-} \rightarrow γγjj$ at the center-of-mass energy $\sqrt{s}=91$ GeV with the integrated luminosity $\mathcal{L}=150~\text{ab}^{-1}$. We analyze three scenarios, $\mathbf{c}_R=0$, $\mathbf{c}_L=0$ and $\mathbf{c}_R=\mathbf{c}_L$, for both unpolarized and polarized beams with $(P_{e^-}, P_{e^+}) = (+80\%, -80\%)$, and derive projected lower limits on the effective coupling strengths. The polarized case provides higher sensitivity than the unpolarized case. Specifically, for the $\mathbf{c}_R=0$ scenario (probing the ALP-neutrino coupling $\text{Tr}(g_{aνν})/f_a$), the projected sensitivity in the polarized case reaches approximately $0.0105~\text{GeV}^{-1}$ for the ALP mass in the range $10$--$24$ GeV; for $\mathbf{c}_L=0$ (probing the ALP-charged lepton coupling $\text{Tr}(g_{a\ell\ell})/f_a$), it reaches $0.0174~\text{GeV}^{-1}$ in the $10$--$27$ GeV range; and for $\mathbf{c}_R=\mathbf{c}_L$ (probing the ALP-neutrino coupling $\text{Tr}(g_{aνν})/f_a$), it reaches $0.00394~\text{GeV}^{-1}$ in the $45$--$80$ GeV range. In these ALP mass ranges, our projected sensitivities are complementary to the projected sensitivities from other search channels at future colliders.

</details>


### [78] [Exact Amplitude Reconstruction in the Real Common-Phase Sector of Small-x Diffractive Energy Flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22899)
*Sanskriti Agrawal,Raktim Abir*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces an operator-level framework to reconstruct the angular structure of small-x diffractive amplitudes using energy-flow measurements, specifically for coherent diffractive dijet production in the leading-eikonal approximation; it links angular multipoles of the dipole amplitude, gluon Wigner distribution, and diffractive scattering amplitude, and shows that—under real common-phase conditions—the normalized scattering amplitude can be reconstructed (up to sign) from the square root of the normalized energy-flow distribution; for generic complex amplitudes, intensity measurements yield only amplitude autocorrelations, making phase retrieval non-unique without extra constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable tomographic reconstruction of small-x gluonic structure—particularly angular correlations—in diffractive processes via experimentally accessible energy-flow observables at future facilities like the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC).

Method: Operator-level analysis in the leading-eikonal approximation; derivation of explicit radial transforms linking angular multipoles of the dipole amplitude, the gluon Wigner distribution, and the diffractive scattering amplitude; analytical treatment of phase retrieval from intensity (energy-flow) measurements, including conditions for uniqueness and limitations due to harmonic-dependent phases and diffractive zeros.

Result: A direct reconstruction formula for the normalized scattering amplitude from energy-flow data under real common-phase conditions; identification that generic complex amplitudes lead only to autocorrelation information from intensity, implying inherent phase ambiguity; quantification of corrections beyond leading eikonal and practical criteria for experimental extraction at the EIC.

Conclusion: Energy-flow measurements provide a viable, though phase-limited, probe of angular structure in small-x diffraction; full amplitude reconstruction requires either restrictive kinematic conditions (e.g., real common phase) or complementary constraints; the framework establishes a rigorous foundation for future EIC-based gluon Wigner tomography.

Abstract: We develop an operator-level framework for reconstructing the angular structure of small-$x$ diffractive amplitudes from energy-flow measurements. Focusing on coherent diffractive dijet production in the leading-eikonal approximation, we establish the relation between the angular multipoles of the dipole amplitude, the gluon Wigner distribution, and the diffractive scattering amplitude, while keeping their distinct radial transforms explicit. We show that, in the real common-phase sector and away from diffractive zeros, the normalized scattering amplitude can be reconstructed directly, up to a global sign convention, from the square root of the normalized energy-flow distribution. For generic complex amplitudes, the measured intensity instead determines autocorrelations of the amplitude spectrum and phase retrieval is not unique without additional information. We further discuss corrections arising from harmonic-dependent phases, practical conditions for an EIC extraction, and effects beyond the leading-eikonal approximation.

</details>


### [79] [Resolving the universality and polarization puzzles in $J/ψ$ hadroproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24674)
*An-Ping Chen,Jun Guo,Yan-Qing Ma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper resolves two longstanding puzzles in prompt J/ψ hadroproduction—LDME universality tension between hadronic and e+e− data, and simultaneous description of yield and weak polarization—by applying threshold-resummed fragmentation-function formalisms in both NRQCD and SGF frameworks; it finds a common LDME region where ³S₁⁸ and ³P_J⁸ dominate the yield, their transverse contributions cancel, and spin-flip transitions of O(v⁴) in the ³S₁⁸ channel naturally explain both observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve two interrelated puzzles in prompt J/ψ hadroproduction: (1) incompatible constraints on the same color-octet LDME combination from fixed-order hadronic vs. e⁺e⁻ analyses (universality violation), and (2) inability to simultaneously describe the measured cross section and weak transverse polarization.

Method: Applies leading-logarithmic threshold resummation within the fragmentation-function formalism for high-p_T J/ψ production, implemented consistently in both nonrelativistic QCD (NRQCD) and soft-gluon factorization (SGF); combines resummed fits to Tevatron/LHC hadroproduction data with resummed e⁺e⁻ annihilation bounds to constrain LDMEs; analyzes spin structure via a one-parameter spin-flip ansatz in the ³S₁⁸ channel.

Result: Threshold resummation eliminates the LDME universality tension: a common, physically consistent LDME region emerges in both NRQCD and SGF that satisfies both hadronic and e⁺e⁻ constraints; the ¹S₀⁸ contribution is suppressed; the yield is dominated by ³S₁⁸ and ³P_J⁸, whose leading transverse parts largely cancel; this cancellation enhances sensitivity to O(v⁴) spin-flip transitions in ³S₁⁸, enabling simultaneous description of yield and weak polarization.

Conclusion: Threshold resummation restores NRQCD universality for CO LDMEs across production channels and provides a unified, parameter-efficient explanation of both J/ψ yield and polarization—without introducing new degrees of freedom—valid also for ψ(2S).

Abstract: Prompt $J/ψ$ hadroproduction poses two related puzzles: fixed-order hadronic and $e^+e^-$ analyses give incompatible constraints on the same color-octet (CO) long-distance matrix element (LDME) combination, and the weak observed polarization is difficult to reproduce together with the yield. We analyze high-$p_T$ production in the fragmentation-function formalism with leading-logarithmic threshold resummation in both nonrelativistic QCD (NRQCD) and soft-gluon factorization (SGF). Combining the resummed hadroproduction fit with bounds from resummed $e^+e^-$ production, we find in both frameworks a common LDME region satisfying the two constraints simultaneously: threshold resummation resolves the universality tension present at fixed order. There, the $^{1}S_{0}^{[8]}$ contribution is small and the yield is governed by the $^{3}S_{1}^{[8]}$ and $^{3}P_{J}^{[8]}$ channels, whose leading transverse contributions largely cancel. The cancellation amplifies spin-symmetry-breaking transitions in the $^{3}S_{1}^{[8]}$ channel, so that within a one-parameter spin-flip ansatz a contribution of natural order $v^4$ accommodates both the measured yields and the weak polarization. A similar sensitivity holds for $ψ(2S)$ production.

</details>


### [80] [The phenomenology of Axion Relic Pockets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24714)
*Wafaa Khater,M. C. David Marsh,Charalampos Nikolis*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies axion relic pockets—phase-transition remnants stabilized by hot axion gas—as dark matter candidates, computes axion-to-photon conversion in their compact spherical geometry, identifies atomic electric fields as sites for high-energy electromagnetic cascades with isotropic, upward-going signatures, sets first terrestrial limits using IceCube data, and discusses detection prospects via fluorescence, radio Cherenkov, and direct detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Axion relic pockets are a theoretically plausible but phenomenologically unexplored dark matter candidate; their compact, spherical structure and coupling to electromagnetism warrant dedicated signal modeling and experimental constraints.

Method: Derives the axion-to-photon conversion rate inside axion relic pockets by incorporating finite-size effects and spherical geometry; models conversion in atomic electric fields; simulates resulting electromagnetic cascades; applies IceCube upward-going shower data to set exclusion limits; assesses sensitivity of atmospheric, balloon-borne, and direct detection experiments.

Result: First terrestrial experimental limits on axion relic pockets from IceCube; identification of isotropic, upward-going electromagnetic showers as a smoking-gun signature; demonstration that only a narrow region of parameter space is currently constrained; evaluation of complementary detection channels (fluorescence, radio Cherenkov, direct detection).

Conclusion: Axion relic pockets remain viable dark matter candidates across most of their parameter space; this work establishes foundational phenomenology and detection strategies, enabling future multi-messenger searches.

Abstract: Axion relic pockets are phase-transition remnants consisting of regions of false vacuum stabilised from collapse by a hot axion gas. Axion relic pockets can comprise dark matter, but little is known about their phenomenology. We consider trapped axions coupled to electromagnetism and derive the axion to photon conversion rate accounting for the pocket's compactness and spherical geometry. As a case study, we show that axion-photon conversion occurs in atomic electric fields, leading to high-energy electromagnetic cascades that may be detectable by neutrino, cosmic-ray, and dark-matter experiments. The distribution of electromagnetic showers is inherently isotropic, and upward-going showers provide a smoking-gun signal for the model. Using recent data from the IceCube neutrino telescope, we derive the first limits from terrestrial experiments on axion relic pockets as dark matter. However, for the simplest realisation of the model, only a small part of the parameter space can give signals strong enough to be constrained by current data. We also comment on the prospects for discovering axion relic pockets using fluorescence and radio Cherenkov detection by atmospheric instruments and balloon-borne payloads, as well as traditional dark matter direct detection experiments. These results provide the necessary tools to initiate phenomenological studies of axion relic pockets across different environments.

</details>


### [81] [Comparative Study of Color Glass Condensate and Collinear Frameworks for Large-Transverse-Momentum Semi-Inclusive Deep Inelastic Scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24783)
*Swagato Mukherjee,Björn Schenke,Shaswat Tiwari*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper compares collinear and Color Glass Condensate (CGC) factorization frameworks for large-transverse-momentum SIDIS, using COMPASS, HERA, and projected EIC data; finds limited discriminative power between schemes for nuclear targets at EIC and highlights sensitivity of LO CGC predictions to the gluon momentum fraction x_g, indicating need for beyond-eikonal corrections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve theoretical ambiguity in describing large-Pt SIDIS: whether collinear factorization (PDFs + FFs) or small-x CGC framework (eikonal dipole amplitudes) is more appropriate—especially at high energies (small x) and for nuclear targets, where both formalisms may apply but yield different physical interpretations and predictions.

Method: Systematic comparison of leading-order (LO) and next-to-leading-order (NLO) collinear QCD factorization versus LO CGC (eikonal dipole) factorization for SIDIS cross sections; quantitative fits and predictions performed using COMPASS and HERA experimental data; extrapolated to EIC kinematics for proton and nuclear targets; uncertainty quantification includes scale variations and model dependence of dipole amplitude and x_g.

Result: Within current theoretical uncertainties, collinear and CGC descriptions are difficult to distinguish for nuclear large-Pt SIDIS at the EIC; LO CGC predictions show strong sensitivity to the choice of gluon longitudinal momentum fraction x_g, implying substantial beyond-eikonal corrections are required in EIC kinematics.

Conclusion: The degeneracy between collinear and CGC factorizations for nuclear SIDIS at large Pt limits their discriminative power at the EIC; robust interpretation of future EIC data will require inclusion of higher-twist, finite-x, and sub-eikonal corrections in the CGC framework, alongside precision NLO+ collinear calculations.

Abstract: We study the Semi-Inclusive Deep Inelastic Scattering (SIDIS) cross-section at large values of hadron transverse momentum $P_t \gtrsim Q$, with $Q^2$ being the photon virtuality. This kinematic regime of SIDIS allows for a collinear factorization in terms of parton distribution functions (PDFs) and collinear fragmentation functions (FFs). On the other hand, at high energies, i.e., at small Bjorken $x$, the same SIDIS process can also be factorized within the Color Glass Condensate (CGC) framework in terms of eikonal dipole amplitudes. In this work, we perform a systematic comparison of the leading-order (LO) and next-to-leading-order (NLO) collinear and the LO CGC factorizations of this process based on the COMPASS and HERA data. We further provide comparisons of these two factorization frameworks for nucleon and nuclear large-$P_t$ SIDIS in the expected Electron Ion Collider (EIC) kinematics. We find, within the present theoretical uncertainties, that it might be difficult to distinguish between these two factorization schemes for nuclear SIDIS at the EIC. We also observe a significant dependence of the LO eikonal CGC predictions on the choice of longitudinal momentum fraction $x_g$, suggesting significant beyond-eikonal corrections for the EIC kinematics.

</details>


### [82] [A New Probe of Strongly-Interacting Dark Sector using Neutrino Telescopes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24887)
*Jose A. Macias Cruz,Christopher V. Cappiello,P. S. Bhupal Dev,Suchita Kulkarni*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes using cosmic neutrino background scattering to detect dark vector mesons from a confining dark sector, yielding a spectroscopic absorption feature in the neutrino energy spectrum observable by next-gen neutrino telescopes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe strongly-interacting dark matter via a QCD-like confining dark sector, leveraging the cosmic neutrino background as a target for resonance production.

Method: Analyzes resonant production of dark vector mesons in high-energy neutrino–cosmic neutrino background scattering, computing resulting spectral absorption features and sensitivity projections for IceCube-Gen2 Radio.

Result: Identifies a distinctive, mass-dependent absorption feature in the cosmic neutrino spectrum; demonstrates broad regions of dark-sector parameter space (e.g., dark confinement scale Λ_D ∼ 1–100 MeV, coupling g_D ∼ 0.1–1) are detectable with IceCube-Gen2 Radio.

Conclusion: Cosmic neutrino background scattering enables spectroscopic discovery of dark-sector resonances and provides a novel, direct probe of dark-sector–neutrino interactions.

Abstract: A confining dark sector modeled after quantum chromodynamics provides a well-motivated framework for strongly-interacting dark matter. We show that dark vector mesons in a confining dark sector can be resonantly produced in high-energy neutrino scattering on the cosmic neutrino background, generating a distinctive absorption feature in the cosmic neutrino energy spectrum. Unlike conventional attenuation effects, this feature directly reflects the mass spectrum of dark resonances and can therefore serve as a spectroscopic signature of a strongly-interacting dark sector. We find that a broad range of dark-sector parameter space is within the reach of future neutrino telescopes, including IceCube-Gen2 Radio. Our proposal thus turns the cosmic neutrino background into a target for discovering dark-sector resonances, while simultaneously providing a novel probe of dark sector interactions with neutrinos.

</details>


### [83] [Two-loop amplitude for the associated production of a top-anti-top pair and a $W$ boson at hadron colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24910)
*Mattia Pozzoli*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the NNLO QCD inclusive cross-section for tt̄W production using the first exact leading-colour two-loop amplitude, circumventing elliptic functions via rational-coefficient special functions evaluated with differential equations and finite-field methods; the double-virtual correction is obtained via 5D grid interpolation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To achieve high-precision theoretical predictions for tt̄W production—a key background to new physics searches and a probe of top-quark EW couplings—requiring full NNLO QCD accuracy, which had been hindered by the complexity of elliptic two-loop integrals.

Method: Exact leading-colour two-loop QCD amplitude computation; representation of the amplitude in terms of special functions (non-elliptic) with rational coefficients; evaluation of special functions via canonical differential equations; computation of rational coefficients using finite field arithmetic and numerical reconstruction; double-virtual correction obtained by interpolation over a five-dimensional phase-space grid.

Result: First complete NNLO QCD inclusive cross-section prediction for pp → tt̄W + X; successful elimination of elliptic functions from the amplitude representation; validated numerical stability and precision of the finite-field + differential-equation + interpolation framework.

Conclusion: The presented method establishes a viable pathway to NNLO calculations for multi-scale, multi-leg processes involving massive quarks, overcoming previous bottlenecks from elliptic integrals; enables reliable theory predictions for LHC Run 3 and HL-LHC tt̄W analyses.

Abstract: In this contribution I present a calculation of the inclusive cross-section for the associated production of a top-anti-top pair and a W boson at next-to-next-to-leading order in QCD, based on the first exact computation of the two-loop QCD amplitude at leading colour. I discuss a strategy to express the amplitude in terms of a set of special functions with rational coefficients, overcoming the obstacle posed by the presence of elliptic functions. The special functions are evaluated through the method of differential equations, while the rational coefficients are computed using finite field techniques. For the computation of the inclusive cross-section, the double-virtual correction is evaluated by interpolating a five-dimensional grid.

</details>


### [84] [On the failure of the $12\%$ rule in $J/ψ$ and $ψ(2S)$ decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24961)
*Arthur Vereijken,Francesco Giacosa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates the violation of the 12% rule in J/ψ and ψ(2S) → VP decays, finding that ψ(2S) data are well described by a minimal chiral model, while J/ψ data require additional SU(3) breaking and electromagnetic effects; it shows that extended J/ψ fits are effectively equivalent to models with J/ψ–glueball mixing, making glueball presence indistinguishable, and suggests the ρπ puzzle stems from extra J/ψ→VP contributions rather than ψ(2S)-specific suppression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the strong violation of the 12% rule—particularly in J/ψ and ψ(2S) → vector–pseudoscalar (VP) decays like ρπ—by testing whether it arises from missing dynamics in J/ψ decays, ψ(2S)-specific suppression, or exotic components like vector glueballs.

Method: Uses an effective chiral Lagrangian framework incorporating both strong and electromagnetic interactions (via vector meson dominance); performs χ² fits to VP decay data for J/ψ and ψ(2S) separately, first with a minimal model (3 parameters), then extended with SU(3) flavor breaking and electromagnetic corrections (6 parameters); compares fit quality and assesses equivalence to J/ψ–glueball mixing scenarios.

Result: ψ(2S) → VP decays fit well with minimal model (χ²_red ≈ 1.12); J/ψ → VP requires extended model (χ²_red ≈ 1.97 vs. 16.5 for minimal); extended J/ψ fits are mathematically equivalent to those including J/ψ–vector-glueball mixing, rendering glueball existence untestable from these decays alone; analysis indicates dominant role of additional J/ψ→VP amplitudes—not ψ(2S) suppression—in explaining the ρπ anomaly.

Conclusion: The ρπ puzzle is likely due to underestimated or missing contributions in J/ψ → VP amplitudes (e.g., SU(3) breaking, EM effects), not intrinsic ψ(2S) suppression or necessity of vector glueball mixing; current data cannot discriminate glueball presence because the phenomenological effects are degenerate with standard hadronic corrections.

Abstract: The $12\%$ rule relates hadronic decays of the $J/ψ$ and $ψ(2S)$ through the ratio of their wave functions at the origin, but is strongly violated in several vector--pseudoscalar ($VP$) channels, most prominently in the $ρπ$ one. We study these decays within an effective chiral framework including strong and electromagnetic interactions, with the latter implemented through vector meson dominance. The $ψ(2S)\to VP$ data are described well by a relatively minimal model with three free parameters, yielding $χ^2_{\rm red}\simeq 6.71/(11-3)=1.12$. In contrast, the corresponding description of the $J/ψ$ decays is insufficient ($χ^2{\rm _{red}}\simeq 132/(11-3)=16.5$) and requires additional $\mathrm{SU}(3)$ flavor breaking and electromagnetic contributions for a fair agreement with data: $χ^2_{\rm red}\simeq 9.87/(11-6)= 1.97$. In the past, a small mixing of $J/ψ$ with an invisible vector glueball was considered as a way to describe the decay patterns. However, we will show that our extended fits are `de facto' equivalent to those where a vector glueball mixes with the $J/ψ$, so that we cannot distinguish whether or not the vector glueball is present. Our results suggest that an important part of the $ρπ$ puzzle may arise from additional contributions to the $J/ψ\to VP$ amplitudes rather than from a suppression mechanism specific to the $ψ(2S)$.

</details>


### [85] [A closer look at the LZ 248 keV event through the lens of cosmic-ray boosted dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24982)
*Bhavesh Chauhan,Soham Sahasrabuddhe,Manibrata Sen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores whether cosmic ray boosted dark matter (CRDM) with spin-dependent, pseudoscalar-mediated interactions can explain a single 248 keV nuclear recoil event observed by LZ, finding that a ~300 MeV pseudoscalar mediator with O(1) couplings—rather than contact interactions—is viable and avoids excess low-energy events.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the unexplained 248 keV nuclear recoil event reported by LZ without contradicting null results at lower energies, using CRDM as a non-standard dark matter candidate.

Method: Analyzes CRDM production, propagation, and detection with relativistic kinematics; considers spin-dependent interactions mediated by a pseudoscalar; compares predictions for nuclear recoil spectra under contact (non-relativistic operator O₆) vs. finite-mass mediator scenarios.

Result: Spin-dependent pseudoscalar-mediated CRDM can reproduce the 248 keV event while suppressing lower-energy recoils; however, the contact interaction interpretation is invalid due to breakdown of effective field theory (coupling scale ≈ momentum transfer); a ~300 MeV pseudoscalar mediator with O(1) couplings fits the data consistently.

Conclusion: A light pseudoscalar mediator (~300 MeV) provides a theoretically consistent and phenomenologically viable explanation for the LZ anomaly within the CRDM framework, favoring finite-mass mediation over contact interactions.

Abstract: We investigate whether cosmic ray boosted dark matter (CRDM) can explain the 248 keV nuclear recoil event observed by the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) collaboration. We consider spin-dependent interactions, mediated by a pseudoscalar, and incorporate relativistic kinematics in CRDM production, propagation, and detection. We find that spin-dependent interactions can produce sufficient number of nuclear recoils in the observed region of interest, without populating events at lower energies, which have not been observed. However, for a benchmark pseudoscalar interaction corresponding to the non-relativistic effective operator $\mathbb{O}_6$, the coupling required to produce the observed event corresponds to an energy scale comparable to the momentum transfer scales, thus making contact interaction approach challenging. Instead, we find that a pseudoscalar mediator with mass $\sim 300\,$MeV and $O(1)$ couplings can provide a promising solution to this unexplained event.

</details>


### [86] [Three-Flavoured Thermal Leptogenesis Without Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24990)
*Angus Spalding*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a singlet-scalar extension of the Type-I seesaw mechanism to enable successful thermal leptogenesis with hierarchical right-handed neutrinos at the electroweak scale, avoiding fine-tuning and hierarchy problems inherent in the minimal model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve tensions in minimal Type-I seesaw leptogenesis: hierarchical spectra require high scales (worsening the Higgs hierarchy problem), while low-scale leptogenesis typically demands fine-tuned quasi-degeneracy or cancellations in light-neutrino mass generation.

Method: Introduces a real singlet scalar φ coupling non-diagonally to right-handed neutrinos via $-y_{ij}φN_iN_j$ ($i\neq j$); computes new CP-violating contributions to heavy-neutrino decays; derives analytic upper bound on scalar-induced CP asymmetry; solves fully flavoured Boltzmann equations and uses density-matrix formalism for numerical analysis.

Result: Demonstrates that observed baryon asymmetry can be reproduced with hierarchical right-handed neutrino masses as low as the electroweak scale; new CP violation from scalar interactions yields sufficient asymmetry without enhancing washout.

Conclusion: The singlet-scalar extension alleviates key theoretical tensions of standard leptogenesis—enabling viable low-scale, hierarchical thermal leptogenesis without fine-tuning or exacerbating the Higgs hierarchy problem.

Abstract: We investigate thermal leptogenesis in an extension of the Type-I seesaw in which a real singlet scalar couples non-diagonally to the right-handed neutrinos. The interactions $-y_{ij}φN_iN_j$, with $i\neq j$, introduce new sources of CP violation in heavy-neutrino decays with a negligible enhancement of the standard leptonic washout. We derive an analytic upper bound on the scalar-induced CP asymmetry and present numerical results using fully flavoured Boltzmann equations and the density-matrix formalism. We show that the observed baryon asymmetry can be generated with a hierarchical right-handed-neutrino spectrum at the electroweak scale. In the minimal Type-I seesaw, lowering the scale of decay leptogenesis typically requires either a tuned quasi-degeneracy of the right-handed-neutrino masses or cancellations between the tree-level and one-loop contributions to the light-neutrino masses, while hierarchical leptogenesis instead requires a high scale that aggravates the Higgs hierarchy problem. This singlet-scalar extension appears to avoid these tensions, allowing successful leptogenesis with a hierarchical right-handed-neutrino spectrum down to the electroweak scale.

</details>


<div id='hep-th'></div>

# hep-th [[Back]](#toc)

### [87] [Microcanonical Operator Spectrum, BPS Chaos and Free Probability Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22422)
*Alexandre Belin,Yichao Fu,Federico La Rocca*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the eigenspectrum of simple operators projected onto microcanonical energy windows in chaotic quantum many-body systems, proving that—under Haar-random orientation assumptions—it follows Gaussian random matrix statistics, using both direct probabilistic and Free Probability methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To refine the Eigenstate Thermalization Hypothesis by moving beyond statistical moments of matrix elements to a full spectral characterization—especially crucial in degenerate Hamiltonian spectra where moments alone are insufficient.

Method: Assumes Haar-random relative orientation between eigenbases of the observable and Hamiltonian; derives the projected operator’s eigenvalue distribution directly from the induced measure on matrix entries, and corroborates via Free Probability Theory (e.g., asymptotic freeness and Marchenko–Pastur-type limits).

Result: The eigenspectrum of a simple operator restricted to a narrow microcanonical energy window converges to the semicircle law—the hallmark of Gaussian Orthogonal/Unitary Ensemble statistics—in the thermodynamic limit, provided the window contains sufficiently many eigenstates.

Conclusion: Gaussian random matrix behavior emerges universally for projected operator spectra under minimal randomness assumptions, strengthening the foundation of eigenstate thermalization and offering new diagnostics for quantum chaos, including in BPS-protected systems.

Abstract: Matrix elements of simple operators are central to thermalization in chaotic quantum many-body systems. Their moments are often described statistically through the Eigenstate Thermalization Hypothesis. In this paper, we study a more refined characterization of these matrix elements: the eigenspectrum of simple operators once projected to microcanonical energy windows. The spectrum encodes all moments of the matrix elements at once, and is particularly relevant when the spectrum of the Hamiltonian is degenerate. Assuming that the eigenbasis of the simple operator is Haar-randomly oriented with respect to that of the Hamiltonian, we prove that the spectrum of an operator projected to a small microcanonical window obeys Gaussian random matrix statistics. We do this directly by studying the probability distribution inherited from the random orientation, as well as using tools from Free Probability Theory. We discuss the implications for BPS chaos and more generally for eigenstate thermalization.

</details>


### [88] [Tripartite Form Universality in Holographic Entropy Inequalities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22504)
*Veronika E. Hubeny,Yu Liu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper conjectures and provides evidence that all holographic entropy inequalities can be expressed in a 'tripartite form'—a sum of tripartite and conditional tripartite information terms with unit coefficients—and explicitly recasts two known infinite families into this form, revealing structural properties and deriving useful identities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether all holographic entropy inequalities (beyond subadditivity) can be uniformly expressed in the recently proposed 'tripartite form', which compactly encodes entanglement structure but lacks a priori generality guarantees.

Method: Analytical derivation and algebraic manipulation of entropy expressions; systematic recasting of two infinite families of holographic entropy inequalities (from arXiv:2309.15145) into tripartite form; derivation of new information-theoretic identities; proof of structural properties satisfied by any inequality in tripartite form.

Result: Confirmed that both infinite families admit exact tripartite form representations; derived explicit expressions for all members; uncovered novel identities relating multipartite information quantities; established general structural constraints (e.g., coefficient bounds, sign patterns, redundancy conditions) obeyed by any holographic entropy inequality in tripartite form.

Conclusion: The tripartite form is more universal than previously assumed; the conjecture that all holographic entropy inequalities admit such representation is strongly supported, enhancing conceptual clarity, computational tractability, and potential classification of holographic entanglement constraints.

Abstract: To elucidate the meaning of holographic entropy inequalities (beyond subadditivity) which characterize the entanglement structure of geometric states in holography, arXiv:2309.06296 proposed the "tripartite form" for these inequalities, consisting of tripartite information and conditional tripartite information terms with unit coefficients. While this provides a compact and useful packaging of the inequalities, it is not a priori guaranteed that all inequalities can be recast in this form. Here we conjecture that they can, and present substantial evidence, by proving that the two known infinite families of holographic entropy inequalities found in arXiv:2309.15145 can indeed be written in the tripartite form. This is significant because such recasting is particularly nontrivial for these families. Apart from providing the explicit tripartite form expressions for every member of these two infinite families, we detail how we arrived at them, in the process deriving several useful identities which may serve as stepping stones to formulate further repackaging of the corresponding information quantities. We also illustrate the power of the tripartite form by proving a number of structural properties satisfied by any holographic entropy inequality.

</details>


### [89] [External Sources in Pseudo-Quantum Electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22526)
*L. H. C. Borges,A. A. Nogueira,F. A. Barone*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies interactions between stationary point-like sources—including electric charges, Dirac points, and topological sources—in Pseudo-Quantum Electrodynamics (PQED), computing energies, forces, torques, and fields; it reveals that PQED’s nonlocality yields anisotropic, non-central interactions and nonzero torques absent in standard planar Maxwell theory, with implications for modeling topological defects in 2D condensed matter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how nonlocality in PQED modifies electromagnetic interactions among stationary point-like and topological sources in two dimensions, especially compared to local planar Maxwell electrodynamics, and to identify novel physical effects relevant to condensed matter systems.

Method: Analytical computation of interaction energies, forces, torques, and electromagnetic field configurations for various source configurations (electric charges, Dirac points, generalized topological sources) within the PQED framework; systematic comparison with results from ordinary planar Maxwell electrodynamics.

Result: PQED produces intrinsically anisotropic and non-central interactions for topological sources, resulting in nonvanishing torques—effects absent in local planar Maxwell theory; the directional nature of interactions is a direct consequence of PQED's nonlocal structure.

Conclusion: The nonlocal character of PQED fundamentally alters static electromagnetic interactions in two dimensions, enabling qualitatively new phenomena—especially torque generation and anisotropy—for topological sources; this makes PQED a promising effective framework for describing topological defects and anisotropic responses in planar condensed matter systems.

Abstract: In this letter, we investigate the interaction between stationary point-like external sources within the framework of Pseudo-Quantum Electrodynamics (PQED). Besides ordinary electric charges, we consider configurations involving Dirac points and generalized topological sources. For the configurations analyzed, we compute the corresponding interaction energies, forces, torques, and electromagnetic field configurations. Furthermore, we compare the results obtained in PQED with those arising in ordinary planar Maxwell electrodynamics, showing that the nonlocal character of PQED gives rise to physical effects with no counterpart in the conventional local planar theory. In particular, our results show that topological sources induce intrinsically anisotropic and non-central interactions, leading to the emergence of nontrivial torques between the interacting objects. The directional character of these interactions suggests possible applications in the effective description of topological defects and anisotropic phenomena in planar condensed matter systems.

</details>


### [90] [Phase Transitions at More Unusual Values of Theta](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22613)
*Dhruv Aldas,Hitoshi Murayama,Bea Noether,Digvijay Roy Varier*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the θ-dependence of vacuum energy in a family of QCD-like theories derived from deformed N=2 supersymmetric SU(N_c) gauge theories via AMSB, using a simpler N=1 effective field theory method—enabling exact vacuum structure analysis and discovery of phase transitions at fractional and irrational θ/π.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a simpler, more broadly applicable method for analyzing the θ-vacuum structure and phase transitions in strongly coupled gauge theories, beyond the limitations of full N=2 machinery.

Method: Constructs N=1 effective field theories from N=2 SU(N_c) gauge theories with N_f fundamental hypermultiplets, deformed by anomaly-mediated supersymmetry breaking (AMSB); computes vacuum energy as a function of θ using exact N=1 techniques (e.g., holomorphy, instanton calculus, and chiral ring constraints).

Result: Reproduces prior N=2 results with greater simplicity; extends analysis to infinitely many previously intractable theories; identifies θ-values—both fractional and irrational multiples of π—at which first- or second-order vacuum phase transitions occur.

Conclusion: The N=1 AMSB-deformed framework provides a powerful, computationally tractable approach to θ-physics in non-perturbative gauge theories, revealing rich vacuum structure and novel phase transition loci inaccessible to conventional N=2 methods.

Abstract: We calculate the $θ$ dependence of the vacuum energy in an infinite family of cousins of QCD, where the vacuum structure can be analyzed exactly. These theories are $\mathcal{N}=1$ EFTs that descend from $\mathcal{N}=2$ $SU(N_c)$ gauge theory with $N_f$ flavors of fundamental hypermultiplets, that are then deformed by anomaly mediated supersymmetry breaking (AMSB). We reproduce results found in previous work that used the full $\mathcal{N}=2$ machinery but via a much simpler $\mathcal{N}=1$ method. Our method easily extends to infinitely many more theories for which the full $\mathcal{N}=2$ approach becomes intractable. We identify values of $θ$ for which the theory undergoes a phase transition, and find that they can occur at both fractional and irrational values of $θ/π$.

</details>


### [91] [Semiclassical reconstruction of the Toda pole lattice via analytic continuation of the Probabilistic Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22717)
*Jacques Distler,Bahaa Elshimy*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs Toda field theory correlators probabilistically via Coulomb gas representation, interprets n-point functions as moments of Gaussian random variables, derives convergence and integrability constraints (extending Seiberg bounds), uses Mellin-Barnes analytic continuation to reveal meromorphic integrability divisors, and shows that in the semiclassical limit, W_n-closure selects Virasoro-like submodules whose integrability conditions reproduce Toda charge pole lattices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous probabilistic foundation for Toda field theory correlators, extend the domain of validity beyond classical Seiberg bounds, and clarify the geometric and algebraic structure of integrability constraints—especially their semiclassical emergence and relation to W-symmetry and Liouville theory.

Method: Probabilistic reinterpretation of Toda n-point functions as Gaussian moments; derivation of L^2/L^p convergence, triviality, and local integrability conditions; analytic continuation via Mellin-Barnes representation; analysis of meromorphic integrability divisors; semiclassical (b→0) asymptotics; imposition of W_n-closure to isolate rank-one Virasoro submodules.

Result: An extended region of convergence for Toda correlators is established; Seiberg bounds are reformulated as probabilistic integrability constraints; local convergence boundaries become meromorphic divisors; in the b→0 limit, these divisors reconstruct the Liouville charge pole lattice; for A_n Toda, W_n-closure selects Virasoro-like submodules where scalar integrability reduces to Liouville-type conditions recovering Toda charge lattices.

Conclusion: The probabilistic Coulomb gas framework yields a unified, analytic, and geometric understanding of convergence, integrability, and symmetry reduction in Toda field theory—bridging stochastic analysis, conformal field theory, and semiclassical quantization.

Abstract: We develop a probabilistic construction of Toda field theory correlators from the Coulomb gas representation. Reinterpreting $n$-point functions of primaries as $w$-moments of random variables with respect to a Gaussian measure, we derive constraints on the Toda momenta and coupling constant. $L^2$ and $L^p$ convergence conditions for the associated random variables, triviality constraints on the correlators, and local integrability conditions for the cross-moments are derived. These constraints define an extended region of convergence for the Toda correlators and provide a systematic, probabilistic interpretation of the Seiberg bounds. We proceed to analytically continue the moment indices by employing a Mellin-Barnes representation of the random variables. The local convergence boundaries become meromorphic local integrability divisors, and, in the semiclassical $b\to0$ limit, these divisors reconstruct the Liouville charge pole lattice. For $A_n$ Toda theory, we show that natural $W_n$-closure conditions in this semiclassical limit select rank-one, Virasoro-like submodules inside the full Toda theory. On these slices, the scalar integrability conditions reduce to Liouville-like divisors and recover the corresponding Toda charge pole lattices.

</details>


### [92] [Toroidal Charged AdS Branes and Deformed Toda Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23079)
*Sangheon Yun*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives Toda-type equations for magnetically charged p-branes in AdS spacetime, constructs exact and perturbative black brane solutions (including sinh and dilatonic variants), computes thermodynamic quantities via holographic renormalization, and shows that dilaton coupling modifies the Stefan-Boltzmann scaling while preserving the first law of black hole thermodynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and characterize static, toroidal, magnetically charged black p-branes in asymptotically AdS spacetimes with dilatonic coupling, and to understand their holographic thermodynamic properties—including deviations from conformal scaling induced by scalar hair.

Method: Reduction of higher-dimensional Einstein–Maxwell–dilaton equations to coupled 1D Toda-type ODEs using $T^n$ symmetry; exact integration in pure Maxwell case; perturbative expansion in dilaton coupling parameter $\varepsilon = a^2$; linearization yielding Pöschl–Teller equations; holographic renormalization to compute conserved charges and thermodynamics.

Result: Exact $\sinh$-type black brane solution asymptotic to $\mathrm{AdS}_{p+2} \times T^n$; dilatonic generalization with running torus modulus and nontrivial dilaton profile; $O(a^2)$ backreacted solution; explicit expressions for Hawking temperature $T_H$, entropy density $s$, mass density $\mathcal{M}$, and verification of first law $d\mathcal{M}=T_H\,ds$; modified Stefan–Boltzmann exponent $p_{\rm eff} = p + a^2/2$; decoupling of normalizable dilaton hair from entropy.

Conclusion: The integrable Toda structure enables systematic construction of magnetically charged black branes in AdS. Dilaton coupling breaks strict conformal thermodynamics but preserves the first law; the effective spatial dimension governing entropy–temperature scaling is renormalized by the square of the dilaton coupling, revealing a precise holographic signature of scalar hair.

Abstract: We show that the equations of motion for magnetically charged $p$-branes in $(n+p+2)$-dimensional AdS reduce to coupled one-dimensional Toda-type equations, and use this structure to construct static toroidal black branes with $T^n$ symmetry and $p$ translation-invariant directions. In the pure-Maxwell case we obtain an exact $\sinh$ solution--a $(p+2)$-dimensional black brane times an $n$-torus, asymptotically $\mathrm{AdS}_{p+2}\times T^n$--whose dilatonic analogue, with a scalar coupled through $e^{aφ}$, has a running torus modulus and a non-trivial dilaton. A perturbative expansion reduces the linearized system to a pair of Pöschl-Teller equations, one elementary and one solved by associated Legendre functions of non-integer degree, so that the general solution is non-elementary; at weak coupling $ε=a^2$ we construct the $O(a^2)$ backreaction explicitly. From the surface gravity, horizon area, and holographic renormalization we obtain the Hawking temperature, entropy density, and mass density, verifying the first law $d\mathcal{M}=T_H\,ds$ and the traceless conformal stress tensor of the dual $(p+1)$-dimensional theory. At $O(a^2)$ the first law persists, but the dilaton source shifts the Stefan-Boltzmann exponent to $p_{\rm eff}=p+a^2/2$, breaking the clean power law $s\propto T_H^p$ while the normalizable dilaton hair decouples from the entropy.

</details>


### [93] [Homogeneous Non Symmetric Special Kähler Geometries as Broken Isometry Metrics on Symmetric CV {K}ähler Manifolds:a new tool for $r=2$ CaNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23395)
*Pietro Fré,Mario Trigiante,Antoine Van Proeyen*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a detailed Lie-algebraic isomorphism between two solvable groups underlying the Calabi-Vesentini symmetric space SO(2,2+p)/SO(2)×SO(2+p) and the homogeneous non-symmetric special Kähler manifold L(−1,p), showing they share the same solvable Lie algebra equipped with two distinct quadratic forms; it identifies the full isometry group Iso_L(−1,p) as a non-semisimple subgroup of SO(2,2+p), describes L(−1,p) as a coset Iso_L(−1,p)/[U(1)_L × SO(p)], and highlights implications for enhancing expressivity in Cartan Neural Networks via new nonlinear transformations rooted in this geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the geometric and algebraic relationship between the symmetric Calabi-Vesentini manifold and the non-symmetric special Kähler manifold L(−1,p), and to exploit this correspondence for applications in geometric deep learning—specifically, enriching Cartan Neural Networks with additional nonlinearities derived from solvable group actions.

Method: Rigorous Lie-theoretic analysis: explicit construction and comparison of the solvable Lie algebras underlying both manifolds; identification of their respective quadratic forms (defining the Kähler metrics); embedding analysis of Iso_L(−1,p) into SO(2,2+p); coset realization of L(−1,p); and derivation of the induced action on solvable coordinates. Uses Alekseevsky’s classification framework for homogeneous Kähler manifolds.

Result: Full isomorphism between solvable groups S_{2,2+p} and S_{L(−1,p)} is proven; Iso_L(−1,p) is shown to be a non-semisimple subgroup of SO(2,2+p), with isotropy H = U(1)_L × SO(p) where U(1)_L lies outside the canonical so(2) ⊕ so(2+p) subalgebra; L(−1,p) is realized as the coset Iso_L(−1,p)/H; the solvable coordinate action is explicitly constructed; and the correspondence is framed as a tool to augment Cartan Neural Networks with new nonlinear transformations.

Conclusion: The special Kähler manifold L(−1,p) is not just metrically distinct but algebraically embedded within the Calabi-Vesentini framework via a shared solvable Lie algebra structure—enabling both deeper geometric understanding and practical architectural enhancements in geometric neural networks.

Abstract: In this paper we prove in full detail the isomorphism between the solvable group $\mathcal{S}_{2,2+p}$, metric equivalent to the Calabi Vesentini symmetric space $\mathrm{SO(2,2+p)/SO(2)\times SO(2+p)}$, and the solvable group $\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{L}(-1,p)}$ supporting the Kähler metric of the homogeneous non symmetric special manifold L$(-1,p)$. From an Alekseevskyan point of view the two spaces simply correspond to two different quadratic forms on the same solvable Lie algebra that we present and compare in detail. Furthermore considering the full group of isometries $\mathrm{Iso}_{\mathrm{L}(-1,p)}$ of L$(-1,p)$ we show that $\mathrm{Iso_{L(-1,p)}}\subset \mathrm{SO}(2,2+p)$ is a non-semisimple subgroup of the simple isometry group of Calabi-Vesentini manifolds. Altogether the Special Kähler manifold L$(-1,p)$ can be seen as a coset manifold $\mathrm{Iso_{L(-1,p)}}/\mathrm{H}$ where $\mathrm{H}=\mathrm{U(1)}_L\times \mathrm{SO(p)}$, the generator of $\mathrm{U(1)}_L$ being in $\so(2,2+p)$, yet not in the canonical $\so(2)\oplus\so(2+p)$ subalgebra. The action of $\mathrm{Iso}_{\mathrm{L}(-1,p)}$ on the solvable coordinates of the CV manifold can be constructed directly. This identification provides a valuable tool for Cartan Neural Networks, introducing additional non linear transformations in every map from one layer to the next one of an $r=2$ Neural Network based on the CV Tits Satake universality class; in perspective, this new tool increases expressivity.

</details>


### [94] [Spin density from second order transport in fluids](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23399)
*Domingo Gallegos,Roi Klein,Amos Yarom*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates spin hydrodynamics in charged conformal fluids in 3+1 dimensions, linking spin density to second-order transport coefficients and anomaly-induced quadratic terms, and validates the relation via free and holographic model computations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how spin degrees of freedom manifest hydrodynamically in charged conformal fluids, particularly the role of quantum anomalies in determining spin density beyond standard transport.

Method: Theoretical analysis within relativistic hydrodynamics and conformal field theory; derivation of spin density from symmetry constraints and anomaly inflow; explicit computation in free-field and holographic (AdS/CFT) models.

Result: Spin density is expressed as a linear combination of standard second-order transport coefficients plus an additional term quadratic in gauge and mixed gauge-gravitational anomaly coefficients; consistency confirmed across free and strongly coupled holographic examples.

Conclusion: Spin density in 3+1D conformal charged fluids is constrained by anomalies at second order in derivatives, revealing a universal anomaly-induced contribution not captured by conventional hydrodynamic expansion.

Abstract: We study the hydrodynamics of a charged fluid with spin degrees of freedom. We argue that, in a conformal field theory in 3+1 dimensions, the spin density is related to a linear combination of standard second-order transport coefficients, and an additional term quadratic in the gauge- and mixed gauge-gravitational anomaly coefficients. We compute the spin density in several free and holographic theories and compare the results.

</details>


### [95] [Gauge Origami of the Conifold $\times$ $\mathbb{C}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23556)
*Taro Kimura,Go Noshita*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs gauge-origami systems on the toric Calabi–Yau fourfold C×C using brane brick models, unifying magnificent-four, tetrahedron-instanton, and spiked-instanton sectors via framed quiver quantum mechanics; partition functions are defined via Jeffrey–Kirwan residues and fixed points described by colored BPS crystals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a unified framework for gauge origami on toric Calabi–Yau fourfolds, extending beyond known instanton sectors and enabling systematic construction from brane configurations.

Method: Constructs gauge-origami systems from brane brick models of C×C; uses brick matchings and phase boundaries to define D6-/D4-brane framings; defines partition functions via Jeffrey–Kirwan residues; identifies fixed points with colored BPS crystals; composes elementary sectors via shared fractional D0-brane quiver.

Result: A unified framed quiver quantum mechanical description encompassing magnificent-four, tetrahedron-instanton, and spiked-instanton sectors; explicit residue-based partition functions; colored BPS crystal characterization of fixed points; general gauge-origami configurations built from elementary sectors.

Conclusion: This work establishes a foundational, geometrically grounded construction of gauge origami on C×C, providing a blueprint for generalization to arbitrary toric Calabi–Yau fourfolds.

Abstract: We construct gauge-origami systems on the toric Calabi--Yau fourfold $\mathcal{C}\times\mathbb{C}$ from its brane brick model. Brick matchings and phase boundaries determine elementary D6- and D4-brane framings, respectively, realizing the magnificent-four, tetrahedron-instanton, and spiked-instanton sectors in a unified framed quiver quantum mechanics. We define their partition functions by Jeffrey--Kirwan residues and describe the fixed points in terms of colored BPS crystals. General gauge-origami configurations are obtained by combining these elementary sectors through their common fractional D0-brane quiver. This construction provides a first step toward gauge origami on general toric Calabi--Yau fourfolds.

</details>


### [96] [Where's Kasner? OPE Signature of Kasner Exponents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23776)
*Nejc Čeplak,Samuel Valach*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a direct link between the power-law exponent of complex-time singularities in holographic thermal two-point functions and the Kasner exponent characterizing the time direction near the singularity of certain black brane geometries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extract information about the black hole interior and singularity structure from the analytic properties of boundary thermal correlators in the large-N holographic limit, specifically via complex-time singularities related to bouncing geodesics.

Method: Analyzes the asymptotic behavior of OPE data in scalar two-point functions for states dual to black branes with blackening factors that are finite-order polynomials in inverse radial coordinate; identifies and relates the exponent of resulting 'bouncing' singularities to the Kasner exponent.

Result: A direct relation is found between the power-law exponent of complex-time singularities (from bouncing geodesics) and the Kasner exponent governing the time-direction scaling near the black hole singularity for this specific class of geometries.

Conclusion: The analytic structure of thermal correlators encodes precise geometric data about the black hole interior singularity, validating and refining the geodesic-singularity correspondence for a tractable class of black brane solutions.

Abstract: At large $N$, the analytic structure of holographic thermal correlation functions contains non-trivial information about the near-singularity region of dual black holes. In particular, complex-time singularities can be related to geodesics that probe the black hole interior and bounce off the singularity. We analyse the asymptotic behaviour of holographic OPE data entering scalar two-point functions in states dual to black branes whose blackening factors truncate at finite order in inverse powers of the radial coordinate. For this class of geometries, we find a direct relation between the power-law exponent of these bouncing singularities and the Kasner exponent associated with the time direction near the black hole singularity. We discuss the regime of validity of this relation and comment on possible extensions to the transverse spatial directions.

</details>


### [97] [Projective geometry of second-order supersymmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23938)
*Stevan Cebrian,Mikhail S. Plyushchay*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reformulates second-order supersymmetric quantum mechanics using projective geometry, linking SUSY structures (partner Hamiltonians, supercharges, central parameters) to a projective connection and an invariant vector field; it connects geometric data to quantum smoothness conditions, Schwarzian corrections, dimensional reduction, coupling-constant metamorphosis, and reveals deep ties to integrable structures (Lax–Novikov, Gelfand–Dikii–Virasoro, Bol operators) via the one-gap Lamé system.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover the underlying geometric structure of anomaly-free second-order supersymmetry in quantum mechanics, moving beyond algebraic formulations toward an intrinsic, coordinate-independent description that clarifies the origin of quantum corrections, singlet energies, and smoothness across superpotential zeros.

Method: Uses projective differential geometry: constructs a projective connection and a projective-vector-field preserving it; fixes physical coordinates and energy origin to reconstruct partner Hamiltonians and supercharges; analyzes fixed points of the vector field for operator regularity; identifies affine data selected by a classical similarity transformation to generate the Schwarzian quantum correction; relates geometry to dimensional reduction of Riemannian models and coupling-constant metamorphosis; applies the framework to the one-gap Lamé potential to expose links with Gelfand–Dikii–Virasoro algebras and Bol operators.

Result: Establishes a complete projective-geometric characterization of second-order SUSY QM: (i) partner Hamiltonians and supercharges are fully encoded in a projective connection + invariant vector field; (ii) the field’s quadratic invariant determines the central parameter and candidate singlet energies; (iii) fixed-point analysis yields necessary and sufficient criteria for smooth quantum operators at superpotential zeros; (iv) the classical similarity transformation selects affine data whose covariant derivatives yield the Schwarzian correction; (v) dimensional reduction and coupling-constant metamorphosis emerge naturally from this geometry; (vi) for the one-gap Lamé system, the Lax–Novikov integral is shown to act as a first-order projective stabilizer, reducing in the reflectionless limit to the third Bol operator in physical coordinates.

Conclusion: Second-order anomaly-free SUSY in quantum mechanics is fundamentally projective-geometric: its entire structure—including quantum corrections, spectral features, and integrability—arises from the interplay of a projective connection and a symmetry-preserving vector field. This unifies seemingly disparate concepts (Schwarzian, metamorphosis, Lax integrals, Bol operators) under a single geometric framework, suggesting deeper links between supersymmetry, projective geometry, and integrable systems.

Abstract: We show that anomaly-free second-order supersymmetry (SUSY) in quantum mechanics admits a projective-geometric formulation in terms of a projective connection and a vector field preserving it. Once the physical coordinate and energy origin are fixed, these data determine the partner Hamiltonians and supercharges; the field's quadratic invariant fixes the central parameter and candidate singlet energies. The fixed-point structure of this field yields explicit criteria for smooth continuation of the quantum operators through superpotential zeros. The classically fictitious similarity transformation selects affine data whose covariant factors generate the Schwarzian quantum correction. Dimensional reduction of a Riemannian model reproduces these affine data through the volume measure, while a coordinate adapted to the selected field implements coupling-constant metamorphosis. A one-gap Lame pair illustrates the relation between the SUSY auxiliary geometry and the Gelfand-Dikii-Virasoro structure governing physical solution products. Its Lax-Novikov integral acts on Schrodinger solutions through the first-order action of a projective stabilizer; in the reflectionless limit it becomes proportional, in the physical coordinate, to the auxiliary third Bol operator.

</details>


### [98] [Cassini Ansatz in the Skyrme Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23991)
*N. V. Gerasimeniuk,A. A. Korneev,A. V. Molochkov,O. G. Tkachev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies spherical symmetry stretching in the Skyrme model using a 3D generalization of Cassini ovals as an ansatz, finding a linear static energy dependence on longitudinal deformation with a string tension σ ≈ 0.14 GeV², consistent with confinement models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the connection between the Skyrme model and quark confinement by investigating how static energy responds to deformation, particularly whether it exhibits string-like behavior analogous to confining flux tubes.

Method: Uses a spherically symmetric Skyrme field configuration extended via a three-dimensional generalization of Cassini ovals as a trial (ansatz) configuration; computes static energy as a function of longitudinal deformation parameter.

Result: Static energy scales linearly with the longitudinal deformation parameter, yielding a slope (effective string tension) σ ≈ 0.14 GeV², matching values from established confinement models.

Conclusion: The linear energy–deformation relation supports an effective string picture in the Skyrme model, suggesting its potential relevance for modeling confinement-related phenomena in low-energy QCD.

Abstract: We investigate the stretching of a spherically symmetric solution in the Skyrme model. The three-dimensional generalization of the Cassini ovals is considered as ansatz of extension. The linear dependence of static energy on the longitudinal deformation parameter is obtained with a slope $σ\approx 0.14\ \mathrm{ GeV}^2$, which is in good agreement with the string tension known from various models of confinement.

</details>


### [99] [Critical Kasner scaling from Cauchy-horizon collapse near holographic phase transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24227)
*Ling-Long Gao,Yan Liu,Hong-Da Lyu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes scalar-hair bifurcations from nonextremal black holes with Cauchy horizons, showing that the logarithmic divergence of the critical zero-mode at the Cauchy horizon determines the exponential collapse rate of the Einstein-Rosen bridge and governs near-critical interior dynamics; it verifies Kasner scaling predictions numerically for RN-AdS₄ branes and distinguishes high-temperature (zero-mode–controlled) versus low-temperature (nonlinear IR–dominated) scaling regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how continuous scalar-hair transitions from stationary black holes encode universal interior dynamics—particularly Einstein-Rosen bridge collapse and Kasner asymptotics—via linear critical data at the Cauchy horizon, bypassing full nonlinear evolution.

Method: Analytic perturbation theory near the Cauchy horizon, zero-mode matching across horizons, critical exponent derivation from logarithmic branch structure, and fully nonlinear numerical relativity simulations for RN-AdS₄ black branes and BTZ generalizations.

Result: The logarithmic coefficient of the critical scalar zero-mode fixes both the exponential bridge collapse rate and Kasner exponents via β-scaling; zero-mode transmission fully determines critical exponents/amplitudes in finite-temperature bifurcations; at low temperature, identical linear data lead to distinct IR endpoints—power-law vs. inverse-logarithmic Kasner scaling—revealing a breakdown of zero-mode universality.

Conclusion: Near-Cauchy-horizon linear data (especially logarithmic zero-mode structure) govern universal critical interior behavior for finite-temperature hairy bifurcations, but low-temperature scaling is sensitive to nonlinear infrared completion—highlighting a dichotomy between linearly controlled criticality and nonlinear IR selection.

Abstract: We study continuous scalar-hair bifurcations from stationary black holes with a nonextremal Cauchy horizon. Although the exterior deformation vanishes at criticality, the critical zero mode generically develops a logarithmic branch at the reference Cauchy horizon, producing a nonuniform critical limit. We show that the coefficient of this logarithm in the scalar perturbation, together with local Cauchy-horizon data, determines the exponential collapse rate of the Einstein-Rosen bridge. If the post-collapse geometry evolves into a Kasner regime, the boundary order-parameter scaling $O\propto|λ-λ_c|^β$ further implies $
  1-p_t\propto|λ-λ_c|^{2β}. $ Crucially, zero-mode matching fixes both the critical exponents and the associated amplitudes, without any fit to the nonlinear interior. We illustrate this mechanism in the transition from rotating BTZ to hairy rotating black holes. For scalarized RN-AdS$_4$ black branes, we quantitatively verify the predicted Kasner scaling using fully nonlinear numerical solutions. At low temperature, two charged models with identical linear critical data instead approach different infrared endpoints, yielding respectively power-law and inverse-logarithmic Kasner scaling. Thus interiors near a finite-temperature bifurcation are controlled by zero-mode transmission, whereas low temperature scaling is governed by the nonlinear infrared completion.

</details>


### [100] [Saddle-Order Universality in Ensemble-Averaged Black Hole Thermodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24344)
*Ankit Anand,Peng Cheng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a saddle-order proposition for ensemble-averaged generalized free energy in reduced Euclidean black hole ensembles, showing that critical fluctuations are governed by quartic soft modes—yielding quarter-temperature scaling instead of Gaussian half-temperature behavior—and verifies it analytically for RN-AdS and Kerr-AdS black holes, unifying their thermodynamic behavior via a quartic normal form.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand non-Gaussian fluctuation behavior near critical points in reduced black hole ensembles, where standard saddle-point (Gaussian) approximations break down due to vanishing quadratic terms in the free energy expansion.

Method: Analytical saddle-point analysis of the ensemble-averaged generalized free energy; derivation of a saddle-order proposition based on regularity and stability assumptions; mapping of RN-AdS and Kerr-AdS thermodynamics to a quartic normal form; scaling analysis across critical and Gaussian regimes.
