Result: Critical fluctuations scale as T^{1/4} (quarter-temperature), not T^{1/2}; explicit verification for 4D RN-AdS and Kerr-AdS; identification of quartic soft mode as universal leading stabilizer; extension to weighted-homogeneous coupled collective modes.

Conclusion: The saddle-order proposition provides a unified framework for reduced-ensemble fluctuations in black hole thermodynamics, linking criticality, normal forms, and non-Gaussian scaling—generalizing beyond mean-field Gaussian expectations.

Abstract: We establish a saddle-order proposition for the ensemble-averaged generalized free energy in reduced Euclidean black hole ensembles. Under regularity and stability assumptions, the leading fluctuation contribution is determined by the order of the first stabilizing term around the dominant saddle. An ordinary critical point is governed by a quartic soft mode, giving a quarter-temperature contribution instead of the familiar half-temperature Gaussian result. We verify this analytically for four-dimensional RN-AdS and Kerr-AdS black holes and map their thermodynamic controls to a common quartic normal form, whose scaling function connects the critical and Gaussian regimes along the zero-field one-well direction. The proposition also extends to coupled collective modes with weighted-homogeneous leading potentials. These results provide a unified description of reduced-ensemble fluctuations through black hole criticality.

</details>


### [101] [Miwa deformation of WLZZ matrix models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24360)
*T. Diudin,A. Popolitov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces a universal definition of multivariate orthogonal polynomials for the positive branch of WLZZ matrix models, extending strict superintegrability and Miwa deformation beyond the Gaussian case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the strict superintegrability property and Miwa deformation formulas from the Gaussian case to broader settings in WLZZ matrix models.

Method: Proposes a universal construction of multivariate orthogonal polynomials tailored to the positive branch of WLZZ matrix models.

Result: Successful generalization of strict superintegrability and Miwa deformation formulas beyond the Gaussian case.

Conclusion: The proposed framework provides a robust, model-independent approach to multivariate orthogonal polynomials in WLZZ matrix models, enabling broader applicability of integrability structures.

Abstract: In this paper we propose a universal way to define multivariate orthogonal polynomials for the positive branch of WLZZ matrix models. This allows us to generalize from the Gaussian case, previously considered in the literature, the strict superintegrability property and the Miwa deformation formulas.

</details>


### [102] [On the (In)equivalence of de Sitter and Holographic Entanglement Entropies in Lovelock Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24557)
*Takamasa Kanai*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper examines the equivalence between de Sitter entropy and holographic entanglement entropy in higher-curvature gravity (Gauss-Bonnet and general Lovelock) within RS II braneworld models, finding exact agreement for static asymptotically de Sitter (and black hole) spacetimes, but a mismatch in stationary cases due to extrinsic curvature contributions on constant-time slices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the relation between gravitational (de Sitter/black hole) entropy and holographic entanglement entropy in higher-curvature gravity and its braneworld realizations, especially under generalized entropy functionals beyond Einstein gravity.

Method: Canonical formulation of gravitational action and Euclidean path integral for de Sitter entropy; comparison with holographic entanglement entropy computed from Wald–Iyer-type entropy functionals (e.g., Jacobson–Myers for Gauss-Bonnet, general Lovelock functional); analysis restricted to static vs. stationary asymptotically de Sitter RS II braneworlds and braneworld black holes.

Result: De Sitter entropy matches holographic entanglement entropy exactly for static asymptotically de Sitter RS II braneworlds in Gauss-Bonnet and general Lovelock gravity; discrepancy arises in stationary cases due to nonzero extrinsic curvature on constant-time hypersurfaces; same static/stationary dichotomy holds for braneworld black hole entropy vs. holographic entanglement entropy.

Conclusion: The equivalence between gravitational and holographic entropies in higher-curvature braneworlds is conditional on spacetime symmetry—specifically, staticity—and reflects the role of boundary geometry in entropy prescriptions; this refines the holographic dictionary in Lovelock theories and supports geometric consistency of entropy functionals in warped compactifications.

Abstract: We investigate the relation between de Sitter entropy and holographic entanglement entropy in higher-curvature gravity and its braneworld realization. Using the canonical formulation of the gravitational action and the Euclidean gravitational path integral, we derive the de Sitter entropy, including contributions from higher-curvature surface terms, and compare it with the holographic entanglement entropy obtained from the corresponding entropy functional.
  We first consider Gauss-Bonnet gravity and show that the two entropies coincide for static asymptotically de Sitter braneworld spacetimes on the RS II model, while they generally differ for stationary spacetimes. The mismatch in the stationary case arises from an extrinsic-curvature contribution associated with the constant-time surface, which vanishes for static configurations but is generally nonzero for stationary geometries.
  We then extend the analysis to general Lovelock gravity and show that the agreement between the de Sitter entropy and holographic entanglement entropy persists for static asymptotically de Sitter braneworld spacetimes in the RS II model beyond the Gauss-Bonnet case. We also comment that the same distinction between static and stationary configurations applies to braneworld black holes: the black hole entropy agrees with the holographic entanglement entropy in the static case, while the two generally differ for stationary braneworld black holes.
  Our results clarify the relation between gravitational and holographic entropies in higher-curvature gravity and provide a broader perspective on holographic correspondence in Lovelock theories and their braneworld realizations.

</details>


### [103] [The Gaiotto-Kim Trace Formula and Line Defects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24582)
*Sungjoon Kim,Spencer Stubbs*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how 4d half-BPS line defects embed into the Gaiotto–Kim trace formula for 3d TQFT partition functions, yielding a homomorphism to 3d Wilson loops that preserves fusion; it provides an algorithm to identify simple TQFT objects as UV Wilson loops in abelian Chern–Simons–matter theories and computes modular $S$- and $T$-matrices via supersymmetric localization, applied to $(A_1,G)$ Argyres–Douglas theories with higher-power monodromy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the correspondence between 4d $\mathcal{N}=2$ SCFT line defects and 3d TQFT observables—specifically how half-BPS line defects map to Wilson loops—and to systematically reconstruct the categorical data (simple objects, fusion, modular matrices) of the IR 3d TQFT from UV abelian Chern-Simons-matter descriptions.

Method: Uses the Gaiotto–Kim trace formula for the ellipsoid partition function of the 3d TQFT obtained by compactifying a 4d $\mathcal{N}=2$ SCFT on $S^1$; inserts 4d half-BPS line defects, analyzes their image under the trace map; applies supersymmetric localization to compute Wilson loop expectation values and derive $S$- and $T$-matrices; develops an algorithm to identify simple objects as gauge-invariant Wilson loops in the UV abelian Chern–Simons–matter theory.

Result: Establishes a defect-to-Wilson-loop homomorphism preserving fusion algebra; provides a constructive algorithm to enumerate simple objects of the 3d TQFT as specific Wilson loops in the UV theory; explicitly computes modular $S$- and $T$-matrices for TQFTs arising from $(A_1,G)$ Argyres–Douglas theories with higher-power monodromy.

Conclusion: The 3d TQFT data—including its modular tensor category structure—is fully encoded in the UV abelian Chern–Simons–matter description and the insertion of 4d line defects; the trace formula serves as a precise bridge linking 4d defect operator algebras to 3d topological observables.

Abstract: We study the insertion of 4d half-BPS line defects into the trace formula proposed by Gaiotto and Kim, which computes the ellipsoid partition function of the 3d topological quantum field theory (TQFT) arising from a 4d $\mathcal{N}=2$ superconformal field theory. This yields a homomorphism to 3d Wilson loops, preserving the fusion algebra of the 4d defects. We also present a systematic algorithm for determining the simple objects of a 3d TQFT in terms of Wilson loops in the UV 3d $\mathcal{N}=2$ abelian Chern-Simons-matter theory. Utilizing supersymmetric localization, we characterize the modular $S$- and $T$-matrices of the TQFT. We apply our algorithm to various 3d TQFTs arising from the trace formula with higher-power monodromy of the $(A_1,G)$ Argyres-Douglas theories.

</details>


### [104] [ABJM to BMN in a Double Scale Limit: Indices and Bubbling Geometries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24649)
*Zhengyuan Du*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a precise double-scaling limit of ABJM theory that maps its monopole-charge-q sector to the rank-q BMN matrix model, with coupling controlled by ν = N/k²; the mapping is confirmed via both Penrose geometry limits of half-BPS supergravity solutions and exact factorization of the supersymmetric index.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify and rigorously derive the effective low-energy quantum mechanical description—specifically the BMN matrix model—for fixed monopole charge sectors of ABJM theory in a controlled double-scaling limit where both N and k are large but their ratio N/k² remains finite.

Method: Combines gravitational and field-theoretic techniques: (i) takes a double-scaling limit (N,k→∞, N/k²→ν) of Hopf-quotiented Donos–Simón half-BPS geometries to obtain Lin–Maldacena electrostatic configurations dual to BMN vacua; (ii) performs asymptotic analysis of the ABJM supersymmetric index using the exact localization formula, proving coefficientwise factorization into neutral (q=0) and BMN-refined-index parts weighted by longitudinal momentum.

Result: The monopole-charge-q sector of ABJM theory in the double-scaling limit is exactly described by the rank-q BMN matrix model with dimensionless coupling ν; quantized fluxes in the gravity dual map bijectively to BMN vacuum labels (integer partitions), and the ABJM index factorizes as Index_{ABJM}^{(q)} = Index_{neutral} × (z^q × Index_{BMN}^{(q)}), where z tracks longitudinal momentum.

Conclusion: The rank-q BMN matrix model emerges universally as the holographic quantum mechanical dual of the q-monopole sector of ABJM theory in the N,k→∞, N/k²→ν limit; this provides a nontrivial, exact, and index-based confirmation of the BMN correspondence beyond the plane-wave limit.

Abstract: We study a double scale limit of $U(N)_k\times U(N)_{-k}$ ABJM theory, with $N,k\to\infty$ and $N/k^2\toν\in(0,\infty)$, at fixed positive total monopole charge $q$. In this limit, the Penrose geometry retains a compact null circle of finite radius, and the sector of monopole charge $q$ carries $q$ units of longitudinal momentum. It is therefore naturally associated with the rank-$q$ BMN matrix model, while $ν$ fixes its dimensionless coupling. We establish this relation from both gravity and the supersymmetric index. On the gravity side, we take the double scale limit of Hopf quotients of the Donos--Simón half-BPS geometries. The disk carrying the growing background flux becomes an infinite conducting plane, while the remaining finite disks reproduce the Lin--Maldacena electrostatic problem. Their quantized fluxes map directly to the partition data labeling BMN vacua. On the field-theory side, starting from the finite-$N$, finite-$k$ ABJM localization formula, we prove that, at fixed positive monopole charge $q$, the ABJM superconformal index factorizes coefficientwise in the transverse fugacities into a universal neutral contribution and the refined Witten index of the rank-$q$ BMN matrix model, together with the longitudinal momentum weight. The neutral contribution is precisely the limiting zero-monopole-charge index. Dividing by this universal factor therefore isolates the BMN index summed over all its supersymmetric vacua.

</details>


### [105] [Finite-Time Doppler Covariance Spectroscopy in the AdS/CFT correspondence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24806)
*Feiyi Liu,Shiyang Chen,Yang Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a finite-duration covariance framework within AdS/CFT to quantitatively extract intrinsic chirality of a rotating BTZ black hole from boundary control measurements, using velocity-resonant quadrature cycles and phase-cancellation to isolate chiral spectral features with subpercent bias.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve and quantify intrinsic bath chirality—distinct from control-induced artifacts—in holographic systems, specifically in the context of rotating BTZ black holes under the AdS/CFT correspondence.

Method: Develops an exact finite-duration real-control covariance formalism on the compact boundary cylinder of rotating BTZ spacetime; exploits relative motion between boundary controls to induce discrete velocity resonances tied to scalar field angular momentum; applies two-setting quadrature (sum/difference frequency) phase cycling to cancel nonresonant contributions and reconstruct resonant pair responses; uses normalized contrast of opposite-velocity resonance signals to eliminate reflection-symmetric control factors and operator normalization; derives leakage bounds and analyzes linewidth bias for smooth spectra.

Result: Achieves quantitative estimation of BTZ rotation parameter with subpercent deterministic bias using only the first two nonzero angular modes; verifies phase-cycle reconstruction and quadratic linewidth scaling via long-pulse exact BTZ spectrum calculations; confirms convergence under bath-weighted truncated transfer amplitudes; establishes explicit real-projection leakage bound for mode contamination control.

Conclusion: The framework cleanly separates control-induced resonance kinematics from intrinsic bath chirality, enabling robust, finite-time covariance-based quantification of black hole rotation in holography.

Abstract: From a holographic perspective, we formulate an exact finite-duration covariance framework that resolves chirality in the AdS/CFT correspondence. In a rotating BTZ black hole spacetime, relative motion between two boundary controls converts sum-frequency matching on the compact boundary cylinder into discrete velocity resonances indexed by the angular momentum of the scalar field. Because the complete real-control covariance also contains difference-frequency sectors, a two-setting quadrature cycle cancels them and reconstructs the resonant pair contribution from complete positive-semidefinite covariance matrices. For an isolated angular mode, reconstructed pair responses at opposite resonance velocities sample the boundary spectrum at opposite angular momentum and a common control-selected frequency. Their normalized contrast cancels reflection-symmetric control factors and the state-independent operator normalization, yielding a finite-linewidth estimator of intrinsic bath chirality. For a smooth bath spectrum, the bias from common centered even narrowing windows begins at quadratic order in linewidth, extending the protection beyond Gaussian sampling. An explicit real-projection leakage bound controls contamination from neighboring modes. Long-pulse calculations with the exact BTZ spectrum verify phase-cycle reconstruction and quadratic linewidth scaling. Bath-weighted calculations with exact truncated transfer amplitudes establish convergence to this baseline. Under the default controls, the first two nonzero angular modes support extraction from the complete angular sum and reconstruct the BTZ rotation parameter with subpercent deterministic bias. The framework thus separates control-induced resonance kinematics from intrinsic bath chirality and converts finite-time covariance data into a quantitative estimate of the rotating BTZ black hole.

</details>


### [106] [The Generalized Uncertainty Principle. Massless QED Renormalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24817)
*Ezequiel Valero,Héctor Gisbert,Victor Ilisie*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper examines whether generalized uncertainty principle (GUP)-deformed quantum electrodynamics remains renormalizable at one loop; it finds that only one new physical counterterm—an axial-axial four-fermion interaction—is needed beyond the tree-level operators, establishing a minimal radiative completion and linking its running to Einstein-Cartan gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the operator content of GUP-deformed QED is closed under renormalization at one loop, addressing an open question in the effective-field-theory interpretation of GUP.

Method: Computes UV divergences of relevant Green functions in massless GUP-deformed QED at one loop and first order in the deformation parameter; identifies and eliminates redundant operators; determines minimal counterterm structure.

Result: Renormalization requires only one independent physical counterterm—an axial-axial four-fermion interaction—beyond the tree-level theory; derives leading-logarithmic running of its Wilson coefficient.

Conclusion: The GUP deformation admits a minimal radiative completion at one-loop order, and the induced contact interaction has operator-level correspondence with that arising in Einstein-Cartan gravity.

Abstract: The effective-field-theory interpretation of generalized uncertainty principle (GUP) deformations does not by itself establish whether their tree-level operator content is closed under renormalization. We investigate this question in massless GUP-deformed quantum electrodynamics at one loop and to first order in the deformation parameter. We compute the ultraviolet divergences of the relevant Green functions and determine the counterterm structure. Our central result is that, after eliminating redundant operators, renormalization requires only one independent physical counterterm beyond those associated with the tree-level theory: an axial-axial four-fermion interaction. The original deformation therefore admits a minimal radiative completion at this order. We derive the leading logarithmic running of the induced Wilson coefficient and discuss its operator-level correspondence with the contact interaction arising in Einstein-Cartan gravity.

</details>


### [107] [Quantum inversion of the vacuum hierarchy and multi-kink unbinding in the non-degenerate double sine-Gordon model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24861)
*Jonathan Lozano-Mayo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates quantum corrections to vacuum energies and multi-kink binding in the non-degenerate double sine-Gordon model, finding a quantum-induced vacuum hierarchy inversion confirmed by both one-loop analytic calculation and MPS numerics; the inversion crossing is quantitatively refined beyond one-loop via Gaussian effective potential, and multi-kink structural response (e.g., expansion and asymptotic unbinding) is validated against collective-coordinate predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve how quantum fluctuations alter the classical vacuum energy hierarchy and multi-kink stability in the non-degenerate double sine-Gordon model—particularly whether vacuum degeneracy lifting and kink binding are robust or reversed under quantum corrections.

Method: Combined analytical and numerical methods: (i) one-loop quantum correction calculation with normal ordering at the primary vacuum meson mass; (ii) matrix-product-state (MPS) simulations for n=4 on the lattice, with continuum extrapolation of bulk energy densities; (iii) Gaussian effective potential (GEP) for improved estimation of the vacuum energy crossing; (iv) finite-chain MPS analysis of Q=4 multi-kink spatial profile and mass scaling across the vacuum crossing.

Result: Quantum fluctuations invert the classical vacuum hierarchy—favoring the central secondary vacuum—occurring at a coupling α below the one-loop prediction (22% lower at α=0.3); MPS confirms inversion and its persistence in the continuum; GEP closely reproduces the corrected crossing; Q=4 multi-kink expands as vacuum energy difference decreases, and becomes asymptotically unbound beyond crossing, yet retains total mass correction <10% of classical value.

Conclusion: Quantum effects fundamentally restructure the vacuum landscape and topological soliton binding in this integrability-breaking deformation of the sine-Gordon model; the hierarchy inversion is robust, quantitatively refined beyond perturbation theory, and manifests in observable geometric and energetic signatures of multi-kinks—demonstrating interplay between vacuum structure, quantum corrections, and extended topological objects.

Abstract: We study the quantum corrections to the vacuum energies and to the binding of the multi-kinks of the non-degenerate double sine-Gordon model. With normal ordering defined at the meson mass of the primary vacuum, the one-loop calculation predicts an inversion of the classical vacuum hierarchy, in which quantum fluctuations favour the central secondary vacuum above a coupling set by the classical splitting of the vacua and their meson mass ratio. Matrix-product-state calculations for $n=4$ confirm the inversion, and extrapolation of the bulk energy densities in the lattice spacing supports its persistence in the continuum. The crossing lies below the one-loop prediction, by $22$ percent at $α=0.3$, and is reproduced closely by the Gaussian effective potential. Finite-chain calculations at $α=0.3$ show that the central region of the multi-kink of topological charge $Q=4$ expands as the difference of the vacuum energies falls, as a collective-coordinate description predicts, and that beyond the crossing it grows with the size of the system. The composite therefore approaches the loss of its binding with a correction to its total mass still under a tenth of the classical value.

</details>


### [108] [Measuring correlations using local and nonlocal quenches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24873)
*Alexey G. Mikhaylenko,Andrew G. Semenov*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Proposes a quantum quench-based method to measure correlation functions—including non-Gaussian ones—in scalar field theories via local/nonlocal perturbations and their imprint on post-quench field evolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a theoretically grounded, experimentally feasible protocol for measuring higher-order (especially non-Gaussian) correlation functions in quantum field systems, which are typically inaccessible via conventional linear-response techniques.

Method: Analyzes the dynamical response of massive and massless scalar QFTs to sudden quantum quenches—both local (e.g., point-like coupling) and nonlocal (e.g., spatially extended coupling)—and derives how the time evolution of the expectation value of the field operator encodes initial-state correlation functions.

Result: Demonstrates that local quenches probe equal-time two-point and higher even-point correlations, while nonlocal quenches access more general (including odd-point and unequal-time) correlations; explicitly shows the mapping between quench-induced field dynamics and initial n-point functions.

Conclusion: Quantum quenches serve as controllable probes for reconstructing arbitrary-order correlation functions in QFTs, enabling characterization of non-Gaussianity and providing a pathway toward tomography of quantum many-body states in field-theoretic settings.

Abstract: We present a theoretical proposal for measuring correlation functions in a quantum field system based on the use of quantum quenches. Using the examples of massive and massless scalar field theories, we show that a short-term perturbation of the system leads to a dependence of the further evolution of the average field on the initial state's correlation functions. Considering two types of perturbations, namely local and nonlocal quenches, we show which correlation functions can be measured in each of these cases. The proposed procedure can be applied to study non-Gaussian correlations.

</details>


### [109] [Gauss--Bonnet running and the de Sitter saddle of quadratic gravity inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24912)
*Ruolin Liu,Niayesh Afshordi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper shows that including the Gauss-Bonnet term in the one-loop renormalization group running of quadratic gravity restores a stationary de Sitter solution compatible with CMB data, yielding hilltop-like inflation with predictable ns and r, constrained by matter content and loop control.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile quadratic gravity inflation with the high scalar spectral tilt (ns ≈ 0.97–0.98) favored by CMB observations, while resolving the instability of the previously proposed Euclidean four-sphere initial state due to scheme dependence and omission of the Euler (Gauss–Bonnet) trace anomaly.

Method: Analyzes the full one-loop RG running of curvature-squared couplings—including the usually neglected Gauss–Bonnet coefficient—in quadratic gravity; identifies a unique coupling ratio where Euler-induced running balances R² running, yielding a stationary de Sitter state for both gravitational constraints and compact Euclidean action; computes scalaron potential, inflationary dynamics (e-folds, ns, r), and consistency bounds from tensor limits and perturbative control.

Result: A stationary de Sitter solution emerges with an extremely flat hilltop scalaron potential (m²/H² ≈ −5×10⁻¹⁰) smoothly joined to an inverse-linear plateau; last N* ≈ 50–60 e-folds are scheme-independent, predicting ns ≈ 1 − 4/(3N*) and r governed by matter field count; current r < 0.036 bound implies ≥ ~4×10⁶ conformal scalars or ≥ ~3×10⁵ vectors; r ≥ 0.008 and 0.973 ≤ ns ≤ 0.978 across viable parameter window.

Conclusion: Quadratic gravity with full one-loop running—including Gauss–Bonnet—provides a robust, predictive inflationary framework consistent with CMB data and perturbative control, requiring substantial but finite matter content, and testable via next-generation CMB B-mode experiments.

Abstract: Inflation driven purely by the quantum running of the curvature-squared couplings of quadratic gravity was shown to accommodate the high scalar tilt favored by recent cosmic microwave background observations, starting from a Euclidean four-sphere at a maximum of the running $R^2$ coupling. That maximum exists only in one renormalization scheme, and is not stationary once the Euler trace anomaly is included. We show that the standard one-loop running, with the usually neglected Gauss--Bonnet coefficient retained, recovers the missing de Sitter state: the Euler running balances the scale dependence of the $R^2$ term at a unique coupling ratio, giving a de Sitter solution that is stationary for the gravitational constraint and for the compact Euclidean action alike, and whose scalaron potential is an extremely flat hilltop ($m^2/H^2\simeq-5\times10^{-10}$) joined to an inverse-linear inflationary plateau. The last $N_*\simeq50$--$60$ $e$-folds are scheme independent, with scalar tilt $n_s\simeq1-4/(3N_*)$, while the tensor-to-scalar ratio, $r$, is set by the number of matter fields that enhance the running. The current tensor bound excludes pure gravity and sets a minimum matter content, some $4\times10^6$ conformally coupled scalars or $3\times10^5$ vectors---$2.6$ times fewer than the momentum-induced scheme requires---while one-loop control to the end of inflation sets a maximum about ten times higher. Across that window the model predicts $r\gtrsim0.008$ with $0.973\lesssim n_s\lesssim0.978$, within reach of upcoming CMB polarization surveys.

</details>


### [110] [Chiral Carroll string near Schwarzschild black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24939)
*Kedar S. Kolekar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Derives the chiral Carroll string action via Carroll expansion of the relativistic bosonic string action and studies its dynamics near a Schwarzschild black hole horizon, revealing chiral (left/right-moving) fluctuations and non-trivial Rindler motion dependent on horizon behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the ultra-relativistic (c → 0) limit of string theory in curved spacetimes, particularly near black hole horizons, by constructing and analyzing the chiral Carroll string as a geometric limit of the relativistic string.

Method: Performs a Carroll expansion (1/c expansion) of the closed bosonic string action to obtain the chiral Carroll string action in a curved string-Carroll target space; applies it to the near-horizon geometry of a 4D Schwarzschild black hole, interpreted as a string-Carroll spacetime, and analyzes classical string dynamics therein.

Result: The chiral Carroll string exhibits purely left- or right-moving fluctuations at the horizon; its bulk motion in the Rindler-like near-horizon region is fully determined by these chiral horizon modes.

Conclusion: The chiral Carroll string provides a consistent ultra-relativistic limit for strings in black hole backgrounds, with horizon degrees of freedom dictating bulk dynamics—suggesting a holographic structure in the Carrollian limit of string theory.

Abstract: We obtain the action for the chiral Carroll string moving in a curved string-Carroll target spacetime from a Carroll expansion, in powers of the speed of light $c$, of the action for the relativistic closed bosonic string. Applying this setup to a classical closed bosonic string approaching the $4$-dimensional Schwarzschild black hole, we study the dynamics of the string when it behaves as a chiral Carroll string with the near-horizon string-Carroll expanded geometry as the target spacetime. We find that the chiral Carroll string can have either left-moving or right-moving fluctuations on the horizon, while exhibiting a non-trivial motion in the Rindler spacetime which depends on these horizon fluctuations.

</details>


### [111] [Towards All-Order Locality in the Higher-Spin Holomorphic Zero-Form Sector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24956)
*D. A. Valerev*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes necessary and sufficient conditions for constructing space-time spin-local holomorphic zero-form vertices in Vasiliev's higher-spin theory, extending prior results by enforcing a stricter locality requirement and explicitly computing the spin-local η² vertex.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To ensure space-time spin-locality of interaction vertices in Vasiliev’s higher-spin gravity, beyond the weaker condition of spinor spin-locality previously used.

Method: Derives necessary and sufficient conditions on holomorphic one-form vertices that guarantee existence of holomorphic zero-form vertices satisfying a refined locality requirement; applies the scheme to compute the η² zero-form vertex explicitly.

Result: A complete characterization of holomorphic one-form vertices admitting spin-local holomorphic zero-form completions; explicit construction of the spin-local η² zero-form vertex, surpassing earlier spinor-local results.

Conclusion: The proposed locality requirement—combined with spinor spin-locality—is sufficient to ensure space-time spin-locality, enabling a systematic, all-order construction of local vertices in higher-spin theory.

Abstract: This work addresses the construction of local interaction vertices in the holomorphic zero-form sector obtained from Vasiliev equations. We focus on a requirement on zero-form vertices, previously proposed in a less restrictive form in arXiv:2208.02004, which, together with spinor spin-locality, guarantees space-time spin-locality. Necessary and sufficient conditions on the holomorphic one-form sector vertices are derived that ensure the existence of holomorphic zero-form vertices satisfying this requirement. As a concrete demonstration, the proposed scheme is applied to compute the spin-local holomorphic $η^2$ zero-form sector vertex, a result that goes beyond previous work where only spinor spin-locality was established. These findings lay the groundwork for a systematic all-order construction of local vertices in higher-spin theory.

</details>


### [112] [Spin-curvature effects in slowly rotating black hole spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24364)
*Arpita Jana,Subhajit Mazumdar,Sunandan Gangopadhyay*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how the spin of a massive spin-1/2 particle interacts with spacetime curvature near a slowly rotating black hole, deriving a quantum relativistic force from spin-curvature coupling using tetrads, WKB approximation, and Gordon decomposition; the resulting force includes a rotation-dependent correction that decays faster radially than the Schwarzschild counterpart.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how spacetime curvature directly affects quantum spin degrees of freedom in strong-field gravity, beyond geodesic motion—specifically, to quantify the spin-curvature interaction-induced deviation from geodesics for spin-1/2 particles in Kerr-like (slowly rotating) black hole backgrounds.

Method: Constructs co-moving orthonormal tetrads in the slowly rotating black hole (Kerr with a ≪ M) spacetime; incorporates spin connection into the Dirac equation; applies WKB approximation to separate amplitude and phase of the spinor; uses Gordon decomposition of the Dirac four-current to isolate the quantum mechanical force term arising from spin-curvature coupling; computes the force components up to linear order in the rotation parameter a.

Result: Derives an explicit expression for the relativistic quantum force due to spin-curvature interaction; shows that black hole rotation introduces an additional contribution to the force proportional to a, which decays as ∼1/r⁴ (faster than the ∼1/r³ Schwarzschild spin-curvature force), thereby modifying the radial dependence and magnitude of the deviation from geodesic motion.

Conclusion: Spin-curvature coupling generates a measurable quantum force on spin-1/2 particles even in weakly rotating black hole spacetimes; the rotational contribution is subdominant at large distances but enhances spin-dependent gravitational effects near the horizon, implying observable signatures in high-precision spin-polarized probes or analog quantum systems.

Abstract: We investigate the spin-curvature interaction for a massive spin-1/2 quantum particle in a slowly rotating black hole spacetime. In contrast to the flat Minkowski space, the curved spacetime introduces non-trivial spin connections into the Dirac equation due to the direct curvature-spin interaction. The force arising from this non-trivial spin-curvature coupling makes the particle deviate from its geodesics. We first calculate the tetrads in the co-moving frame of the particle in the slowly rotating black hole background. We then compute the components of the relativistic quantum force, arising from the spin-curvature interaction, using these co-moving tetrads up to linear order in the rotation parameter ($a$) of the black hole. The procedure of computing the force is based on the WKB approximation and the Gordon decomposition method for the Dirac probability four current. Our results show that the rotation parameter modifies the magnitude of the force by introducing a term that decays more rapidly with radial distance than the force magnitude in a static spherically symmetric Schwarzschild black hole spacetime.

</details>


### [113] [T-duality of string charge density/bit threads correspondence in type II supergravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24366)
*Houwen Wu,Shuxuan Ying*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper tests the bit-thread/charge-density correspondence under Abelian T-duality in Type II supergravity, showing entropy-preserving invariance of maximal flux for both fundamental string (F1) and wrapped D-brane (e.g., D1→D2) configurations across IIB↔IIA duality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the robustness of the bit-thread/charge-density correspondence — a geometric formulation of holographic entanglement entropy — under Abelian T-duality, and to extend it beyond fundamental strings to wrapped-brane currents.

Method: Applies Abelian T-duality along a compact circle to known supersymmetric backgrounds (F1-NS5-P and D1-D5-P), tracks how source currents (F1, D1) transform (e.g., F1→F1, D1→D2), constructs conserved radial bit-thread flows from projected charge densities or contracted bivectors, and computes dilaton-weighted transverse area densities and integrated maximal fluxes.

Result: Under T-duality: (i) the radial F1 source remains an F1, yielding a bit-thread flow whose maximal flux equals black hole entropy; (ii) the radial D1 becomes a D2 wrapped on the dual circle, and contraction with the dual circle’s normalized 1-form yields an effective radial conserved flow with same maximal flux; (iii) local fields (metric, dilaton, currents, flow norms) change, but dilaton-weighted transverse area density and integrated maximal flux are invariant.

Conclusion: The bit-thread/charge-density correspondence is T-duality invariant, confirming its consistency in string theory; moreover, it generalizes naturally to wrapped-brane currents that induce string-like radial flows, broadening its applicability beyond fundamental strings.

Abstract: In this paper, we study Abelian T-duality of the string charge density/bit threads correspondence in ten-dimensional Type II supergravity. T-duality along the common compact circle maps the Type IIB F1-NS5-P background to a Type IIA P-NS5-F1 background with the winding and momentum charges exchanged. The radial F1 source remains an F1 source, and its projected charge density gives a bit-thread flow whose maximal flux reproduces the black hole entropy. The same duality maps D1-D5-P to D0-D4-F1. In this case, a radial D1 source becomes a D2 source wrapped on the dual circle. Contracting the resulting spatial bivector with the normalized closed one-form on that circle gives a conserved effective radial flow whose maximal flux again reproduces the entropy. Although the local metrics, dilatons, source currents, and flow norms change, the dilaton-weighted transverse area density and the integrated maximal flux are invariant. These results provide a nontrivial T-duality test of the correspondence and extend it from string currents to wrapped-brane currents that reduce to string-like flows.

</details>
