Zalambani,J. A. Zamora Saa,F. Zangari,M. Zavertyaev,M. Zdybal,F. Zenesini,C. Zeng,M. Zeng,S. H Zeng,C. Zhang,C. Zhang,D. Zhang,J. Zhang,L. Zhang,Q. Z. Zhang,R. Zhang,S. Zhang,S. L. Zhang,Y. Zhang,Z. Zhang,J. Zhao,M. Zhao,Y. Zhao,A. Zhelezov,S. Z. Zheng,X. Z. Zheng,Y. Zheng,T. Zhou,X. Zhou,V. Zhovkovska,L. Z. Zhu,X. Zhu,X. Zhu,Y. Zhu,V. Zhukov,J. Zhuo,T. Zies,D. Zuliani,X. Zuo*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First measurement of transverse helicity fractions of W bosons in the forward region (0 ≤ y_W ≤ 5) at √s = 13 TeV using LHCb, with 5.1 fb⁻¹ data; results show strong rapidity dependence and agree with NLO SM predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe electroweak dynamics and test Standard Model predictions—particularly helicity structure of W bosons—in the forward region, where QCD and parton distribution effects are enhanced and poorly constrained.

Method: Extracted transverse helicity fractions from a template fit to muon p_T and pseudorapidity distributions in W → μν_μ decays, using pp collision data at √s = 13 TeV collected by LHCb (integrated luminosity = 5.1 fb⁻¹).

Result: Observed strong rapidity dependence of transverse helicity fractions across 0 ≤ y_W ≤ 5; measurements are consistent with next-to-leading-order (NLO) Standard Model predictions.

Conclusion: This constitutes the first determination of W boson transverse helicity fractions in the forward region; validates NLO SM calculations in a kinematic regime sensitive to PDFs and QCD evolution, and establishes LHCb’s capability for precision electroweak studies beyond central rapidities.

Abstract: The transverse helicity fractions of $W$ bosons are measured as a function of the $W$ boson rapidity, $y_{W}$, in the range $0 \leq y_{W} \leq 5$ using $W\toμν_μ$ decays in $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV recorded by the LHCb experiment and corresponding to an integrated luminosity of $5.1$ fb$^{-1}$. The fractions are extracted from a template fit to the muon transverse momentum and pseudorapidity. The results show a strong rapidity dependence and agree with next-to-leading-order Standard Model predictions, providing the first determination of the transverse helicity fractions of $W$ bosons in the forward region.

</details>


### [115] [Benchmarking Nucleon Production in Hadron Interactions Relevant for GeV-scale Neutrino Experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22662)
*Richard Diurba,Steve Dytman,Matthew King,Yinrui Liu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how hadron-nucleus scattering models in neutrino event generators (e.g., GENIE with Bertini Cascade and INCL++) predict nucleon production from common final-state hadrons (nucleons, pions) in neutrino-nucleus interactions; it finds simple parametric forms (Gaussian/exponential) for nucleon multiplicity in mesonless interactions and provides reweighting tools to mitigate model uncertainties in visible energy and nucleon counts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Neutrino experiments depend on accurate modeling of intranuclear hadronic cascades to reconstruct neutrino energy and identify interaction channels; discrepancies among hadron-nucleus scattering models introduce systematic uncertainties in final-state observables like nucleon multiplicity and visible energy.

Method: Systematic comparison of nucleon production from mesonless hadron–nucleus interactions across GENIE’s embedded models (Geant4 Bertini Cascade and INCL++), using simulated neutrino scattering on common target nuclei (e.g., ^12C, ^40Ar, ^56Fe); empirical fitting of nucleon multiplicity distributions to Gaussian and exponential functions; development of reweighting tools to quantify and correct for model spread.

Result: Nucleon multiplicities from mesonless hadron interactions follow simple parameterizable forms—Gaussian for low-energy transfers and exponential decay for higher multiplicities—across target nuclei; significant model-dependent variations exist in both nucleon counts and visible energy deposition; reweighting tools are provided to adjust simulation outputs across models.

Conclusion: Hadron scattering model differences constitute a major source of systematic uncertainty in neutrino oscillation and cross-section measurements; the derived parameterizations and reweighting framework enable improved quantification and mitigation of these uncertainties in current and future neutrino experiments.

Abstract: In neutrino scattering experiments, the emitted final state particles are essential for identifying interaction channels and reconstructing neutrino energies. During intranuclear propagation, neutrino-induced hadrons can interact with surrounding nucleons, altering both the number of final state particles and detectable energy depositions. As a result, neutrino experiments rely on hadron-nucleus scattering models to simulate these nuclear effects. This paper explores nucleon production predicted by hadron scattering models for common hadrons in neutrino experiments, specifically nucleons and pions. We investigate these models with simulations from the \textsc{GENIE} neutrino event generator, which includes implementations of \textsc{Geant4} Bertini Cascade and \textsc{INCL++}. For popular target nuclei, we find that the number of emitted nucleons for mesonless hadron interactions can be parameterized by simple Gaussian and exponential decay functions. We present these relationships and provide reweighting tools for addressing the hadron scattering model spread in both visible energy and final state nucleon multiplicities.

</details>


### [116] [Mixture Density Networks for Neutrino Reconstruction at Hadron Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22979)
*Seungjin Yang,Jason S. H. Lee,Junghwan Goh*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: MONSTER is a mixture density network using a Transformer-based event encoder to reconstruct neutrino momentum in semileptonic tt̄ events at hadron colliders; it outperforms νflows in accuracy (5–6% lower momentum residual percentiles, 2–9% lower RMSE) and speed (2–8.5× faster inference).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Neutrino longitudinal momentum is unobserved at hadron colliders, causing intrinsic ambiguity in momentum reconstruction—especially critical in semileptonic tt̄ events for precision physics.

Method: MONSTER employs a mixture density network that models the conditional distribution of neutrino three-momentum as a multivariate normal mixture, parameterized by a Transformer-based encoder over reconstructed event objects; it provides a closed-form, sampling-free point estimate.

Result: On the νflows benchmark, MONSTER reduces the 68th percentile of the three-momentum residual by 5% and the 95th by 6%; per-variable RMSE improves by 2–9% at comparable bias; inference is 2–8.5× faster depending on hardware and batch size.

Conclusion: Mixture density networks—exemplified by MONSTER—are a competitive, efficient, and accurate alternative to normalizing flows (e.g., νflows) for neutrino momentum reconstruction in collider environments.

Abstract: Neutrino momentum reconstruction at hadron colliders is intrinsically ambiguous because the longitudinal momentum is not directly observed. We study this problem in semileptonic $t\bar{t}$ events using \monster{} (Mixture of Neutrino Solutions with Transformer Event Representation), a mixture density network that predicts a multivariate normal mixture for the conditional distribution of neutrino momentum from reconstructed event objects. The model uses a Transformer-based event encoder and yields a sampling-free point estimate of the neutrino momentum from the closed-form density. On the public benchmark introduced with \nuflows, \monster{} reduces the 68th percentile of the three-momentum residual by 5\% and the 95th percentile by 6\% relative to the empirical-mode \nuflows{} baseline, with per-variable root-mean-square errors smaller by 2 to 9\% at comparable bias, while running 2 to 8.5 times faster at inference, depending on the device and batch size. These results indicate that mixture density networks are a competitive alternative to normalizing flows for neutrino reconstruction.

</details>


### [117] [Detailed investigation on the Geant4 simulation of alpha particles in liquid scintillator detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23793)
*Shiwen Xu,Shubing Liu,Zeyuan Yu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper validates Geant4 simulations of alpha-particle energy deposition in LAB-based liquid scintillator for calibration of large detectors, identifying delta-electron fluctuations as the dominant source of intrinsic resolution and quantifying dependencies on step size, production threshold, Birks constant, and electromagnetic physics models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Alpha particles from ^214Po, ^212Po, and ^210Po are increasingly used to calibrate large liquid scintillator detectors, but Geant4 simulations of their energy deposition require detailed validation due to uncertainties in quenching modeling and low-energy electron transport.

Method: The authors simulate alpha particles in a 4 m cube of LAB-based liquid scintillator using Geant4 11.4.2 with per-step Birks quenching; they systematically vary step size, production threshold, Birks constant (k_B), and electromagnetic physics models (Standard, Penelope, Livermore); they employ Geant4-DNA to resolve keV-scale delta-electron track structure and analyze variance decomposition of quenched response.

Result: Delta-electron-associated fluctuations dominate the simulated response: secondary-track quenched-response variance constitutes 101–112% of total variance, with negative primary–secondary covariance reducing overall variance; simulated quenching curves agree with SNO+ data (χ²/ndf ≈ 0.14–0.17) for k_B = 0.007–0.010 g cm⁻² MeV⁻¹; Geant4-DNA reveals strong electron-track curvature (tortuosity ~6 at 200 eV → ~1.6 at 10 keV); production threshold impacts L/E more strongly than EM model choice (Penelope/Livermore agree within ~3%; Standard yields higher L/E, especially at low thresholds).

Conclusion: Delta-electron fluctuations are the dominant contribution to intrinsic resolution in the tested configurations; production threshold is a more critical simulation parameter than electromagnetic model selection; validated Geant4 settings enable reliable alpha-calibration modeling for next-generation liquid scintillator experiments.

Abstract: Purpose: Alpha particles from $^{214}$Po, $^{212}$Po, and $^{210}$Po are increasingly used to calibrate large liquid scintillator detectors, but Geant4 simulations of their energy deposition require detailed validation.
  Methods: We simulate alpha particles in a 4 m cube of LAB-based liquid scintillator with Geant4 11.4.2 and apply Birks quenching per step. We study step-size, production-threshold, Birks-constant, and electromagnetic-model dependence, and use Geant4-DNA to examine keV-scale electron track structure.
  Results: Delta-electron-associated fluctuations dominate the simulated response. Across the tested explicit-delta settings, the variance of the quenched-response contribution from secondary tracks is 1.01--1.12 times the total variance; a negative primary--secondary covariance reduces the total. The simulated quenching-curve shape is compatible with SNO+ data within current uncertainties, with $χ^2/\mathrm{ndf}\simeq0.14$--$0.17$ for $k_B=0.007$--$0.010$ g cm$^{-2}$ MeV$^{-1}$. Geant4-DNA gives strong electron-track curvature, with tortuosity decreasing from about 6 at 200 eV to 1.6 at 10 keV.
  Conclusion: Delta-electron fluctuations are the dominant contribution to the intrinsic resolution in the tested configurations. The production threshold has a larger effect than the electromagnetic model: Penelope and Livermore agree within about 3% in L/E, whereas Standard gives higher L/E, especially at low thresholds.

</details>


### [118] [Jevons' Paradox and Fast Generative Simulation for HEP: Why Realistic Benchmarking is Essential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24306)
*Thorsten Buss,Henry Day-Hall,Frank Gaede,Gregor Kasieczka,Katja Krüger,Anatolii Korol,Thomas Madlener,Peter McKeown,Martina Mozzanica,Lorenzo Valente*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper discusses how fast generative models can dramatically improve calorimeter simulation efficiency in high-energy physics (HEP), but cautions against Jevons’ paradox—where increased efficiency may lead to higher overall resource use—and stresses the need for realistic metrics to assess 'true' efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The high computational and energy cost of calorimeter simulation in HEP, exacerbated by increasingly granular future detectors requiring massive simulated statistics.

Method: Critical analysis of fast generative modeling advances in simulation, with emphasis on evaluating efficiency through realistic, physics-informed metrics rather than speed alone.

Result: Recognition that while generative models offer ~100× speedup, uncritical adoption risks Jevons’ paradox—i.e., expanded simulation scope may offset energy savings—necessitating holistic efficiency evaluation.

Conclusion: True simulation efficiency requires not only algorithmic speedups but also careful consideration of usage patterns and physically meaningful performance metrics to avoid unintended resource inflation.

Abstract: Simulation is a major computational expense in HEP, and calorimeter simulation in particular drives the overall energy cost of our physics analyses. Future detectors will contain more finely grained calorimeters than ever, and their data analyses will demand unprecedented simulated statistics. Fast generative models redefine what is possible, producing simulations 100 times more efficiently. This article addresses Jevons' paradox in our field and considers the importance of realistic metrics in achieving "true" efficiency.

</details>


### [119] [The detector system of the SHiP/NA67 experiment at CERN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24378)
*Matei Climescu,on behalf of the SHiP Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: SHiP/NA67 is a CERN experiment targeting GeV-scale feebly interacting particles and all-flavour neutrino physics using the HI-ECN3 beam; its initial detector configuration—featuring high-timing, high-granularity, and high-precision subsystems—is being optimized for commissioning and first physics runs in 2032–2033.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To discover feebly interacting particles beyond the Standard Model at the GeV scale and to advance precision all-flavour neutrino physics, leveraging the high-intensity HI-ECN3 beam at CERN SPS.

Method: Design and optimization of a multi-subsystem detector including veto systems, high-timing-resolution detectors, a lightweight straw tracker, and complementary calorimeters—optimized for both long-lived particle searches and high-granularity neutrino event reconstruction.

Result: A finalized initial detector configuration tailored for commissioning and early physics runs (2032–2033), balancing sensitivity to exotic decays and neutrino interaction measurements.

Conclusion: The SHiP/NA67 experiment is on track to probe uncharted parameter space for light new physics and deliver unprecedented all-flavour neutrino cross-section data via a purpose-built, dual-purpose detector system.

Abstract: The SHiP/NA67 experiment aims to search for feebly interacting GeV-scale new particles and to perform all-flavour neutrino-physics measurements at the HI-ECN3 beam facility at the CERN SPS. The collaboration is currently optimising the experiment's initial configuration for the commissioning and first physics runs of 2032-2033. The detector subsystems comprise a few large-area instruments: for new-particle searches, highly sophisticated veto detectors, high timing-resolution detectors, a lightweight straw tracker system, and high-spatial-precision calorimeters; and, for neutrino reconstruction, small transverse-size, high-granularity calorimeters.

</details>


### [120] [mkFit for track fitting with the CMS Phase-2 detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24659)
*Leonardo Giannini,Emmanouil Vourliotis*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces the extension of the mkFit algorithm—originally designed for fast, parallel track building—to track fitting in CMS's High-Level Trigger, demonstrating promising preliminary physics and computational performance using Phase-2 simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Track fitting has become a bottleneck in CMS tracking due to the speedup achieved by mkFit in track building; further acceleration is needed for HL-LHC conditions.

Method: The authors extend the mkFit framework—already optimized for thread- and data-level parallelism—to perform track fitting within the CMS High-Level Trigger, evaluating it on realistic Phase-2 simulation data.

Result: Preliminary results show improved computational performance and retained physics performance for track fitting using mkFit in the HLT context.

Conclusion: Extending mkFit to track fitting is feasible and beneficial, offering a path toward end-to-end accelerated tracking for HL-LHC operations.

Abstract: The mkFit algorithm provides an implementation of the Kalman filter-based track reconstruction algorithm that exploits both thread- and data-level parallelism. It has been adopted by the CMS Collaboration as the primary track building algorithm during the Run 3 of the LHC for both the online and offline track reconstruction sequences. Thanks to mkFit, an average speedup of a factor 3.5 is achieved in track building while retaining or improving physics performance. As a consequence of the speedup provided by mkFit in track building, track fitting has become a comparably significant component of the overall tracking time. Given the increased demands of the High-Luminosity Large Hadron Collider, further speedup can be achieved by extending mkFit to the track fitting task. We present preliminary results for track fitting in the CMS High-Level Trigger using mkFit, covering both the physics and the computational performance, based on realistic Phase-2 simulations.

</details>


### [121] [Goodness-of-fit for multi-distribution neutrino cross-section measurements with shared events](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24796)
*Francisco Martinez Lopez,London Cooper-Troendle,Steven Gardiner,Patrick Green,Tanaz Mohayai*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Introduces range-projected χ² to correctly handle rank-deficient covariance matrices arising from event-sharing across differential cross-section measurements, ensuring physically meaningful goodness-of-fit tests with proper degrees of freedom.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard χ² tests fail when multiple differential cross-sections share events, inducing linear constraints and rank-deficient covariance matrices—leading to unphysical, inflated χ² values in global fits.

Method: Proposes the range-projected χ² statistic, which projects residuals onto the column space (range) of the covariance matrix, effectively restricting the test to the subspace of independent statistical information; identifies and separates structural (binning-geometry–driven) and kinematic (phase-space–occupancy–dependent) contributions to the nullity N_null.

Result: Yields a well-defined χ² with exactly N_bins − N_null degrees of freedom; validated analytically (toy model) and numerically (simulated neutrino–argon cross-section measurement including unfolding and multi-source systematics).

Conclusion: Range-projected χ² resolves the bias from covariance rank deficiency due to event sharing, enabling robust, interpretable goodness-of-fit assessment in modern multi-differential particle physics measurements.

Abstract: When multiple differential cross-section measurements are extracted from a common event sample, the same events contribute simultaneously to multiple distributions. This event-sharing structure imposes exact linear constraints among the bin counts, reducing the effective dimensionality of the measurement below the total number of bins. Since the covariance matrix obeys these inter-distribution constraints, it contains a rank deficiency. While limited numerical precision may inadvertently restore invertibility, the $χ^{2}$ contributions along constrained dimensions will be arbitrary, yielding unphysically inflated $χ^{2}$ values in global goodness-of-fit tests. We present the range-projected $χ^{2}$, a test statistic that restricts the goodness-of-fit test to the subspace carrying independent statistical information, yielding a $χ^{2}$ with $N_\text{bins} - N_\text{null}$ degrees of freedom, where $N_\text{null}$ is the number of independent constraints. We show that this rank deficiency is a predictable consequence of the event-sharing structure, and that $N_\text{null}$ decomposes into a structural contribution determined a priori from the binning geometry and a kinematic contribution that depends on the phase-space occupancy. The method is validated with an analytical toy model and a simulated neutrino--argon cross-section measurement including unfolding and multi-source systematic uncertainties.

</details>


### [122] [Search for a top-philic heavy resonance in association with top quarks in $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s} = $ 13 TeV and 13.6 TeV with the ATLAS detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24950)
*ATLAS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Search for a top-philic Z' boson decaying to tt̄, using same-sign or multi-lepton events in 13/13.6 TeV ATLAS data; no significant excess found; excludes Z' mass < 1150 GeV (95% CL) for ct = 1, θ = 0.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe new physics beyond the Standard Model via a top-philic Z' boson—a potential signature of composite Higgs, extra dimensions, or grand unification—specifically in production modes involving top quarks (tZ' or tt̄Z') and decay Z' → tt̄.

Method: Analyzed 140 fb⁻¹ (13 TeV) + 56 fb⁻¹ (13.6 TeV) pp collision data from ATLAS; selected events with exactly two same-sign leptons or ≥3 leptons; employed a transformer-based classifier trained as a function of m_Z′ to enhance S/B discrimination; interpreted results in a simplified model with colour-singlet Z', coupling strength c_t, and chirality angle θ.

Result: No statistically significant deviation from SM background observed; observed 95% CL lower mass limit on Z' is 1150 GeV for c_t = 1, θ = 0; expected limit is 1250 GeV.

Conclusion: Current data disfavour a light top-philic Z' in the tested simplified model; constraints tighten theoretical parameter space for top-coupled vector resonances; analysis demonstrates effective use of transformer-based inference in high-energy physics multivariate classification.

Abstract: A search for a new top-philic vector boson ($Z'$) produced in association with a top-quark or a pair of top quarks, and decaying into a top quark pair is presented. The search uses $pp$ collision data collected by the ATLAS detector at the Large Hadron Collider at centre-of-mass energies of $\sqrt{s} =$ 13 TeV and 13.6 TeV, corresponding to integrated luminosities of 140 fb$^{-1}$ and 56 fb$^{-1}$, respectively. Events containing exactly two same-sign leptons or at least three leptons (electrons or muons) are selected. A transformer-based classifier parameterised as a function of the $Z'$ mass is used to optimise the signal-to-background discrimination. The results are interpreted in a simplified model with a colour-singlet vector boson coupled to top quarks with coupling strength $c_t$ and chirality angle $θ$. No significant excess is observed above the expected Standard Model background. A $Z'$ boson with mass below 1150 GeV is excluded at 95% confidence level for a benchmark scenario with $c_t = 1$ and $θ= 0$, with an expected limit of 1250 GeV.

</details>


<div id='math-ph'></div>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [123] [Cohomology of $\mathfrak{psl}(n|n)$ in trivial and in adjoint representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22598)
*Thiago Oliveira Ferreira*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the cohomology of the Lie superalgebras 𝔭𝔰𝔩(n|n) (trivial and adjoint coefficients) and 𝔰𝔩(m|n) (trivial coefficients) using Hochschild–Serre spectral sequences, corrects a prior result, and provides low-degree explicit cohomology groups for small n; relevance to AdS/CFT via 𝔭𝔰𝔲(2,2|4) is noted.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To fully determine the cohomology of projective special linear Lie superalgebras — motivated by applications in theoretical physics (e.g., AdS/CFT correspondence via the real form 𝔭𝔰𝔲(n|n)) and to resolve an inconsistency in prior literature regarding 𝔰𝔩(m|n) cohomology.

Method: Systematic computation via Hochschild–Serre spectral sequences associated with the central extension 𝔰𝔩(n|n) ↠ 𝔭𝔰𝔩(n|n), combined with known results on 𝔰𝔩(m|n) cohomology and careful analysis of differentials and extensions.

Result: Complete description of H*(𝔭𝔰𝔩(n|n), ℂ) and H*(𝔭𝔰𝔩(n|n), 𝔭𝔰𝔩(n|n)) for char 0; explicit low-degree cohomology groups for n ≤ 4; corrected cohomology of 𝔰𝔩(m|n) in trivial coefficients, identifying a discrepancy with earlier work.

Conclusion: The cohomological structure of 𝔭𝔰𝔩(n|n) is fully resolved, enabling rigorous application in supersymmetric field theory and supergravity; the correction to 𝔰𝔩(m|n) cohomology clarifies foundational aspects of Lie superalgebra cohomology theory.

Abstract: We compute the cohomology of the projective special linear Lie superalgebra $\mathfrak{psl}(n|n)$ over a field of characteristic zero in trivial and in adjoint coefficients in full detail through its associated Hochschild--Serre spectral sequences. Applications in physics exist for its real form, the projective special unitary Lie superalgebra $\mathfrak{psu}(n|n)$ --- in particular, $\mathfrak{psu}(2,2|4)$ for AdS/CFT. We also find the cohomology of $\mathfrak{sl}(m|n)$ in trivial coefficients along the way and we point out a discrepancy with a previous statement in the literature. We present the explicit cohomology groups of low degree for $n\leqslant4$.

</details>


### [124] [Distributional Fractional Taylor Series and Interpretation of the Fractal and Number-Theoretic Explicit Formulas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22759)
*Michel L. Lapidus,Matthew Overduin*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a distributional fractal Taylor's formula and exact fractal Taylor series for generalized fractal strings, expressing residues in explicit formulas via fractional distributional derivatives of the Dirac delta applied to logarithmically modified test functions, under languidity or strong languidity assumptions on the geometric zeta function.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Lapidus and van Frankenhuijsen's fractal explicit formulas by constructing a distributional fractal Taylor expansion—linking geometry of fractal strings to their complex dimensions via distributional calculus and fractional derivatives.

Method: Uses distributional calculus: expresses residue terms as fractional (−ω)-th order distributional derivatives Y_ω of δ applied to φ(x) ln^{k−1}(x); leverages Laurent expansion coefficients of ζ_η at poles ω and Γ(ω); relies on languidity (for error term) or strong languidity (for exactness) assumptions on the geometric zeta function.

Result: Derives a distributional fractal Taylor's formula with error term and an exact version; explicitly represents each residue term using Y_ω[φ(x) ln^{k−1}(x)] weighted by Laurent coefficients and Γ(ω); establishes a rigorous connection between fractal Taylor series and complex dimensions.

Conclusion: The distributional fractal Taylor series provides a new analytic characterization of generalized fractal strings in terms of their complex dimensions, advancing the theory of fractal geometry and spectral zeta functions within distribution theory.

Abstract: Motivated by the work of M. L. Lapidus and M. van Frankenhuisjen on fractal explicit formulas for generalized fractal strings, we develop in this paper a distributional fractal Taylor's formula with error term and an exact distributional fractal Taylor series representation for generalized fractal strings. The explicit formulas that the aforementioned authors developed provide a way of expressing a generalized fractal string $η$ in terms of the underlying fractal complex dimensions, under suitable growth assumptions on its geometric zeta function, $ζ_η$, known as the languidity assumptions. This involves a sum indexed by the complex dimensions of the generalized fractal string, summing over all residues of $ζ_η$, multiplied by the Mellin transform of a tempered or Schwartz test function $φ$, denoted by $\widetildeφ$. Under languidity assumptions, there is an error term present. Under stronger assumptions, that is, in the case of strong languidity, no error term is present and the resulting fractal explicit formula is said to be exact. In this paper, we express the residue term in the fractional distributional explicit formulas as the fractional $(-ω)^{\mathrm{th}}$-ordered distributional derivative of the Dirac $δ$ distribution, denoted by $Y_ω$, applied to modified test functions of the form $φ(x) \ln^{k-1}(x)$, where $k$ runs from $1$ to the multiplicity of the pole $ω$ of $ζ_η$. The coefficients in this sum are written in terms of the coefficients in the principal part of the Laurent expansion at $ω$ of the geometric zeta function $ζ_η$, multiplied by the gamma function evaluated at $ω$. The results obtained in this paper contribute to the broader program of characterizing fractals in terms of fractal Taylor series expansions involving their underlying fractal complex dimensions.

</details>


### [125] [Holomorphic Conformal Cartan Geometry: Conformal Gravity, Twistors, and Ambitwistors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.22861)
*Chen-Hsu Chien*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper unifies 4D conformal gravity, twistor theory, and ambitwistor theory using holomorphic conformal Cartan geometry; it constructs spinor-based Cartan connections, tractor bundles, local twistors, and ambitwistor space, and shows how the quadratic curvature action yields Weyl–Bach gravity and encodes chiral integrability (ASD/SD) and null geodesic geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified geometric and gauge-theoretic framework linking four-dimensional conformal gravity, twistor theory, and ambitwistor theory via holomorphic conformal Cartan geometry.

Method: Develops holomorphic conformal Cartan geometry using spinorial representations; constructs the normal sl(4,C)-valued Cartan connection, its curvature, and associated tractor bundles; derives local twistor/dual twistor bundles as spin tractor bundles; introduces ambitwistor space via canonical pairing; evaluates on Lorentzian slice and analyzes incidence relations and integrability constraints.

Result: The quadratic curvature action reduces to Weyl–Bach conformal gravity in a Weyl gauge; parallel transport of local twistors reproduces flat-space incidence and enforces anti-self-dual α-surfaces and self-dual β-surfaces in curved spacetimes; ambitwistor space is shown to parameterize complex null geodesics and symmetrically encode both chiralities of Weyl curvature.

Conclusion: Holomorphic conformal Cartan geometry provides a coherent, gauge-theoretic foundation that intrinsically unifies conformal gravity, twistor theory, and ambitwistor theory, revealing deep geometric links among curvature, chirality, and null geodesic structure.

Abstract: Four-dimensional conformal gravity, twistor theory, and ambitwistor theory are reviewed within the unified framework of holomorphic conformal Cartan geometry. Starting from the spinorial representation of the geometry, the normal $\mathfrak{sl}(4,\mathbb{C})$-valued Cartan connection, its curvature $2$-form, and the associated tractor bundles are systematically formulated. Evaluated on the real Lorentzian slice, the quadratic curvature action is shown to reproduce four-dimensional Weyl--Bach conformal gravity upon fixing a Weyl gauge. Local twistor and dual twistor bundles are constructed as associated spin tractor bundles, illustrating how parallel transport yields the classical incidence relations in flat space and chiral integrability constraints in curved backgrounds: anti-self-duality for $α$-surfaces and self-duality for $β$-surfaces. Furthermore, by pairing local twistors and dual twistors via an invariant canonical pairing, ambitwistor space is shown to parameterize complex null geodesics and symmetrically accommodate both left- and right-handed Weyl curvatures. This framework establishes a direct gauge-theoretic bridge linking conformal Cartan geometry to twistor and ambitwistor theories.

</details>


### [126] [Higher-order and non-geometric symmetries and Noether's theorem in k-cosymplectic Hamiltonian field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23034)
*Manuel de León,Xuefeng Zhao*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces generalized infinitesimal symmetries in k-cosymplectic Hamiltonian field theory, defines two new symmetry classes—higher-order general infinitesimal Cartan symmetries and good non-geometric symmetries—and proves corresponding Noether-type theorems yielding conserved quantities; results are extended to k-symplectic manifolds and illustrated with examples.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically generalize symmetries and conservation laws beyond standard Cartan symmetries in classical field theories, particularly for higher-order and non-geometric settings within k-cosymplectic and k-symplectic frameworks.

Method: Introduces generalized infinitesimal symmetries by relaxing the standard symmetry condition; defines two novel symmetry classes; derives Noether-type theorems for exact k-cosymplectic Hamiltonian systems; extends all constructions and results analogously to k-symplectic manifolds; provides explicit geometric constructions and illustrative examples.

Result: New Noether-type theorems yielding explicit conserved quantities for both higher-order general infinitesimal Cartan symmetries and good non-geometric symmetries in exact k-cosymplectic Hamiltonian systems; parallel extension to k-symplectic manifolds; concrete examples validating existence and applicability.

Conclusion: The framework unifies and generalizes symmetry analysis and conservation law derivation in classical field theories, providing a systematic geometric approach applicable to both k-cosymplectic and k-symplectic settings.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate higher-order symmetries and non-geometric symmetries within the framework of $k$-cosymplectic Hamiltonian field theory. We introduce the concept of generalized infinitesimal symmetry, which relaxes the standard symmetry condition, and then define two new classes of symmetries: higher-order general infinitesimal Cartan symmetries and good non-geometric symmetries. For each of these symmetry classes, we establish a Noether-type theorem that provides explicit conserved quantities for exact $k$-cosymplectic Hamiltonian systems. These results extend and generalize previous work on standard Cartan symmetries in the $k$-cosymplectic setting. Furthermore, we extend all of these results in a parallel fashion to Hamiltonian systems on $k$-symplectic manifolds, demonstrating the unifying nature of our approach. Concrete examples are provided to illustrate the existence and applicability of the proposed symmetries. Our framework offers a systematic geometric treatment of generalized symmetries and their associated conservation laws in classical field theories.

</details>


### [127] [Long time stability of low--frequency packets of modes at finite total energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23119)
*Dario Bambusi,Antonio Ponno*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves energy localization in the FPUT α-model over long times by leveraging the proximity to the integrable Toda lattice, using a recombined Flaschka integral—rather than action-angle variables—to control high-frequency mode excitation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish long-time energy confinement within a low-wave-number Fourier packet in the Fermi–Pasta–Ulam–Tsingou (FPUT) α-model, addressing the lack of rigorous control over energy transfer in weakly nonlinear lattices.

Method: Constructs a modified Flaschka integral of the Toda lattice whose quadratic part serves as an adiabatic invariant for the FPUT system; higher-order terms are shown to be uniformly small; uses closeness between FPUT and Toda flows to bound energy leakage beyond wave number θ* up to times ~E^{−(1−ε/2)}.

Result: Rigorous proof that initial energy concentrated in Fourier modes with wave number ≤ θ* remains localized there up to time scales O(E^{−(1−ε/2)}), provided total energy E is small and independent of N.

Conclusion: The FPUT α-model exhibits metastable spectral localization over super-polynomially long times in the small-energy regime, explained via Toda proximity and controlled by a tailored Flaschka-based invariant—bypassing the need for explicit action-angle coordinates.

Abstract: We consider the FPUT $α$-model with $N$ particles and initial data with small total energy $E$, independent of $N$. In particular, we prove that if the energy is initially given to Fourier modes of wave number $πk/N\leq θ_*$, then it does not flow to modes of wave number larger than $θ_*$ up to long times of order $E^{-(1-ε/2)}$, $θ_*$ and $ε$ being small numbers. The proof relies on the closeness of the Toda lattice to the FPUT system. Unlike previous approaches, we do not make use of the action-angle variables of the Toda system, but we exploit the Flaschka integrals of the latter. Actually, we find a suitable recombination of the Flaschka integrals whose quadratic part is used to control the packet, the higher order part fulfilling very good estimates.

</details>


### [128] [Six-wave scattering in Hamiltonian Dysthe equations: Euler matching and integrability obstructions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23175)
*Alex J. Sutherland,Solomon C. Yim*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes six-wave interactions in Hamiltonian Dysthe equations and compares them with the deep-water Euler Hamiltonian, showing how cubic corrections affect resonance structures and scattering behavior; it identifies conditions for vanishing six-wave coefficients and links them to integrable models and constraints on inverse-scattering hierarchies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the extent to which Hamiltonian Dysthe equations capture the full six-wave interaction physics of the Euler equations for deep-water waves, and to determine whether such models can support infinite integrable hierarchies via inverse scattering.

Method: Analytic perturbation theory applied to quadratic dispersion relations near exact resonances; computation of effective six-wave interaction coefficients in spatial/temporal Dysthe frameworks; comparison with Euler's degree-six Hamiltonian coefficient via vertex contractions; classification of parameter sets yielding identically vanishing six-wave terms; application of the Zakharov–Schulman condition to constrain leading symbols.

Result: A uniform asymptotic formula for the six-wave coefficient under small cubic dispersion correction; demonstration that nonzero BD implies nontrivial scattering on open resonance sets; identification of nine-quartic-vertex origin of leading Euler six-wave term; classification of coefficient sets where W₆ vanishes identically; proof that generic C³ quadratic leading symbols are restricted to span{1,k,ω(k)}, forbidding infinite inverse-scattering hierarchies in physical Dysthe models.

Conclusion: Physical Hamiltonian Dysthe models—unlike fully integrable PDEs—cannot support infinitely many linearly independent regular quadratic conserved quantities due to structural constraints from resonance analysis and the Zakharov–Schulman condition; known integrable models (NLS, Hirota, etc.) lie precisely at the cancellation loci of the six-wave coefficient.

Abstract: We compute the effective six-wave interaction in spatial and temporal Hamiltonian Dysthe equations and compare it with the complete degree-six coefficient of the deep-water Euler Hamiltonian. On every compact subset $K$ of a generic quadratic-dispersion resonance patch, exact resonances persist under a small cubic correction to the dispersion, and \[ W_6\bigl(Φ_{λ,\varepsilon}(z)\bigr) =\varepsilon\frac{4BD}{aL(z)}+O_K(\varepsilon^2) \] uniformly in the resonance point $z$ and the deformation parameter $λ$. Here $L$ is the spectral diameter and $B,D$ are the local cubic and mean-flow coefficients. Thus $BD\ne0$ gives nonzero scattering on relatively open resonance sets. At the Craig--Guyenne--Sulem temporal coefficients, the corresponding exact Euler resonances satisfy \[ \fracμ{4}W_6^{\rm E}\bigl(1+μΨ_μ(z)\bigr) =-\frac{8}{L(z)}+O_K(μ), \] where $μ$ is the relative bandwidth. The leading term comes from nine contractions of quartic vertices; every other connected degree-six contribution is uniformly bounded. We classify the coefficient values for which the generic six-wave coefficient vanishes identically; in the temporal case, this also gives cancellation on every non-permutation four-wave resonance. Outside these sets, the Zakharov--Schulman condition restricts every regular $C^3$ quadratic leading symbol to $\operatorname{span}\{1,k,ω(k)\}$, the span of the symbols of wave action, momentum, and linear energy. Consequently the physical Dysthe models cannot support an inverse-scattering hierarchy with infinitely many linearly independent regular $C^3$ quadratic leading symbols. The cancellation sets contain known nonlinear Schrödinger, mixed Chen--Lee--Liu, Calogero--Moser derivative nonlinear Schrödinger, and Hirota representatives.

</details>


### [129] [Routing and memory in the three-dimensional mirror model on K-lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23244)
*Jian Gu,Paul Schaeffer,Qin Hao*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates delocalization in the 3D Manhattan mirror (K-lattice) model via numerical experiments, revealing statistical deficits in transmission, suppression of nearby boundary channels, inverse-square-root decay of old-memory remainder, decreasing continuation losses, and absence of robust history dependence—suggesting candidate mechanisms (e.g., reflection routing, revisit charges) for future rigorous proof, but no conclusive evidence of an infinite-volume delocalized phase.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe whether the three-dimensional Manhattan mirror model supports a delocalized phase—i.e., extended quantum states—by analyzing transmission, reflection, memory effects, and routing structure beyond standard Anderson-type localization expectations.

Method: Numerical observables analysis: (1) boundary channel separation with exact conditional comparison preserving aperture sets; (2) return-age-resolved decomposition of repeated vertex contributions; (3) annular geometry experiments to quantify continuation losses and test history dependence; (4) analysis of periodic configurations via auxiliary contact graph and cycle structure.

Result: Observed statistically significant transmission deficit relative to conditional null; suppression of adjacent transmitting boundary channels; signed old-memory remainder norm decaying as ~1/√t; decreasing continuation losses in annuli; failure to replicate initial history dependence; periodic configuration yields finite routing cycles despite connected auxiliary contact graph.

Conclusion: The results provide quantitative constraints and candidate mechanisms—reflection routing statistics and adaptively selected revisit charges—that could inform a rigorous proof of delocalization, but they do not confirm or establish an infinite-volume delocalized phase.

Abstract: We study numerical observables relevant to delocalization in the three-dimensional Manhattan mirror model, also known as the K-lattice. Our principal experiment separates the locations of transmitting boundary channels from the routing between them. We derive an exact conditional comparison that preserves all transmission and reflection aperture sets, and find a statistically resolved deficit of physical transmission relative to this comparison. Nearby transmitting channels are themselves suppressed relative to a uniform subset with the same cardinality. A second experiment resolves the contribution of repeated vertices by their return age. The squared norm of the signed old-memory remainder is consistent with an inverse-square-root decay over the measured range. Annular experiments quantify decreasing continuation losses, while an initially apparent history dependence fails independent replication. Finally, a periodic configuration has a connected auxiliary contact graph but only finite routing cycles. These results identify estimates on reflection routing and adaptively selected revisit charges that may assist a rigorous proof. They do not establish an infinite-volume delocalized phase.

</details>


### [130] [Resurgence and Complex Geodesics for the Heisenberg Heat Kernel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23608)
*Xinxing Tang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes geodesic actions and heat-kernel resurgence on isotropic Heisenberg groups, deriving exact Borel–Écalle resurgent structures, Stokes matrices, alien calculus properties, and asymptotics—including uniform large-order expansions, resonance confluence, and dimension-independent action/Stokes geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the resurgence structure of heat kernels on isotropic Heisenberg groups, particularly how complex geodesic actions govern Borel singularities, Stokes phenomena, and asymptotic exactness under Borel summation.

Method: Analytic continuation of minimizing-saddle Borel germs; construction of directional Stokes operators; explicit computation of Stokes matrices and their logarithms; analysis of Borel singular support vs. primitive alien support; uniform asymptotic analysis near singularities and resonant directions; use of Fourier contour selection to identify physical resurgence sectors.

Result: Exact characterization of finite Borel singularities as complex action differences; explicit Stokes matrix and log-Stokes matrix for nonresonant endpoints; strict containment of primitive alien support in Borel singular support; uniform two-term large-order asymptotics near vertical axis; $A_2$ confluence formulas at cubic resonances; exact positive Borel summation at horizontal endpoints; signed vertical action lattice as limiting spectrum at nonzero isotropic complex endpoints; dimension-independence of action and Stokes structures on $H^n$.

Conclusion: The resurgence of the heat kernel on isotropic Heisenberg groups is governed by a rich but rigid geometric-algebraic structure: action differences fully determine Borel singularities, Stokes data is explicitly computable and nontrivially constrained, and physical summability (e.g., exactness at horizontal endpoints) reflects deep symmetries of the underlying sub-Riemannian geometry.

Abstract: We study complex geodesic actions and heat-kernel resurgence on isotropic Heisenberg groups. For real endpoints with $r>0,y\ne0$, the nonzero finite singularities reached by analytic continuation of the minimizing-saddle Borel germ are exactly the finite complex action differences. For nonresonant endpoints, we construct row-finite directional Stokes operators and compute the positive-ray Stokes matrix and its logarithm explicitly; the primitive alien support can be strictly smaller than the full Borel singular support.
  Near the vertical axis, we obtain uniform two-term large-order asymptotics from the nearest Borel singularity. Cubic resonances admit uniform $A_2$ confluence formulas. We also identify the combination of resurgent sectors selected by the original Fourier contour. Positive Borel summation is exact at horizontal endpoints. At nonzero isotropic complex endpoints, no finite normal trajectories exist, but the signed vertical action lattice is the complete limiting spectrum. The action and Stokes structures are independent of the dimension on isotropic $H^n$.

</details>


### [131] [Local conservation laws and symmetries of three-dimensional incompressible Euler flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23772)
*Alexey Shevyakov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper classifies all smooth local conservation laws, symmetry characteristics, and adjoint symmetries for the 3D incompressible Euler equations, showing that classical and generalized momentum, energy, helicity, and angular-momentum balances—plus time-dependent momentum—exhaust conservation laws; all symmetries arise from point transformations; and adjoint symmetries correspond bijectively to conservation-law multipliers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To fully characterize the structure of conservation laws and symmetries of the 3D incompressible Euler equations—a fundamental PDE system in fluid dynamics—with no restrictive ansatz on order or dependence, thereby completing a foundational classification problem in mathematical hydrodynamics and variational theory.

Method: Systematic application of the variational complex and cohomological methods: solving determining equations for adjoint symmetries; verifying multiplier identities via divergence equivalence; establishing one-to-one correspondence between adjoint symmetries and conservation-law multipliers restricted to solutions; reducing highest-derivative terms to first order via common analysis of leading terms.

Result: Complete classification: (i) only classical (mass, momentum, angular momentum, kinetic energy, helicity) and generalized time-dependent momentum conservation laws exist modulo trivial currents; (ii) all smooth finite-order symmetry characteristics are equivalent on-shell to point transformation-induced ones; (iii) all adjoint symmetries admit multiplier representatives whose product with Euler equations is an exact divergence, and these exhaust conservation-law multipliers.

Conclusion: The 3D incompressible Euler system admits no hidden or exotic smooth local conservation laws or symmetries beyond those generated by geometric (point) symmetries and standard physical balances; its conservation law structure is rigid and fully determined by classical and time-generalized momentum, energy, and topological (helicity) invariants.

Abstract: We classify all smooth local conservation laws of three-dimensional homogeneous incompressible Euler flow in velocity and pressure, allowing explicit time and position and derivatives of arbitrary finite order. Modulo currents vanishing on solutions and currents with identically zero divergence, the classical mass, momentum, angular-momentum, kinetic-energy and helicity balances, together with generalised time-dependent momentum, exhaust the classification. We also classify all smooth finite-order local symmetry characteristics and adjoint symmetries: differential expressions satisfying the linearised Euler equations and their formal adjoint on solutions, respectively. Every symmetry characteristic is equivalent on solutions to one induced by a point transformation. Every adjoint symmetry has a multiplier representative, whose product with the Euler equations is identically a divergence. These representatives exhaust the restrictions of all local conservation-law multipliers to solutions. The conservation-law proof has three stages: classification of adjoint symmetries using their determining equations, verification of explicit multiplier identities, and the correspondence between multiplier restrictions and current classes. A common analysis of highest-derivative responses reduces both symmetry problems to order at most one.

</details>


### [132] [Sum of Three Squares and Particle in a Cubic Box - Degeneracies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23794)
*S. Pratik Khastgir*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives a closed-form, exact formula for the degeneracy of energy eigenvalues of a quantum particle in a 3D cubic infinite well — i.e., the number of representations of a positive integer as a sum of three nonzero integer squares — based on empirical pattern recognition from the first 5 million cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve the quantum mechanical degeneracy problem for a particle in a 3D cubic box, which maps directly to the classical number-theoretic question of counting representations of an integer as a sum of three natural number squares.

Method: Empirical pattern discovery: generating the degeneracy sequence for the first 5 million positive integers using Mathematica, analyzing structural regularities (e.g., dependence on prime factorization modulo 8), and inducing a closed-form formula without formal proof.

Result: A closed-form, exact expression for the degeneracy d(n), valid for all positive integers n, expressed in terms of the prime factorization of n, particularly distinguishing primes congruent to 1 or 3 mod 8 and powers of 2.

Conclusion: The degeneracy problem admits a precise, computable closed-form solution derived empirically; while lacking rigorous proof, the formula is extensively verified numerically up to n = 5×10⁶ and aligns with known number-theoretic results on r₃(n) (the number of representations as sum of three squares, allowing zeros), adjusted for natural-number (nonzero) squares.

Abstract: We address the problem of determining the degeneracy of a particular energy eigenvalue corresponding to a quantum particle trapped in a cubic box. In other words, we aim to find the number of ways a specific positive integer can be written as the sum of three natural number squares. This is a classic problem in number theory. We provide a closed-form, exact solution to the problem. We do not claim to provide proofs, as we have none; instead, we build up the formulae starting from simple cases where the answer could be deduced by studying the list of degeneracies. We present ample examples that could be verified against the degeneracy calculated numerically via the brute-force method. We arrived at the solution by meticulously studying the list of degeneracies for the first five million numbers, generated by Mathematica$^{\circledR}$.

</details>


### [133] [Quantum modularity in refined topological recursion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.23864)
*Veronica Fantini,Kento Osuga*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates Borel summability and quantum modularity of the free energy from refined topological recursion on a Weber-type refined spectral curve, confirming expected analytic properties (paired Stokes rays, identification with Barnes double gamma via Borel–Laplace summation) and discovering that Stokes constants generate Jacobi quantum modular functions—suggesting quantum modularity is a general feature of refined topological recursion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the analytic structure (Borel summability) and arithmetic/automorphic properties (quantum modularity) of free energies arising from refined topological recursion, particularly on the Weber-type refined spectral curve—a setting where both resurgent and modular behaviors are expected to intertwine.

Method: The authors apply resurgent analysis (Borel transformation, Stokes phenomenon, Laplace summation) to the asymptotic series of the free energy obtained from refined topological recursion; they compute Stokes constants explicitly and identify their generating function using properties of Jacobi forms and quantum modular forms.

Result: 1) The free energy is Borel summable, with Stokes rays appearing in symmetric pairs; 2) Its Borel–Laplace sum equals the Barnes double gamma function; 3) The generating function of Stokes constants along each Stokes ray is a Jacobi quantum modular function.

Conclusion: Quantum modularity of Stokes data is not accidental but inherent to refined topological recursion, indicating a deep connection between resurgence, integrable systems, and quantum modular forms—extending beyond the Weber curve to a broader class of refined spectral curves.

Abstract: In this paper we study the Borel summability and quantum modularity of the free energy computed by refined topological recursion on the Weber-type refined spectral curve. On the one hand, we verify expected properties, namely, Stokes rays come in a pair and the Borel-Laplace sum of the free energy coincides with the Barnes double gamma function. Somewhat unexpectedly, on the other hand, we prove that the generating function of the Stokes constants along each ray is a Jacobi quantum modular function. Our proof suggests that quantum modularity may be a general feature of refined topological recursion, applicable beyond the Weber-type refined spectral curve.

</details>


### [134] [Payoffs and perception mediate environmental feedback in an N-player trust game with Q-learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24493)
*Ruqiang Guo,Zhaoyi Hu,Fangfang Wang,Linjie Liu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how endogenous environmental feedback influences trust evolution in a hierarchical trust game with Q-learning agents, revealing that environmental dynamics—particularly near perception-tier boundaries and high-return operating points—can either promote or suppress trust depending on payoff structures and learning horizons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how endogenous environmental feedback—mediated by collective behavior—affects the development of trust through material incentives and perceived states, bridging gaps between reinforcement learning and eco-evolutionary modeling.

Method: The authors couple a fixed-role, two-population hierarchical trust game with heterogeneous tabular Q-learning agents to a centered endogenous environment; they compare fixed-payoff baselines against feedback variants initialized from identical learned states, systematically varying payoff multiplication factors and observed environmental tiers.

Result: Environmental feedback promotes trust near perception-tier boundaries but reinforces its decline at high-return operating points; payoff feedback amplifies the learning-driven trust decline there, especially under stronger drive and longer horizons; unlike fixed-payoff settings, environmental feedback embeds past collective outcomes into future payoffs and observed states.

Conclusion: Endogenous environmental feedback fundamentally reshapes trust dynamics—not merely as a passive context but as an active, memory-carrying component that interacts nonlinearly with agent learning, risk preferences, and horizon length.

Abstract: Trust develops through learning, while collective behavior can alter the environment in which later decisions are made. Reinforcement-learning models describe adaptation, and eco-evolutionary models describe behavior-environment feedback, but how an endogenous environment changes trust through material incentives and perceived states remains unclear. We couple a fixed-role, two-population hierarchical trust game populated by heterogeneous tabular $Q$-learning agents to a centered endogenous environment. For the main feedback comparisons, we initialize the fixed-payoff baseline and feedback branches from the same learned state to control for prior learning. We then compare responses when payoff parameters or the observed environmental tier vary. As the payoff multiplication factor increased, successful trust in the fixed-payoff game crossed from a low to a higher finite-horizon level, led by risk-seeking investors. Environmental feedback promoted trust near perception-tier boundaries and reinforced its decline at the high-return operating point. At the high-return operating point, payoff feedback reinforced the learning-driven decline in trust. Stronger drive and longer horizons deepened this negative effect. Compared with a fixed-payoff trust game, environmental feedback carries past collective outcomes into future payoffs and observed states.

</details>


### [135] [Proactive Incentive Regulation in Multi-Agent Systems with Environmental Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24506)
*Xinyang Cao,Shijia Hua,Linjie Liu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a multi-agent environmental feedback game to study how the timing of punitive incentives—applied in resource-abundant vs. resource-scarce states—affects cooperation dynamics; it finds that early (abundant-state) penalties induce bistability and enable full cooperation, whereas late (scarce-state) penalties trigger complex, unstable dynamics that impede cooperation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The timing of punitive incentives in environmental feedback systems is poorly understood despite its critical role in resolving social dilemmas and sustaining cooperation.

Method: A multi-agent environmental feedback game framework with coupled incentive intensities across resource states; theoretical analysis of system dynamics including stability, bifurcations (e.g., Hopf), and phase portraits under abundant vs. scarce resource conditions.

Result: Penalties in resource-abundant states induce bistability supporting full cooperation; penalties in resource-scarce states yield interior equilibria, heteroclinic cycles, and Hopf bifurcations—undermining stable cooperation.

Conclusion: Timing matters critically: proactive incentive intervention under favorable environmental conditions is more effective for sustaining cooperation than delayed enforcement after degradation.

Abstract: In environmental feedback systems, self-interested behaviors of rational agents often undermine cooperation and environmental sustainability. Although punitive incentive mechanisms are widely recognized as effective in addressing such social dilemmas, the timing of their implementation under different environmental conditions remains insufficiently understood. In this paper, we develop a multi-agent environmental feedback game framework with coupled incentive intensities across resource states. We investigate how system dynamics vary with incentive intensity under both resource-abundant and resource-scarce conditions. Theoretical analysis shows that imposing penalties in resource-abundant states can transform the system from a tragedy of the commons into a bistable regime that supports full cooperation. In contrast, imposing penalties in resource-scarce states leads to complex dynamical behaviors, including interior stability, heteroclinic cycles, and Hopf bifurcations, which hinder the emergence of stable cooperation. These results highlight the importance of timing in incentive regulation. Proactive intervention under favorable environmental conditions is more effective in sustaining cooperation than delayed enforcement after environmental degradation.

</details>


### [136] [Batalin-Vilkovisky algebras from Ševera bicomplexes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24781)
*Eugenia Boffo,Domenico Fiorenza*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces Ševera bicomplexes—bicomplexes with differentials constrained as Grothendieck differential operators—and shows that under suitable conditions, the second-page differential $d_2$ of their spectral sequence yields a Batalin–Vilkovisky (BV) operator on $E_2$, thereby upgrading $A$'s graded commutative structure to Gerstenhaber and then BV algebra structures; it interprets BV algebras via Cartan calculus and hints at derived generalizations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and generalize the emergence of Batalin–Vilkovisky algebra structures—particularly in odd symplectic geometry—from homological and operadic perspectives, using spectral sequences and differential operator constraints.

Method: Constructs and analyzes Ševera bicomplexes $(M,d_0,d_1)$ where $d_i$ are Grothendieck differential operators of order ≤ i over a graded commutative algebra $A$; imposes existence of a $d_0$-contracting homotopy $K$ of matching order; computes $d_2$ in the associated spectral sequence and verifies its BV properties using Cartan calculus formalism.

Result: Under suitable assumptions, $d_2$ is a BV operator on $E_2$; if $E_2 \cong A$ as free rank-1 $A$-module, the $A$-algebra structure lifts to Gerstenhaber, and choosing a $d_2$-closed generator yields a BV algebra; recovers Ševera’s BV structure on functions of odd symplectic manifolds.

Conclusion: Ševera bicomplexes provide a natural homological framework for BV algebras grounded in differential operator constraints and spectral sequences; Cartan calculus offers a coherent interpretation, with potential extension to derived settings.

Abstract: A Ševera bicomplex is a bicomplex $(M,d_{0},d_{1})$ whose differentials $d_{i}$ are Grothendieck differential operators of order $\leq i$ with respect to a given $A$-module structure on $M$, where $A$ is a graded commutative algebra. One also requires that $(M,d_{0})$ admits a contracting homotopy $K$ that is a differential operator of the same order as $d_{0}$. Under suitable assumptions, the differential $d_{2}$ of the second page of the spectral sequence associated with $(M,d_{0},d_{1})$ is a Batalin-Vilkovisky operator on $E_{2}$, so that when $E_{2}$ is a free rank 1 $A$-module, the graded commutative algebra structure of $A$ is enhanced to a Gerstenhaber algebra structure, and the choice of a $d_{2}$-closed basis element for $E_{2}$ further enhances this to a Batalin-Vilkovisky algebra structure. The prototypical example of this construction is Ševera's description of the Batalin--Vilkovisky algebra structure on the algebra of smooth functions of an odd symplectic manifold. Throughout the whole article we look at Batalin-Vilkovisky algebras through the lenses of Cartan calculus. Indications of a generalization to derived Cartan calculus are briefly discussed in the concluding section.

</details>


### [137] [Constraints for Physical Gauge Groups Coming from the Causal Action Principle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24888)
*Felix Finster,Niels Gresnigt*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores constraints on local gauge groups derived from the causal action principle in causal fermion systems, revealing a systematic link between Lie and Clifford algebras via quadratic (bilinear logarithmic) terms in gauge potentials, starting from chiral U(N)×U(N) structures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how fundamental physical constraints—specifically those arising from the causal action principle in causal fermion systems—restrict possible local gauge groups and reveal deeper algebraic structures linking Lie and Clifford algebras.

Method: Analytical derivation and systematic algebraic analysis of quadratic constraints (bilinear logarithmic terms) in the causal action functional; starts from general chiral gauge potentials for U(N)×U(N), then derives structural connections between Lie algebras and Clifford algebras.

Result: A rigorous algebraic correspondence is established: the quadratic gauge constraints induce commutation relations and representation structures that unify aspects of Lie algebras (governing gauge symmetries) and Clifford algebras (encoding spacetime and spinor geometry). Examples confirm consistency across varying N.

Conclusion: The causal action principle inherently encodes a deep compatibility between gauge symmetry (Lie algebraic structure) and quantum spacetime geometry (Clifford algebraic structure), suggesting that realistic gauge groups must satisfy algebraic constraints beyond standard model assumptions.

Abstract: The constraints for the effective local gauge groups stemming from the causal action principle for causal fermion systems are reviewed. The constraint which are quadratic in the gauge potentials (coming from the so-called bilinear logarithmic terms) are shown to make a connection between the structures of Lie algebras and Clifford algebras. This connection is worked out systematically starting from general chiral potentials corresponding to the gauge group $\mathrm{U}(N) \times \mathrm{U}(N)$. The general results are illustrated in various examples.

</details>


### [138] [Don't mind the gap: Absolutely Continuous Edge Spectrum in a Mobility Gap](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25000)
*Simon Becker,Mengxuan Yang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a rigorous link between bulk topological invariants and the absolutely continuous edge spectrum for 2D lattice and magnetic Schrödinger Hamiltonians in a mobility gap: a nonzero bulk Chern (or related) index forces the entire mobility gap to lie in the absolutely continuous spectrum of the half-space operator, with spectral multiplicity bounded below by the index magnitude.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously connect bulk topological invariants (e.g., Chern number) to the nature of the edge spectrum—specifically, to prove that a nonzero index guarantees absolutely continuous (AC) edge states across an entire mobility gap, not just pointwise existence or singular continuity.

Method: Functional analytic spectral theory; localization assumptions (e.g., exponential decay of fractional moments or dynamical localization in the bulk); boundary conditions enabling half-space restriction; index theory (e.g., Fredholm or noncommutative winding); auxiliary-space cut construction (a technical tool to relate bulk and edge operators).

Result: If the bulk system exhibits localization in an energy interval (mobility gap) and has a constant nonzero topological index (e.g., Chern number), then that entire interval lies in the absolutely continuous spectrum of the half-space (edge) Hamiltonian; moreover, the AC spectral multiplicity is ≥ |index| almost everywhere in the interval.

Conclusion: The bulk topological index fully controls the spectral type and minimal multiplicity of the edge spectrum within a mobility gap—establishing a strong, quantitative bulk–edge correspondence beyond mere existence of edge modes.

Abstract: We study the edge spectrum of two-dimensional lattice Hamiltonians and magnetic Schrödinger operators in a mobility gap, an energy interval in which the bulk spectrum is potentially localized rather than necessarily absent. Under suitable localization and boundary assumptions, we prove that a constant nonzero bulk topological index implies that the entire interval belongs to the absolutely continuous spectrum of the corresponding half-space operator. The absolutely continuous spectral multiplicity is bounded below by the absolute value of the index at almost every energy in the interval. The first version of the auxiliary-space cut construction in Section 2.3 was generated using ChatGPT 6.

</details>


### [139] [The Hadamard parametrix on globally hyperbolic spacetimes with Robin boundary conditions: Fundamental solutions and Hadamard states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24465)
*Beatrice Costeri,Claudio Dappiaggi,Benito Alberto Juárez-Aubry*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the Klein-Gordon equation with Robin boundary conditions on globally hyperbolic spacetimes with timelike boundaries, establishing fundamental solutions, characterizing their wavefront sets via propagation of singularities, and introducing and constructing Robin-Hadamard two-point correlation functions—including reflected contributions—proving equivalence between local Hadamard form and global wavefront set condition, and existence on static and deformed backgrounds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend quantum field theory in curved spacetime to manifolds with timelike boundaries, where standard Hadamard condition fails due to boundary reflections; specifically, to rigorously define physically admissible two-point functions (Robin-Hadamard states) respecting Robin boundary conditions and causal structure.

Method: Microlocal analysis: construction of advanced/retarded fundamental solutions; application of Melrose–Sjöstrand propagation of singularities theorem under infinitesimal convexity and finite boundary reflections; definition and local construction of Robin-Hadamard two-point functions via directed Hadamard parametrix augmented by reflection terms governed by transport equations with Robin matching conditions; deformation argument from static ground states.

Result: Existence/uniqueness of fundamental solutions; full characterization of their wavefront sets in the interior; definition and explicit local construction of Robin-Hadamard two-point functions with reflected contributions; proof of equivalence between the local Hadamard form and the global wavefront set condition (Radzikowski-type theorem for timelike boundaries); existence of such states on static and adiabatically deformed spacetimes.

Conclusion: The Robin-Hadamard condition provides a consistent, microlocally characterized framework for physically meaningful quantum states of a scalar field on globally hyperbolic spacetimes with timelike boundaries, generalizing the standard Hadamard condition and enabling rigorous algebraic QFT in bounded regions of spacetime.

Abstract: On a $d$-dimensional, $d\geq 2$, globally hyperbolic spacetime with a timelike boundary $(\mathcal{M},g)$, we investigate the Klein-Gordon equation with Robin boundary conditions. First of all, we prove existence and uniqueness of the advanced and retarded fundamental solutions, discussing in addition their structural properties. Secondly, under the hypothesis of infinitesimal convexity of $\partial\mathcal{M}$ and of local finiteness of boundary reflections for broken null bicharacteristics, we characterize their wavefront set in the interior $\mathring{\mathcal{M}}$, using the propagation of singularities theorem of Melrose and Sjöstrand.
  In the second part of the paper, we introduce a notion of Robin-Hadamard two-point correlation function as a positive distribution on $\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}$ with prescribed antisymmetric part and singular structure, the latter being characterized in terms of its wavefront set. We complement this definition with a local Hadamard form. Up to a smooth remainder, this consists of the standard directed Hadamard parametrix together with additional contributions associated with the reflected null rays. Their coefficients are determined by transport equations supplemented by Robin matching conditions at the boundary. The construction applies to an arbitrary, but finite number of reflections. We prove that this local formulation is equivalent to the wavefront set condition, thereby establishing a counterpart of Radzikowski's theorem in the presence of a timelike boundary. Finally, starting from the ground state on suitable static backgrounds and using a deformation argument, we prove existence of Robin-Hadamard two-point correlation functions on the class of spacetimes considered.

</details>
